# Disease-side decisions and assumptions (running list)

What we decided, why, and what we are assuming. One line per item, in plain language. Add new items at the end of each
section; never delete. If a decision is replaced, strike it through (`~~...~~`) and add the new one with a pointer.
Details and numbers: [`PROGRESS.md`](PROGRESS.md). Drug-side decisions are kept in the `celligner` worktree.

"PI" = decided by the PI / user. "Claude" = chosen by Claude after the user delegated the choice.

## 1. Decisions

### Scope and disease groups (Step 1)
| # | Date | Decision | Why | By |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | 10-01 | Use the Toil TCGA + TARGET + GTEx data (all on GENCODE v23) as the main dataset | Tumors and healthy tissue processed by one pipeline | PI |
| D2 | 10-01 | Split mixed cohorts into subtypes (BRCA by PAM50, LGG by IDH/codel, ESCA, CESC, UCEC, PCPG, TGCT, SARC; ALL by lineage) | Different subtypes are different diseases with different drugs | PI |
| D3 | 10-01 | A group needs >= 30 tumor patients; 49 groups included | Too few patients give unstable signatures | PI |
| D4 | 10-01 | GTEx tissue choices reviewed per cancer (e.g. HNSC -> esophagus mucosa; SARC -> muscle + fat) | Best available healthy match for each cancer | PI |
| D5 | 10-01 | Steps 5-7 for every group with a signature; Steps 8-10 only for breast and colorectal | Focus deep work where data are richest | PI |
| D6 | 10-01 | Blood cancers (AML, ALL, DLBC) de-prioritized; their normal-reference search parked | Data more limited and variable; no good healthy reference | PI |

### Covariates and signatures (Steps 3-4)
| # | Date | Decision | Why | By |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D7 | 10-01 | One sample per patient (diagnosis sample first) and one per GTEx donor | Repeat samples would over-count patients | PI |
| D8 | 10-01 | Tumor vs healthy signature with limma-voom: ~ tumor/normal + age + sex + ESTIMATE purity + sample-type indicators; no plate term | Standard method; plate cannot be separated from tumor vs GTEx | PI |
| D9 | 10-01 | Protein-coding genes for drug matching; extended gene set only for mechanism work | Drug signatures measure protein-coding genes | PI |
| D10 | 10-01 | Keep every gene's score (signed z); no significance cut-off | Drug matching uses the whole ranked list | PI |
| D11 | 10-01 | CESC-SCC normals = cervix + vagina; CESC-AD = cervix only (minimum lowered to 5 normals) | Same tissue type; too few cervix donors otherwise | PI |
| D12 | 10-01 | Breast is female-only throughout | Male breast cancer is biologically distinct | PI |
| D13 | 10-01 | BLCA compared with adjacent normal instead of GTEx | GTEx has only 9 bladder samples | PI |

### Regulator networks and activity (Steps 5-6)
| # | Date | Decision | Why | By |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D14 | 10-02 | Build our own ARACNe networks per cancer, with the full regulator set (TFs, kinases, signaling; ~6,200), no target cap | Drivers may be kinases or signaling proteins; published networks failed our checks | PI |
| D15 | 10-02 | Significance from a "normal vs normal" null (1,000 random splits of the healthy samples); the faster analytic null is never reported | The analytic null assumes genes are independent and overstates significance | PI |
| D16 | 10-02 | Regulator kept only if expressed in the tumors (median logCPM >= 1) and >= 10 targets / >= 50% of targets measured | Avoid scoring regulators that are absent or barely measured | PI |
| D17 | 10-08 | ARACNe is the primary network; CollecTRI and DoRothEA are supporting evidence | ARACNe passes known-biology checks; curated sets do not fit tissue context | PI |
| D18 | 10-08 | Confirmed regulator = ARACNe FDR < 0.05, or FDR < 0.10 with a same-direction FDR < 0.10 call in CollecTRI or DoRothEA; FDR < 0.10 for exploratory selection | Balance discovery and confidence | PI |
| D19 | 10-08 | Failing known-biology checks are reported as limitations; no tuning to make them pass | Avoid fitting the method to expectations | PI |
| D20 | 10-08 | Small/weak groups (CESC-AD, ESCA-SCC, UCS) kept for drug matching | See what they match; flagged | PI |
| D21 | 10-08 | ARACNe consolidation threshold kept as built | Networks pass confounding checks | PI |

### Patient-level corrections (Step 6, input to clustering)
| # | Date | Decision | Why | By |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D22 | 10-07 | Remove tumor purity from patient scores (ESTIMATE; ABSOLUTE for LGG from 10-08) | Purity was the main pattern between patients, not mechanism | PI |
| D23 | 10-08 | Gene-level correction for purity + RNA quality (long-minus-short gene index) before patient VIPER | Plate pattern traced to RNA quality differences between batches | PI |
| D24 | 10-09 | CQN normalization tested and not adopted | Did not fix the recurring module; mixed side effects | PI |
| D25 | 10-09 | Mappability filter adopted as standard (Saha & Battle 2018): genes with mappability < 0.8 and cross-mappable edges removed | Removes multi-mapping artifacts at no cost to biology | PI |
| D26 | 10-09 | Recurring PILRB/CCNL2 module kept in clustering | PI reads it as growth / mitochondrial / immune-rescue biology (our data show no proliferation or immune link and a negative OXPHOS link) | PI |
| D27 | 10-08 | TGCT-SEM immune question tabled | Seminomas are lymphocyte-rich by definition | PI |

### Clustering and replication (Steps 8-9)
| # | Date | Decision | Why | By |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D28 | 10-09 | Cluster each of BRCA-Basal/Her2/LumA/LumB, COAD, READ separately | Subtypes and colon/rectum differ too much to pool | PI |
| D29 | 10-09 | Features = one score per regulator module (>= 10 co-varying regulators); all modules as a sensitivity check | Thousands of regulators collapse into a few hundred groups | PI |
| D30 | 10-09 | M3C consensus clustering; rule fixed before running (best K significant on both p-values); "no clusters" is an accepted outcome | Avoid finding clusters in continuous variation | PI |
| D31 | 10-08 | Two replication cohorts per cancer (breast: METABRIC + CPTAC-BRCA; colorectal: GSE39582 + CPTAC-COAD) | Independent platforms and populations | PI |
| D32 | 10-09 | In replication cohorts each patient is compared with the cohort's average tumor | No healthy samples in these cohorts; clustering compares patients with each other | PI |
| D33 | 10-12 | Colorectal replication by three methods (transfer TCGA clusters, cluster independently, compare module differences) + CMS comparison | Each method answers a different question | PI |

### Strata and Step 10 signatures
| # | Date | Decision | Why | By |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D34 | 10-12 | Colon split named COAD-CMS1/3-like (MSI-like, 92) and COAD-CMS2/4-like (195); used as the colon strata | Replicated in both cohorts; matches CMS 1+3 vs 2+4 | PI |
| D35 | 10-12 | The three READ clusters kept as separate subgroups, each matched to drugs; flagged tentative | No rectal cohort to replicate; plate-associated | PI |
| D36 | 10-12 | Breast: no clusters, so continuous axes = principal components of the module scores within each subtype | Few independent axes, no hand-picking, transferable to other cohorts | Claude (delegated) |
| D37 | 10-12 | Axis kept if above a shuffled-data null, stable under resampling, and not technical (plate above chance < 0.2; RNA quality and purity rho < 0.5); 13 axes kept | Rule fixed before use; plate measured above chance because small groups have high chance overlap | Claude (delegated) |
| D38 | 10-12 | Every stratum signature = stratum tumors vs healthy GTEx tissue, same model as Step 4 | All signatures mean the same thing ("disease state to reverse") | PI |
| D39 | 10-12 | Breast axis signatures: top third ("high") and bottom third ("low") each vs GTEx (26 signatures), plus a companion list of genes that follow each axis (explanation only) | Consistent with D38; companion shows what drives the axis | PI |
| D40 | 10-12 | Breast axes checked in METABRIC and CPTAC-BRCA by projecting TCGA axis weights (rule fixed before running) | Axes should be used as fixed TCGA directions | PI / Claude |
| D41 | 10-12 | Her2 PC4 kept, flagged weak | Did not replicate in METABRIC | PI |
| D42 | 10-12 | Stratum master regulators built exactly as Step 6, with the regulator-expressed filter judged on the stratum's own tumors | Drug side scores reversal of master regulators | PI |
| D43 | 10-12 | Drugs for strata scored on reversal + specificity (specificity vs sibling strata proposed) | Stratum signatures are 0.97-0.99 similar to the parent; specificity is what separates strata | PI |
| D44 | 10-12 | For strata, cell-line matching uses the average of the stratum's own patients; target gate judged on the stratum's own patients | Strata may match different lines and express different targets | PI (drug side implements) |

### Step 4 rebuild (corrections carried to signatures and networks)
| # | Date | Decision | Why | By |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D45 | 10-18 | ESTIMATE purity wherever possible, including LGG patient-level correction (replaces the LGG ABSOLUTE part of D22) | ESTIMATE exists for tumors and normals alike, so it is the only purity measure usable in tumor-vs-normal models; one measure everywhere. Note: ESTIMATE agrees poorly with ABSOLUTE in LGG (rho -0.28 to -0.33) | PI |
| D46 | 10-18 | Genes with mappability < 0.8 (Saha & Battle 2018) and readthrough loci removed before Step 4 and before rebuilding the ARACNe networks (1,824 of 19,168 Step 4 genes); cross-mappable edges removed | Until now these filters reached only patient-level scores, not the signatures or networks the drug side uses | PI |
| D47 | 10-18 | Before the rebuild, measure the TCGA-vs-GTEx lab difference (adjacent vs GTEx normals), GTEx post-mortem effects (ischemic time, ventilator death) and the RNA-quality index across tumors and normals together (`scripts/31_reference_diagnostics.R`) | Decide the corrections from measurements, not by default | PI |
| D48 | 10-18 | ~~Remove the shared TCGA-vs-GTEx lab difference from every GTEx-referenced signature: per gene, median adjacent-vs-GTEx logFC over 10 organ units, leave-one-unit-out, added to the GTEx normals' log-expression (gene corrected only if estimated in >= 6 units)~~ Replaced by D57 (user 10-18: the gap is not lab alone). | Healthy-vs-healthy difference is as large as a tumor signature (median 3,844 genes), shared across organs (rho 0.61) and overlaps the tumor signatures (median rho 0.62); GC/length explain only ~10% so CQN alone is not enough | PI |
| D49 | 10-18 | ~~GTEx-only covariates in Step 4 (and the lab-unit models): ischemic time (standardized within GTEx normals) and ventilator death (Hardy 0, centered within GTEx normals; only if >= 5 donors in each class); 0 for tumors~~ Replaced by D57 (ischemic time + death circumstance, corrected toward best-preserved donors). | Post-mortem effects are large in lung, esophagus/HNSC, OV and align with the tumor signature there | PI |
| D50 | 10-18 | RNA-quality index NOT added to Step 4; kept only for within-tumor / within-normal patient-level correction | Computed jointly, TCGA adjacent normals score higher than GTEx (d +0.22) while tumors score lower (d -0.70), and the index barely tracks GTEx RIN (rho 0.13): the tumor-vs-GTEx gap is not RNA integrity, so adjusting would remove tumor biology | PI |
| D51 | 10-18 | Rebuild all 28 ARACNe networks on the v2 gene set (input otherwise as v1, no covariate regression), remove cross-mappable edges after the build | Networks and signatures must use the same genes; v1 edge checks showed covariates barely affect edges (D21) | PI |
| D52 | 10-18 | ~~Patient-level scores (Step 6b) use the same lab shift on the normals, ESTIMATE purity in every group, and ischemic time + ventilator in the normals' correction~~ Lab-shift part replaced by D57; ESTIMATE everywhere stays (D45). | Patient scores are also tumor vs GTEx normals | PI |
| D53 | 10-18 | All v2 outputs in `_v2` paths; v1 kept for comparison; acceptance checks C1-C10 fixed before running and reported whatever they show | Comparable before/after; no tuning (D19) | PI |
| D54 | 10-18 | Genes without a mappability value (71) are kept and flagged | No evidence of a mapping problem | Claude (delegated) |
| D55 | 10-18 | VIPER normal-split null kept exactly as v1 (unadjusted Welch t on normals' logCPM, v2 genes) | Change one thing at a time; the lab shift cancels in the null | Claude (delegated) |
| D56 | 10-18 | Steps 8-10 and the drug side are rerun on v2 only after the checks are reviewed; the recurring-module decision (D26) is revisited first | The recurring module is now linked to the lab axis | PI |
| D57 | 10-18 | GTEx stays the healthy reference. GTEx normals are corrected from within GTEx only: per tissue, each gene modelled on log ischemic time + death circumstance (ventilator / fast / slow; Hardy 0 / 1-2 / 3-4) with age, sex, ESTIMATE; all donors kept and shifted so their average equals the best-preserved donors (ventilator, ischemic time in the tissue's shortest third, autolysis <= 1): the observed best-preserved average where the tissue has >= 45 such donors, the model's prediction where fewer; tissues with < 10 such donors (brain cortex 0, kidney 7) corrected to the average donor only, flagged. No subtraction of the TCGA-adjacent vs GTEx gap | Ischemic time + death circumstance lower held-out donor variance in about half the tissues (to ~6%); autolysis / fixative / RIN / batch add nothing. The shifted reference is closer to hidden best-preserved donors than the average-donor reference in 20 of 20 tissues (gap closed median 0.13); the plain best-preserved average is closer still where enough donors exist (median 0.18). The adjacent-vs-GTEx gap mixes lab, surgery vs death, donor population and tumor-adjacent biology, and GTEx-internal collection variation explains only 0-10% of it (scripts 33, 34) | PI |
| D58 | 10-18 | TCGA adjacent normals: check only for the disease signature (never subtracted); candidate secondary use in drug ranking under discussion | GTEx gives equally or more reproducible signatures across disjoint patient halves in 8 of 11 organs; adjacent tissue is not healthy (field effect, tumor cells; Aran 2017) | PI |
| D59 | 10-18 | GTEx colon (Colon - Transverse) and kidney (Kidney - Cortex): drop samples from a different part of the organ before any use. Colon: keep samples with mean log-CPM of 8 epithelial markers (CDX1, CDX2, VIL1, KRT20, CDH17, LGALS4, CEACAM5, EPCAM) >= 7, the valley between a mucosa mode and a muscle-wall mode and the lowest value seen in TCGA adjacent colon (165 -> 109 samples). Kidney: keep samples with mean log-CPM of 8 proximal-tubule markers (SLC34A1, LRP2, CUBN, SLC22A6, SLC22A8, SLC5A12, ALDOB, GATM) >= 5 (28 -> 21; removed: 2 near-empty samples, 5 medulla-dominant) | Colon GTEx contains muscle-wall-only samples; kidney GTEx contains medulla-dominant and failed samples. After removal GTEx variability matches TCGA adjacent: colon BCV 0.67 -> 0.41 (adjacent 0.44), PC spread 92 -> 54 (56); kidney BCV 0.78 -> 0.47 (0.50), spread 87 -> 71 (70) | PI (rule: Claude) |
| D60 | 10-18 | Kidney flagged for the PI: after D59 only 21 GTEx donors (7 best-preserved, so no best-preserved shift); KICH arises from collecting-duct cells, which the proximal-tubule rule de-emphasizes | TCGA adjacent kidney gave more reproducible signatures than GTEx (0.92 vs 0.87) before D59 | Open (PI) |
| D61 | 10-18 | Measure tissue composition per cohort (tumors vs v2 GTEx reference vs TCGA adjacent): MCP-counter immune / stromal scores + Human Protein Atlas single-cell marker panels; share of each signature explained by cell-type membership. Spec: [`SPEC_tissue_composition.md`](SPEC_tissue_composition.md); not executed | Signature genes that only reflect a different cell mix in normal tissue (fat in breast, muscle in uterus) cannot be reversed by a drug acting on tumor cells | PI |
| D62 | 10-18 | Project all v2 GTEx reference donors (and TCGA adjacent normals) through Celligner per cohort: line weights, lineage composition, shared weight with the tumors, organ-driven vs cancer-specific lines. Spec on the `celligner` branch: `docs/notes/SPEC_celligner_normals.md`; not executed | Shows how much of each disease's cell-line match reflects the organ rather than the cancer | PI |
| D63 | 10-18 | Network build questions settled before the v2 build, prompted by drug-side findings (proliferation carries most known-drug signal, C15; ESR1 regulon = luminal identity, C3/C14) | A regulator list or sample pooling cannot be changed after the build without rebuilding | PI |
| D64 | 10-18 | Build two v2 network sets: the full regulator set as D14 (5,789 candidates) and a TF-only set (Lambert TFs, ~1,150-1,360 per cohort), same inputs and settings | Restricting a full build to TFs afterwards is not the same as a TF-only build (DPI makes regulators compete for targets); TF-only is comparable with CollecTRI and avoids non-TF proliferation 'regulators' (MKI67, TOP2A, CKS1B); costs ~0.7 extra days | PI |
| D65 | 10-18 | Add a luminal-only breast network (BRCA_LUM: LumA + LumB, 769 tumors) next to the pooled BRCA network | In the pooled network the ESR1 regulon reflects luminal-vs-basal identity, so anti-estrogens read wrongly on the drug side; within luminal tumors ESR1 targets should reflect ER signaling. Her2 (81) and Basal (190) stay pooled (too small alone) | PI |
| D66 | 10-18 | No proliferation regression of the network input; keep the v2 gene filter (D46) although it removes 28 of 978 L1000 landmark genes (e.g. FOXO3, TFDP1, CHEK2, HSPA1A) and 412 candidate regulators (100 TFs; none of the known-biology controls) | E2F / FOXM1 regulons are real biology and pass controls; proliferation is handled at drug scoring (79i). The lost regulators are mostly artefacts (AGAP4/5/6, readthrough) or proliferation genes (CKS1B, CKS2, UHRF1) | PI |
| D67 | 10-18 | Composition marker panels from cell-type families: 15 fixed families of closely related HPA types (e.g. enterocytes + Paneth, keratinocytes + squamous epithelium, alveolar type 1 + 2, blood + lymphatic endothelium, fibroblasts + endometrial / ovarian stroma) scored as one unit, markers >= 4x every type outside the family; positive controls renamed to the families | Under the single-type rule 9 of 18 controls had no panel; with families 51 of 56 units get a panel. Smooth muscle still has none (its genes are shared with HPA Sertoli, skeletal muscle and myoepithelial cells) | User |
| D68 | 10-20 | Subtype networks: only the breast luminal test (BRCA_LUM vs pooled BRCA master regulators, cutoff for a material change fixed before the comparison); other subtypes stay on the shared cohort network. If luminal regulators change materially, build networks for the other subtypes with >= 100 tumors (BRCA LumA / LumB / Basal, CESC-SCC, LGG IDHmut-codel / -noncodel, PCPG-PHEO, UCEC-EEC, SARC-LMS). Strata found with the networks (COAD / READ) tested only after v2 Steps 8-10, against size- and split-matched control networks. No synthetic patients to reach the ARACNe sample size | Subtypes are assumed to share wiring and differ in regulator use, which VIPER measures on a shared network (the colon strata were found from activity differences on the shared network). A network built on part of a cohort differs even with identical wiring (less variation, fewer patients), so a difference alone is not rewiring. Resampled or generated patients repeat the real ones and inflate mutual information | User |
| D69 | 10-20 | Step 4 v2 uses voomByGroup (separate mean-variance trends for tumors and normals) instead of voom; the voom version is kept as check C12 and the 13-group test results are kept (`results/step4/voom_group_variance/`) | Tumors are 1.2-1.8x more variable than normals (A2 wrong in 12 of 13 groups); split-half reproducibility of z and NES slightly higher (median +0.001), controls unchanged. Whole rankings barely move (rho >= 0.996, below the pre-set threshold for a practical effect), but the top-500 genes and the FDR-cut regulator lists that the drug side uses shift 10-30% (READ 157 -> 230, THCA 1 -> 55), which the user judged significant | User |
| D70 | 10-20 | Step 4 v2 signatures accepted as built. The larger GC tilt (C1 median -0.24 -> -0.30) needs no correction; C2 still reports whether it reaches the regulators. Pediatric groups (TARGET-WT, NBL) are recorded as more variable (A36) and left as they are | A GC difference between healthy donors and tumors may be partly real and is not by itself a problem. Corrections are made only when they apply to all cohorts: the goal is an accurate, reproducible workflow, not fixes for single edge cases (WT genes changed 2,801 -> 6,916 when kidney normals went 28 -> 21) | User |
| D71 | 10-28 | Step 8 subgroups kept only if all hold (TF arm, modules10 at best K; `84_subgroup_consistency.R`): M3C significant; split-half prediction strength >= 0.75; smallest cluster >= 30 patients; modules10 vs all ARI >= 0.3; plate Cramer's V excess over permutation < 0.1; purity and RNA-quality R^2 < 0.1. Pass: TCGA-BLCA, LGG-IDHmut-noncodel, TCGA-HNSC, TCGA-ACC (correction 10-28: TCGA-COAD was passed by rounding its prediction strength 0.748 to 0.75; strictly it fails); fail: STAD (plate 0.14), LUSC (ARI 0.19), KICH (min 21, ARI 0.19); 33 groups no stable subgroups. READ v1 strata and BRCA-LumA clusters not reproduced. BLCA / HNSC re-clustered without the recurring module (80 EXCLUDE_MODULE=recurring): same splits (ARI 0.87 / 0.91 with the original), prediction strength 0.90 / 0.81; BLCA = basal-squamous (mRNA cluster 3) vs luminal, HNSC = basal + mesenchymal vs atypical + classical. Which of the five to keep: user, pending | Prediction strength of any 2-way split of unstructured groups here is ~0.5-0.65, so 0.75 separates real structure; subgroups get their own signatures and drug lists, so a technical or unstable split would propagate | User |
| D72 | 10-28 | Step 10 v2 subgroups: keep all five groups that pass D71, K = 2 each (`85_step10_strata_v2.R` -> `results/step10_v2/`): BLCA basal-squamous-like / luminal-like and HNSC basal-mesenchymal-like / atypical-classical-like (clusters from the run without the recurring module, both feature sets significant), COAD CIN-like / MSI-like, ACC proliferative / low-proliferation, LGG-IDHmut-noncodel LGr3 / LGr2. Each subgroup: Step 4 v2 signature vs the parent's normals (`30_signatures.R` STRATA=), group VIPER with the corrected null and patient VIPER (`50_viper.R` STRATA=), drug matching. v1 strata (READ C1-C3, breast axes) not carried over | BLCA and HNSC splits do not depend on the C8 module (ARI 0.87 / 0.91 without it) and match known TCGA subtypes; group and patient level both, to measure variability within each subgroup | User |
| D73 | 10-28 | v2 group null = Step 4a reference normals (fix B; `50_viper.R`). Covariate-adjusted null (C) not adopted: pre-set rule failed on one control (BRCA-LumA MYBL2 lost a pass; C raised the median FDR < 0.05 count 19 -> 74, 0 calibration false positives, +4 control passes). `64_null_adjust_test.R`, 11 of 12 groups (CESC-AD: calibration on 7 normals gives a 2-vs-1 split; msviper hangs on the all-NA signature) | B is the spec (null from the normals the signature used); the rule was fixed before running. FDR-cut MR counts jumped between nulls (COAD 425 / 0 / 1, STAD FDR < 0.10 210 / 0 / 220) while NES rankings agreed (rho >= 0.98): many regulators sit at the threshold | Rule (pre-set) |
| D74 | 10-28 | Drug ranking weights every regulator tested in the disease by its NES, no MR cutoff (`79e` SCORE=weighted, default for v2) | FDR-cut MR lists are unstable (D73); the NES ranking is not. Reference diseases for specificity: every other tissue key, scored the same way | User |
| D75 | 10-28 | Drug side scores regulators with a consensus of ARACNe v2 TF and CollecTRI (`79e` REGULON=consensus): per regulator, mean drug activity and mean disease NES over the sets where it is scored on both sides. The knockdown benchmark (`79m`/`79n`) is reported, not used to choose a regulon per TF | The benchmark is too thin for per-TF choices (1,803 usable signatures, 1-5 per TF; every set passes 22-30% of TFs; ARACNe vs CollecTRI paired p = 0.6); the pre-set rule would have dropped 143 TFs on noise. ESR1 knockdown is read correctly by ARACNe, so the anti-estrogen inversion is drug-specific (GATA3 / FOXA1 rise under the drugs); CollecTRI's direct-target ESR1 regulon reads the drugs correctly, and the mean carries that | User |
| D76 | 10-28 | Line-match flag on direct drugs (`79e`, `79i`): line_match = Celligner weight of the drug's best measured line in the tissue key / the disease's top in-key line weight; < 0.1 -> unmatched_lines: kept in the ranking, kept out of tiers 1-2. Cutoff fixed from the distribution (bimodal: 25% of direct pairs < 0.01, 50% > 0.19) before rankings were looked at | With no matched line the score falls back to equal weights over the tissue's lines, so a disease gets a dissimilar line's response: BRCA-Basal anti-estrogens were scored on MCF7 (basal weight 0.0001), where ER blockade raises GATA3 / FOXA1, regulators that are down in basal tumors, and ranked 97-100th. Most affected: BRCA-Basal / Her2 (MCF7), GBM / LGG (LN-229), ESCA-SCC (AGS), LUSC (A549), PCPG (SK-N-SH), PRAD (PC-3) | User |
| D77 | 10-28 | Borrowed drugs judged by transfer confidence (`79l` VERSION=v2, run before `79e`): consistency model refitted with an excess-distance term (distance from the disease's patients to the drug's nearest tested line minus to the nearest DepMap line, Celligner 70-PC space; same definition in training). low_transfer = conf < 0.5 (fixed from the distribution before rankings; 11.5% of borrowed pairs, 132 drugs): kept in the ranking, kept out of tiers 1-2 | Flagging all borrowed drugs would empty tiers 1-2 for every cancer without its own lines (12 in v1; 8 of 10 subgroups). Distance was added as agreed but carries no information beyond drug consistency (CV R^2 0.602 -> 0.603 by drug, 0.596 -> 0.586 by held-out line): how well a borrowed drug transfers is a property of the drug | User |
| D78 | 10-28 | Every disease / subgroup result reports its evidence status (`79e` summary: best_line_match, tier 1-2 drugs matched vs borrowed, evidence_status = matched / borrowed only / no adequate compound, evidence_note). Requirement for the LLM coordination layer: `docs/LLM_COORDINATION_NOTES.md` (R1) | Gives a number-based way to say that no compound was tested in a line resembling the disease (e.g. BRCA-Basal: best line BT-20, match 0.018 vs cutoff 0.1), so an adequate compound cannot be reported rather than the top of a ranking built on dissimilar lines | User |
| D79 | 10-28 | Step 10 v2 subgroups (replaces D72's list), option B: every group passing D71 on the clustering without the recurring module (`results/step8_v2_tf_no_c8`; module as nuisance axis, D26): BLCA basal-squamous-like / luminal-like, HNSC basal-mesenchymal-like / atypical-classical-like, LGG-IDHmut-noncodel LGr2 / LGr3, PCPG-PHEO pseudohypoxia-Wnt / kinase-signaling, THCA BRAF-like-classical / RAS-like-follicular, TGCT-SEM C1 / C2; plus TCGA-COAD CIN-like / MSI-like as an explicit exception (prediction strength 0.748 < 0.75; original clustering). TCGA-ACC dropped. 14 subgroups (`85_step10_strata_v2.R`) | One clustering basis for all groups. Without the module the decision changed in 5 of 41 groups (ACC and PCPG-PGL lose, SARC-LMS, WT, LIHC gain); ACC's split is not significant on modules10 without it. COAD kept for its TCGA subtype agreement (ARI 0.42) and v1 external replication (GSE39582, CPTAC-COAD) | User |
| D80 | 10-28 | Reports lead with disease specificity (`spec_mech`, then `spec`: reversal relative to other diseases, proliferation removed), with overall reversal (`rev`) as context | By `rev`, 32 drugs are in the top 10% of >= 20 of 41 groups (statins, palbociclib, MEK inhibitors, anthracyclines); by `spec_mech`, none. Requirement R2 in `docs/LLM_COORDINATION_NOTES.md` | User |
| D81 | 11-01 | BRCA_LUM test cutoff (D68), fixed before the comparison. LumA and LumB group NES (msviper, one shared set of 1,000 normal splits) on pooled BRCA, BRCA_LUM, and 3 size-matched controls (BRCA_SUB1-3: 769 random BRCA tumors of all subtypes, seeds 1-3, built like BRCA_LUM). Material if, in LumA or LumB, either fires. (a) Ranking: weighted Spearman of NES (ranks over regulators scored in both networks, weight = mean |NES|) BRCA_LUM vs pooled is below every control's and at least 0.10 below the controls' median. (c) Known biology: ESR1, FOXA1, GATA3 or XBP1 has opposite sign on BRCA_LUM vs pooled, with |NES| >= 1.96 on at least one, and that flip happens in no control. Unweighted Spearman and top-100 overlap reported as context only. Material -> D68 subtype networks; not material -> subtypes keep the shared cohort network | (a) measures the change that reaches the drug side (every regulator, NES-weighted, D74); weighting stops the many near-zero regulators from dominating. A smaller network differs anyway, so (a) is judged against networks of the same size; 0.10 keeps control-level noise from triggering. | D108 | 11-06 | **Why trials failed, for the top 10 FDA drugs per disease and subgroup (user decision 11-06: top 10; stopped trials by rule + completed trials read by a model; same-class failures not included).** Rule fixed before running. **Pairs:** fda_rank <= 10 in `fda_ranking_v2_consensus.tsv` and `fda_ranking_strata_v2_consensus.tsv` (subgroups use the parent's condition). **Trials:** ClinicalTrials.gov API v2, intervention = FDA drug name, condition = 79o's trial condition, all pages; **kept only if the drug is a listed intervention** (FDA name, LINCS name, ChEMBL or Hub name, salt words removed, case-insensitive, as a whole word in an intervention name or other-name), because the search matches loosely. **Stopped** (terminated, withdrawn, suspended): `whyStopped` sorted by fixed keyword rules, first match wins: (1) **efficacy** (futility, lack of / insufficient / no efficacy, benefit, activity or response, interim analysis, did not meet, ineffective, negative result); (2) **safety** (toxicity, adverse event, safety concern / issue, side effect, death, not tolerated), skipped when the text says no safety issue; (3) **accrual** (accrual, enrollment, recruitment, participants); (4) **business** (sponsor, funding, financial, business, company, commercial, strategic, drug supply / availability, investigator left); (5) **other**; empty = **not given**. Only efficacy and safety count as the drug failing; accrual / business / other = stopped, not failed. **Completed** trials: abstracts of linked papers (CT.gov references of type RESULT or DERIVED; BACKGROUND excluded) from PubMed; a model (Claude subagents in this session, prompt saved with the outputs) reads each trial's abstracts and returns outcome (positive / negative / mixed / not reported / paper not about this trial), reason category (lack of efficacy, toxicity, both, other, not applicable), a one-sentence reason and a **verbatim quote**; an answer whose quote is not found in the abstract text (whitespace-normalized) is rejected and shown as not reported. The reader also records the drug's role (single agent, combination, or background in every arm); a negative trial in which the drug is background does not count as the drug failing (amended 11-06 before any reading was merged). Second amendment (11-06, before merging): the readers handled control-arm drugs inconsistently, so a role-only pass re-judges `drug_role` for all 520 trials with a fourth role, `comparator` (drug only in the control arm); only single_agent and combination trials count as the drug failing or succeeding. Third amendment (11-06, after seeing the first merge: NCT02711137, an INCB057643 trial stopped for safety, counted against azacitidine, a partner drug): stopped trials classed efficacy / safety also get a role judgment from their CT.gov arm groups and summary (same four roles); only single_agent and combination stops count as the drug failing. Fourth amendment (11-06, after the same case survived the third: azacitidine is a combination partner there, but the safety stop was blamed on INCB057643): the role reader also records whether the stop is attributable to the drug (yes / partly / no); a stop marked no does not count. Outputs are labeled model-extracted. Trials of other drugs in the class, and completed trials without a linked paper, are not covered. | Done |
| D109 | 11-06 | **Four evidence flags (user decision 11-06, after metronidazole / pentobarbital / cardiac glycosides / omacetaxine ranked high).** Rule fixed before running (after the metronidazole / pentobarbital / digoxin dose tables only). Signatures = LINCS 2020 `siginfo_beta` (all, any quality); active = is_hiq and TAS >= 0.4 (as 79c). Lines behind a score = as 79e: direct = the drug's active lines in the cohort's strict tissue key, borrowed = its active lines outside the key. Matched line = an in-key line with Celligner weight >= 0.1 x the cohort's top in-key weight (the D76 cutoff). (1) **`dose_inconsistent`**: per drug x line, at each time point with an active signature, the signatures at doses >= 2x the highest active dose (any quality) have median TAS < 0.5 x the median TAS of the active signatures; a profile with no higher dose is not judged. Row flag when >= 50% of the judged lines behind the score are inconsistent. (2) **`inactive_matched_line`**: borrowed row and the drug has >= 1 high-quality (is_hiq) signature in a matched line of the cohort, none active (a reproducible null); rows with only low-quality signatures there are counted as inconclusive, not flagged. (3) **`generic_mechanism`**: ChEMBL curated mechanism (molecule or parent) is a human core-machinery inhibitor: 80S Ribosome inhibitor, Protein synthesis inhibitor, Sodium/potassium-transporting ATPase inhibitor, DNA-directed RNA polymerase inhibitor (bacterial / viral targets excluded). (4) **`low_exposure`** (FDA drugs): Cmax at the highest recommended dose (steady state if given) from the drug's FDA label (openFDA clinical pharmacology / pharmacokinetics text), extracted by a model (Claude subagents) with a verbatim quote that must occur in the label text, converted to uM with the ChEMBL parent molecular weight; flag when the lowest active LINCS dose in the lines behind the score is > 10x Cmax (total, not unbound). No label / no Cmax = not assessed. **Use:** (1), (2), (4) are weak-evidence flags like unmatched_lines / low_transfer / local_route: shown and hidden by default on the pages, excluded from the FDA rankings (79p) and literature pairs (79o). (3) is shown, not hidden or excluded (like proliferation-driven). The FDA rankings and the D108 top-10 lists are rebuilt after the flags. | Done |

| D109a | 11-06 | **Plate-confinement flag replaces the dose rule (user decision 11-06).** Rule fixed before running. Compound plate = first token of the LINCS `sig_id` (e.g. REP.A007; the plate the compound was dispensed from, shared by every cell line run from it). Per drug, over all cell lines: **`plate_confined`** when (a) every active signature (is_hiq, TAS >= 0.4) comes from one compound plate, and (b) at least one other compound plate tested the drug at doses overlapping the active range (between half the lowest and twice the highest active dose) with >= 3 signatures, none active, and their median TAS < 0.5 x the median TAS of the active signatures. Drug-level: applies to every row of the drug. Use as the other weak-evidence flags (hidden by default, excluded from 79p / 79o). Reported: drugs flagged, and whether metronidazole and pentobarbital (the motivating cases) and established drugs (doxorubicin, temsirolimus, tofacitinib, erlotinib) are among them. | Done |

Not taken from the HGNC kinase list (it lists BCR) or from binding data (single stray BCR values; imatinib ABL1 6.6 and crizotinib ALK 7.4 medians below the 7.5 cutoff). Applies to every evidence-target path of `79i2` (FDA D105a main targets and the D102a default for other compounds); `79w` (flags only) unchanged. Everything else as D105b. **Expected:** alectinib / ceritinib / crizotinib target-absent where ALK fails the receptor rule (audit estimate ~67 group, ~20 subgroup rows); Bcr/Abl drugs unchanged (ABL1 broadly present). | D113 | 11-08 | **ChEMBL ids of FDA drugs resolved from the FDA ingredient name (user go-ahead 11-08; found under D112).** Rule fixed before running. Applies to every ranked FDA drug (a LINCS compound matched to Drugs@FDA) none of whose ChEMBL ids (LINCS annotation InChIKey match, or 79w name lookup, plus their ChEMBL parents) has a ChEMBL 37 mechanism record. (1) **Lookup:** the FDA ingredient with salt words removed (`fda_rows.drug_name`) as an exact ChEMBL preferred name; if none, as an exact ChEMBL synonym; the hit's parent compound is taken. Zero hits, or hits with more than one distinct parent: unresolved, unchanged, listed. (2) **Merge:** if the annotated entry and the resolved parent have the same InChIKey skeleton (first 14 characters: same connectivity, i.e. a duplicate entry of the same compound), the resolved parent is added to the drug's ids; otherwise (a different compound, e.g. tioguanine annotated as thioguanosine) it replaces them. The mapping is written to `data/raw/chembl/ChEMBL_37/d105_all/d113_ids.json`; `79w.chembl` applies it whenever that file is in its cache directory (79i2 only). (3) **Fetch:** ChEMBL 37 mechanisms, targets and activities (same filters as 79w) for the resolved parents, appended to the `*_by_parent.json` caches (`activities_by_parent.json` new, read by 79w.chembl and 79i2 D105b). The live API must report ChEMBL 37. *Amendment 11-08, after the fetch and before any pipeline step:* a resolution is kept only if the resolved parent has a ChEMBL mechanism record (12 of 44 had none; for 9 the resolved id was the annotated one, and for beclomethasone, clobetasol and fludrocortisone, removing the ester word found the parent steroid while ChEMBL records the mechanism on the ester, so replacing would only drop the annotated entry's activities); those 12 stay unchanged. No other rule changes: 79i2 main targets then follow D105a (curated mechanism first). Downstream as D112 (79i2, 79i3, flags, 79p, literature, D108 trials for new top-10 pairs, evaluations reported only, pages). | User |

| D120 | 11-08 | **Target-role score: is the drug's main target needed, altered or turned up in this cancer? (user 11-08: all three measures, decodable into its parts, with a full analysis of how each part moves the score).** Rule fixed before running; **reported beside the current ranking, not used to rank, gate or tier.** Unit: 41 groups x FDA drug rows (`fda_rows` compounds) whose D105b main targets are human proteins (`evidence_targets`; target_source chembl_mechanism / confirmed_annotation / annotation); drugs without a protein target get no score (counted). **Gene components** (group c, gene g; each a within-group percentile over genes, 0-100, ties averaged): **D dependency** = 79n `gene_dep` (weighted mean of -z Chronos over the in-key lines, z across all cancer lines; >= 2 screened lines, >= 25% of the weight), percentile over the group's genes. **A alteration** = share f of the group's TCGA tumors with an activating alteration: MC3 FILTER PASS missense / in-frame indel at a recurrent position (same gene and protein position in >= 3 MC3 tumors pan-cancer; denominator: tumors in MC3), or GISTIC thresholded +2 (denominator: tumors in GISTIC); either counts; f < 5% counts as 0; A = f - median of f over the groups with data, percentile over the group's genes. Groups without TCGA genomics (TARGET-NBL, TARGET-WT) have no A; tumor fusions are not on disk and are not counted (ALK / ROS1 / RET fusions are missed; stated gap). **O over-activity** = for a regulator scored in the group's master-regulator tables (ARACNe TF v2, CollecTRI v2): mean NES over the sets present, percentile over the group's scored regulators; any other gene: log fold change tumor vs GTEx normal (step 4 v2; adjacent normal if the GTEx contrast was skipped), percentile over the group's genes; groups without a normal contrast: regulators only. **Direction** (ChEMBL mechanism action_type of the drug on that gene): lowers activity (INHIBITOR, ANTAGONIST, BLOCKER, NEGATIVE (ALLOSTERIC) MODULATOR, ALLOSTERIC ANTAGONIST, INVERSE AGONIST, DEGRADER, DISRUPTING AGENT, STABILISER) -> components as above; raises activity (AGONIST, PARTIAL AGONIST, POSITIVE (ALLOSTERIC) MODULATOR, ACTIVATOR, OPENER, RELEASING AGENT) -> D and O become 100 - percentile, A is not scored; anything else (MODULATOR, BINDING AGENT, none, annotation-only targets) -> scored as lowering, flagged `direction_assumed`; both kinds on one gene -> lowering, flagged. **Gene score** T = mean of the available oriented components (>= 1). **Drug row**: the main target with the highest T; its gene, D / A / O percentiles, raw values (dep z, f, NES or logFC, O source) and action are reported; drug level (group x FDA ingredient) = mean over its gate-passing compounds (as 79l); `target_role_pct` = within-group percentile of T over the group's scored FDA drugs. **Evaluation (reported only):** primary = 79l pooled within-cohort known-use AUC of T, rows: anticancer, gate-passing, not excluded, T present (Hub and ChEMBL; 1000 permutations, 1000 bootstraps), next to `spec_mech_pct_pregate` on the same rows; also on the D118 primary rows (eligible). **Decomposition (all reported; none selects a variant):** (a) D, A, O alone (same rows, and on the rows where each exists); (b) T without D / without A / without O; (c) unsigned (every action scored as lowering); (d) coverage, Spearman between components and with T, and each component's share of T's within-group variance (squared Spearman); (e) top-10 per group kept when each component is dropped; (f) drugs whose main target is a DepMap common essential vs the rest; (g) the 79n yardstick pairs and the breast anti-estrogen controls, decoded; (h) T combined with spec_mech_pct_pregate (mean of the two percentiles); (i) split-half: AUC of T on groups 1, 3, 5... and 2, 4, 6... (sorted by name). **Forking paths:** the equal-weight signed T above is the only score this decision tests; any weighting, variant or component chosen after seeing (a)-(i) is exploratory and must be confirmed on data not used here (new label release or PRISM / GDSC) before it can rank anything. Known caution: dependency was already seen to separate known uses (79n D3), so D's AUC here is not independent. Script `scripts/79t_target_role.py` (runbook step `target_role`, after `dependency`); outputs `results/lincs/target_role/`. | User |

| D121 | 11-08 | **Three-layer drug-disease score: target role (layer 1, D120 components), drug-target engagement (layer 2: target units, binding, direction), drug-disease combination with learned weights (layer 3) (user 11-08: implement layer 3; draft defaults kept for the open questions).** Rule fixed before running; **reported only** (no ranking, gate or tier changes). Design after Open Targets (layer 1) and Rephetio / Hetionet (layer 3: weights learned on known indications, decodable, correction for many targets). **Layer 1** = D120 gene components D / A / O per group (unchanged). **Layer 2:** (i) **Units:** each ChEMBL mechanism target record (direct interaction) of the drug is one unit = its genes among the drug's D105b main targets (family / complex collapsed); main targets not in any record (annotation sources) are single-gene units. Direction per unit from that record's action types (D120 orientation). Unit component c_k = mean of its genes' oriented component k; unit role T_u(w) = sum_k w_k c_k / sum_k w_k over available components. (ii) **Binding:** gene potency = median valid pChEMBL (Ki / Kd / IC50 / EC50; no validity comment, no duplicates) over the drug's ChEMBL molecules (D105b); unit potency p_u = max over its genes; r_u = 10^(p_u - p_best), p_best = the drug's best measured unit; unmeasured units r_u = 1, flagged binding_unmeasured. Engaged E_u = 50 + r_u^gamma (T_u - 50). (iii) **Chance correction:** S = prod_u G_u(50 + (E_best - 50) / r_u^gamma), G_u = CDF of the role of a random unit of the same size and direction in the group (2,000 random units per group x size x direction from the genes with >= 1 component; fixed seed), i.e. P(best of the drug's N units drawn at random <= observed best); S x 100. **Label-free acceptance** (at equal weights, gamma 1): median within-group Spearman of S with the number of units in [-0.10, 0.10]; else fallback in order: (1) E_best without chance correction, (2) mean of E_u over units, (3) D120 T; the first that passes defines S (if none passes, D120 T, and the result says so). **Occupancy at Cmax** (reported only, drugs with a D109 Cmax): occ_u = Cmax / (Cmax + 10^-p_u M), total Cmax. **Layer 3:** drug level (group x FDA ingredient) = mean S over gate-passing compounds (79l rows); score = (1 - v) x pct(S) + v x pct(spec_mech_pct_pregate), both within-group percentiles over the scored rows. **Parameters:** w on the simplex in steps of 0.1 plus equal weights (1/3), gamma in {0, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2}, v in {0, 0.1, ..., 1}. **Fit:** within-group conditional logit, P(approved) proportional to exp(beta x score / 100), beta profiled on a grid 0-20 (step 0.25); the parameter set with the highest log-likelihood. **Rows:** anticancer, gate-passing, not excluded, S and spec present. **Baseline:** equal weights, gamma 1, v 0 (engaged target role alone). **Held-out:** fit on Hub; (a) leave-one-cohort-out: refit without the cohort, score it, pooled AUC of the held-out scores; (b) one test on ChEMBL labels after removing every Hub-approved pair; (c) breast controls: median within-group percentile of the final score, luminal anti-estrogens not lower and Basal not higher than the baseline. **Adoption:** the Hub-fitted parameters replace the baseline only if (a) and (b) are each >= baseline + 0.01 on the same rows and (c) holds; else the baseline is the layer-3 score. **Reported:** 200 bootstraps over cohorts (distribution of the chosen w, gamma, v); reverse fit (ChEMBL -> Hub minus ChEMBL-approved pairs); pooled fit; held-out AUC with gamma 0 vs 1 (binding effect) and v 0 vs the fitted v (does reversal add); D120 T on the same rows; every fallback mode's Spearman and AUC; coverage (units, measured units, family-level measurements not mapped); per-row decoding (score -> (1 - v) pct(S) and v pct(spec); S -> best unit, genes, c_D / c_A / c_O, w, T_u, r_u, p_u, E_u, N units). Not in D121: class weights (parked), the expression reliability check (deferred), chemotherapy and minority-alteration rules (open). Script `scripts/79t2_layers.py` (runbook step `layers`, after `target_role`); outputs `results/lincs/target_role/d121/`. | User |

The ChEMBL-only and pooled fits both choose equal weights (gamma 0, v 0); bootstrap over Hub cohorts: wD 0.10-0.50, wA 0.40-0.90, wO 0.00-0.30 (95%), gamma 0 in all 200, v 0-0.1. **Reversal (v):** never chosen above 0.1; spec alone 0.472 on the ChEMBL test rows. **Binding (gamma, equal w, v 0):** gamma 0 vs 1, Hub 0.660 vs 0.643, ChEMBL test 0.614 vs 0.615: relative binding does not help; occupancy at Cmax (2,918 rows with a Cmax for every unit) 0.657 / 0.612 vs relative binding 0.625 / 0.604 on the same rows (Hub / ChEMBL): absolute occupancy helps where it exists. **D120 T on the same rows:** Hub 0.674, ChEMBL test 0.589; the layer-2 baseline (units, mean, binding) 0.643 / 0.615: units trade some Hub for ChEMBL. Reverse fit (ChEMBL -> Hub minus ChEMBL-approved) uninformative (1 approved pair left). **Implementation detail (not in the rule text):** under weights that give zero weight to every component a drug has, its S is undefined; such rows take the neutral 50th percentile in the grid (the alternative, dropping rows per weight set, would change the rows between parameter sets). | D121a | 11-08 | **Lab binding strength stays in the score by design (user decision 11-08).** Relative binding (r_u = 10^(pChEMBL - best), engaged role E = 50 + r^gamma (T - 50)) is kept at gamma = 1 whether or not it improves known-use accuracy: the score is meant to predict, and a drug that binds a target weakly should not get full credit for that target's role. That population is defined identically in both cohorts and is **not** affected by target_absent / local_route / low_exposure, so the sides are like-for-like; rows carrying those flags stay in the test, because in the low cohort they are exactly the expected-low cases (dropping them deletes the negatives, as in the first D124 breast check: only 1 of 7 anti-estrogens is eligible in Basal). **Pairs, fixed now** (class = D105b evidence target genes; instances): BRAF/MEK (BRAF, MAP2K1, MAP2K2) THCA-BRAF-like-classical > THCA-RAS-like-follicular, 9; BRAF/MEK TCGA-SKCM > TCGA-LUSC, 9; EGFR TCGA-LUAD > TCGA-LUSC, 8; EGFR TCGA-HNSC > TCGA-LUSC, 8; HER2 (ERBB2) BRCA-Her2 > BRCA-Basal, 6; HER2 BRCA-Her2 > BRCA-LumA, 6; PARP (PARP1, PARP2) TCGA-OV > TCGA-LUSC, 3. **Primary set: 49 instances, 7 pairs, 4 classes.** **Declared before running, excluded from the primary and reported separately** (values already seen or prior decisions taken on them): anti-estrogens LumA / LumB vs Basal (7 each; D122 controls 77 / 66 vs 17, D92-D97, D102c); ALK LUAD vs LUSC (3; D102c, D110); CDK4/6 LumA vs Basal (1; palbociclib in the D122 controls). **Not computable, declared now:** AR antagonists PRAD vs BLCA (no AR drug is scored in BLCA). **Primary criterion:** over the 49 instances, the share with layered_pct(high) > layered_pct(low), one-sided sign test. **Pass = share >= 70%, p < 0.05 and median gap >= 10 percentile points.** **Reported, not decision-bearing:** per-pair share and median gap, and the per-pair median beside the pooled share; the same test on spec_mech_pct (reversal) and target_role_pct (layer 1 alone); the end-to-end reading in which an exclusion flag in the low cohort counts as a correct demotion; the previously-seen pairs. **Scope:** the drug side's primary validation generally, not only D125. **Caveats fixed in advance:** (1) it tests that known cancer-specific biology reaches the ranking, and cannot show the objective finds unknown biology - a pipeline could pass on layer 1's alteration component alone; (2) BRAF/MEK supplies 18 of 49 instances, hence the per-pair median; (3) the pairs were chosen for unambiguous biology, not sampled, and a pair added after seeing results is no longer pre-registered; (4) a biomarker-positive minority inside a low cohort (EGFR-mutant squamous lung) is a real patient group the test counts as a negative | Approval confounds efficacy with patents, market size, rare-cancer economics, sponsor strategy and trial history, so an unapproved pair is not a negative: the metric is positive-unlabeled and biased against a method whose purpose is to rank drugs highly where they are not yet approved. | D125-A1 | 11-10 | **A1 implementation choices (Claude, under the D125 pre-registration; written and committed before A1 runs). The spec fixes the lambda grid, the benchmark and the early stop; it does not say how a pairwise metric is read on a benchmark that has no disease vector `d`. These are the gaps, settled by the nesting requirement wherever it decides them.** (1) **Per-regulator read.** `rev_L` needs a `d`; the benchmark reads one regulator in one signature. Take `d = e_i`, the unit vector on regulator `i`, giving `s_i = (e_i^T K g) / sqrt(e_i^T K e_i) = (K g)_i / sqrt(K_ii)`. Forced, not chosen: it is the value `rev_L` returns for a disease whose only regulator is `i`, and at `K = I / lambda` it equals `g_i / sqrt(lambda)`, a positive rescale, so signs and percentiles are production's exactly. Any other reading breaks the `lambda -> inf` nesting that D125 rests on. (2) **The `lambda = inf` reference arm is computed at `K = I` literally** (production NES), not at a large finite lambda, so the baseline must reproduce the published D84 numbers; that identity is checked before any swept arm is read. (3) **Selection quantity = the consensus reading**, as D85 criterion (1) defined it: consensus NES = mean of ARACNe v2 TF and CollecTRI where both score the TF, else the one that does; pooled share of signatures read in the expected direction (`correct` as 79v: NES sign negative for knockdown, positive for overexpression). The consensus is what D125 changes (D75), and the two sets have unequal and overlapping signature counts (1,514 vs 828), so a pooled-over-sets share would weight ARACNe twice. Each set is reported separately. (4) **Benchmark slice.** D84's main analysis (knockdown + overexpression, overexpression kept only where the own mRNA is up when measured, `own_ok`) is the selection slice; the knockdown-only subset is reported beside it, as A3 names both. The no-own-check sensitivity is reported. (5) **Graph.** As A2, from the regulons *as pruned for the NES* (79n `prep`: >= 10 targets, >= 50% present, own gene removed from its own targets), per network and per regulon set: Szymkiewicz-Simpson `|T_i n T_j| / min(|T_i|,|T_j|)`, edges < 0.1 dropped, no self-loops, sign ignored; `L_sym = I - D^-1/2 A D^-1/2`. **Isolated regulators** (no edge >= 0.1) have `D_ii = 0`: their `L` row is the identity row, so `s_i` is `g_i / lambda`-scaled and they are read as production reads them. Reported: how many there are per network. (6) **ARACNe averaging unchanged (79n):** `K` is applied inside each network, then the transformed read is averaged over the signature's lineage-matched networks, else all. `K` is one Cholesky per (network, regulon set, lambda), cached. (7) **Grid and early stop as A3, unchanged:** lambda in {0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8}; best by correct-direction rate; if no lambda beats the `lambda = inf` arm by >= 1.0 percentage points, **D125 is recorded as not met and A2 does not run.** D84 is the selection set and is not an adoption criterion. (8) Outputs `results/lincs/d125/a1/`, scripts `scripts/d125/`; nothing at the top of `results/lincs` is written; 8 threads | A3 names the benchmark and the grid but not how `K` enters a one-regulator read, which set's rate selects, or what happens to an isolated node. Each is settled here before any number is seen; (1) and (2) follow from the nesting D125 claims, (3) from D85's precedent on the same benchmark, (5) from A2's own construction rule | Claude |

| D126-B0 | 11-10 | **B0 implementation choices and two spec corrections (Claude; written and committed before the cached prior is built). Settles the open item that blocked B0.** (1) **The open item - does OmniPath need a `config/datasets.yaml` entry of its own, being a service rather than a file? Yes, and it carries one key the other entries do not.** Precedent already covers services: `oncotree` is a live API pinned by the sha256 of its response plus the version in the URL, `uberon` a moving PURL pinned by sha256 with the retrieval date as its release. OmniPath differs in that it has **no data release label at all** - `omnipathdb.org/about` returns the server software version (omnipath-server 0.1.5), not a data version - and its content moves whenever an upstream resource does. So the entry records the query URL with every parameter, the retrieval date, the sha256 and row count of the cached filtered table, and the signed-and-directed subset size (not OmniPath's headline count, per B2); and it adds **`review:`**, stating that the resource has no upstream release, that the cached file is the dependency, and that a re-pull yields a different prior and is therefore a new decision rather than a refresh. Any D126 number names the retrieval date. (2) **Spec correction, field name: `consensus_sign` does not exist.** The risks section flagged that B2's OmniPath field names were recorded without fetching the source; this is that risk firing. The service returns `consensus_direction`, `consensus_stimulation` and `consensus_inhibition`. B2's 'consensus_sign true' is implemented as **exactly one of `consensus_stimulation` / `consensus_inhibition` is true** - both true means the consensus did not resolve a sign (134 edges), both false means it is unsigned (14,939). Nothing about the intended filter changes; only the name it is written in. (3) **Counting rule for B2's '>= 2 supporting resources or >= 2 references':** resources = distinct tokens of the `sources` field split on `;`; references = distinct PubMed ids of the `references` field (entries are `RESOURCE:PMID`), split the same way. Neither `n_sources` nor `n_references` is requested as a field, so the count is from the strings actually cached and is reproducible from the file. (4) **Scope: `datasets=omnipath`, the literature-curated core, organism 9606.** The extra datasets (pathwayextra, kinaseextra, ligrecextra) add interactions that are largely unsigned, which the filter would drop anyway, while widening provenance; taking them would make B5.2's degree confound worse for no coverage the filter can use. (5) **Access route: the REST API directly (`https://omnipathdb.org/interactions?...`), not OmnipathR.** OmnipathR 4.0.0 is installed but fails in this environment resolving the organism name through its cached `organisms` database (a dplyr join on an absent column inside `get_db("organisms")`). The REST route is in any case the one that makes B2's 'exact query parameters' a registrable string rather than a function call whose defaults can move. `curation_effort` is requested and cached but is not in the filter. (6) **`sign_disputed`** per B2: an edge kept by the filter where some source reports stimulation and another inhibition (`is_stimulation` and `is_inhibition` both true), recorded per edge so a later arm can drop them without a re-pull. (7) **The SIGNOR sensitivity arm is the same query with `resources=SIGNOR`**, cached, checksummed and registered the same way. (8) Outputs `data/raw/omnipath/` (cached tables) and `results/lincs/d126/b0_summary.tsv`; scripts `scripts/d126/`. Nothing in `results/lincs/*.tsv` at the top level is written | The open item asked a question precedent already half-answered; the answer that is actually missing is how to notice drift, hence `review:`. | D126-B0 result (2026-11-10): **the prior is built, cached, checksummed and registered; B0 is complete and B1-B3 are unblocked.** `scripts/d126/b0_omnipath.py`; `data/raw/omnipath/` (git-ignored as all of `data/raw` is, checksums in `data/raw/checksums.sha256` and `config/datasets.yaml`), summary `results/lincs/d126/b0_summary.tsv`. **Primary prior (datasets=omnipath, organism 9606, retrieved 2026-11-10): 86,189 raw interactions -> 16,518 signed and directed edges over 5,493 nodes** (11,662 activating, 4,856 inhibiting, 1,199 `sign_disputed`). **SIGNOR arm: 65,532 -> 10,346 edges over 4,364 nodes** (6,799 / 3,547, 919 disputed). **What the filter actually removes, and it is not what B2 implies:** of the 86,189 raw edges, 14,939 fail `consensus_direction` and 15,073 carry no consensus sign, but **54,598 are dropped for thin support** - a single resource and a single reference. Roughly two thirds of OmniPath's literature-curated core rests on one citation from one resource, so the '>= 2 resources or >= 2 references' clause, not the sign or direction clause, is what defines this prior. **Against B2's stated premise:** B2 chose OmniPath over SIGNOR because 'coverage is the binding constraint'. Measured, SIGNOR keeps **63% of the edges and 79% of the nodes**, so it is a near-equal alternative rather than a sparse fallback, and the sensitivity arm is a stronger comparison than the spec expected. Not acted on: the primary arm stays OmniPath as written. **Coverage preview for B5.3 (reported, no action):** of the 387 D105b evidence targets on scored FDA rows, 258 (67%) appear in the prior at all and only **179 (46%) appear as the source of an outgoing edge**, which is what a signed path from the target needs. A drug whose main target is not a source node has `mechanism_coverage` 0, so B5.3's floor (coverage >= 0.4 for >= 60% of gate-passing drugs) is at genuine risk before B1 runs. The B5.1 known-failure control is computable: ESR1, ESR2, ERBB2, EGFR, BRAF, MAP2K1/2, PARP1/2, AR, CDK4/6 and ALK are all present as source nodes (CYP19A1 and TOP2A are nodes but have no outgoing signed edge) | Done - B0 complete; B1 (signed path consistency) is the next step and is not started |
| D126-B1 | 11-10 | **Implementation choices for the mechanism-consistency flag (Claude; written and committed before it runs). Covers run-order B1 (signed path consistency), B2 (CARNIVAL breast arm) and B3 (criteria).** **Label note:** the spec's run order uses B0-B3 while its sections are numbered B1-B6; run-order B1 = section B3's primary method, run-order B2 = section B3's breast CARNIVAL arm, run-order B3 = section B5's criteria. Run-order labels are used here and in the outputs. (1) **Drug activity** = the per (drug, group) consensus regulator vector 79e builds at the point the score is computed (post-`consensus()`: ARACNe v2 TF and CollecTRI averaged over the regulators tested on both sides, direct / borrowed line weighting unchanged). It is dumped from 79e itself by a new off-by-default `D126_ACT_DIR`, which writes the matrix and exits before any output is written, so the quantity is the production one by construction rather than by reimplementation (the same device as `INDEX_FILE` in 79d and `D125_NES_DIR` in 79n). (2) **Main target** = the D105b `evidence_targets` genes (curated ChEMBL mechanism targets). The spec says 'the main target', singular, but 94 eligible drugs have one and 124 have two or more (up to 38). Rule: **the union** - a regulator is reachable if a signed path exists from any main target, and its predicted sign comes from the shortest such path over all of them. This is what B1's wording ('is there a signed path') asks and what CARNIVAL would do with several perturbation nodes. The single-main-target subset is reported separately, because more targets mean more chances to reach and so higher coverage - the same kind of confound B5.2 guards against for degree. (3) **Drug action sign**: +1 agonist / activator, -1 antagonist / inhibitor / degrader / blocker, read from the Hub and LINCS MOA strings exactly as 79v's `ligands()` reads them; a drug whose MOA gives both or neither has no direction and is **not scored** (35 of 287 eligible drugs). (4) **Path rule**: unweighted shortest path from the target set to the regulator in the filtered prior, predicted sign = drug action x product of edge signs. **No hop cap** - the spec sets none, and inventing one would be a new threshold; the path-length distribution is reported instead. If several shortest paths of the same length disagree on net sign, the regulator is `sign_ambiguous` and is excluded from both the numerator and the denominator, and counted. (5) **Quantities** as B3: over the **top 25** regulators by |activity| (the spec's number), `mechanism_coverage` = reachable / 25, `mechanism_consistent_frac` = consistent / reachable (ambiguous excluded from both). (6) **The flag threshold is not in the spec and is fixed here, before any result:** `mechanism_inconsistent` = `mechanism_coverage >= 0.4` **and** `mechanism_consistent_frac < 0.5`. Neither number is tuned: 0.5 is the no-information point of a sign comparison, and 0.4 is the spec's own B5.3 coverage floor, reused so the flag cannot fire on near-empty evidence rather than a new threshold being introduced. (7) **B5.1 control, made concrete:** 'anti-estrogens in MCF7' is read as the scorable (anti-estrogen, breast group) rows whose scoring lines include MCF7; anti-estrogen = a main target in {ESR1, ESR2, CYP19A1}, as D127 defines the class. **B5.1 passes if the flag fires on > 50% of those rows**; reported per drug and per group. If it fails, D126 is closed, as the spec says. (8) **B5.2**: Spearman of the per-drug flag rate against the main target's **out-degree** in the prior (out-degree because paths leave the target; total degree reported too). |rho| >= 0.3 triggers B4, whose rule is already fixed in the spec and is not restated here. (9) **Run-order B2 (CARNIVAL) is attempted after B1, and its prerequisites are already known to be absent:** no CARNIVAL R package, no Gurobi, no CPLEX, no highspy; `scipy.optimize.milp` (HiGHS) is present. The spec's ladder is followed in its own order and the outcome is recorded either way; **the heuristic is never silently substituted**, and if the arm does not run, B1's flag is reported as unlicensed outside breast, which is what the spec asks. (10) Scope: the D122-eligible FDA rows (`fda_rows.eligible`) over the 41 groups and 14 subgroups. Outputs `results/lincs/d126/`, scripts `scripts/d126/`; nothing in `results/lincs/*.tsv` at the top level is written | The spec fixes the method, the top-25 and the criteria but leaves four things undefined that a run cannot avoid choosing: which target when there are several, what to do when shortest paths disagree, where the flag fires, and what 'in MCF7' means for a per-group flag. Each is settled before the first number, and (6) reuses the spec's own floor rather than adding a threshold | Claude |
| D126-B1 result (2026-11-10): **B5.1 FAILS, so D126 is CLOSED by its own pre-registered rule. B5.3 fails too; B5.2 passes. Scope: 5,914 (drug, cohort) rows, 203 drugs with a main target and a known action direction, over 55 cohorts. **B5.1 (known-failure control): the flag fires on 0 of 22 scorable anti-estrogen rows in the four MCF7 breast cohorts, and on 7 of 22 (0.318) even with my coverage precondition removed - still under the 0.5 bar on either reading.** The spec's instruction is explicit: 'If it does not fire there, the filter does not work and D126 is closed.' **Worse than not firing: it endorses the known failure.** In the cohorts where the ESR1 misread lives, tamoxifen, toremifene and raloxifene come out 1.00 consistent in LumA and 0.83 in LumB, and fulvestrant 1.00 / 0.83. **Why, and this is the finding:** ESR1 is reached in **0** of the 22 control rows, because it is never among the top 25 regulators by |activity|. Those 25 are the proliferation block - FOXM1 (13 rows), MXD3 (12), PA2G4 (11), E2F2 (10), MYC (8), TEAD4 (5). So B3's selection rule points the mechanism check at growth arrest rather than at the drug's own axis, and an ESR1 antagonist does explain lowering FOXM1 and MYC, so the check passes it. The same proliferation dominance D80 / D85 found in the score reappears here in the *diagnostic meant to audit it*. **B5.3 (coverage floor): fails by a wide margin.** `mechanism_coverage` median **0.08** (2 of 25 regulators), max 0.48 over all rows; **37 of 5,914 rows** reach 0.4 and **0 of 203 drugs** reach it on their median. The signed prior simply does not connect curated drug targets to tumor-network TFs: path lengths run 2-4 hops (3,447 / 4,909 / 3,629 reached pairs) and only 24 reached pairs are the target itself. **B5.2 (degree confound): passes**, Spearman rho 0.165 over 203 drugs against main-target out-degree, so B4 is not triggered - though with coverage this low the check has little to bite on. **SIGNOR sensitivity arm:** sparser still, median coverage 0.04, 5,873 rows. **Single vs multi main target:** identical median coverage 0.08, so D126-B1 (2)'s union rule did not inflate coverage for multi-target drugs. **Run-order B2 (CARNIVAL, breast):** not executable as configured - no CARNIVAL package, no Gurobi, no CPLEX, no highspy; `scipy.optimize.milp` (HiGHS) exists but CARNIVAL does not interface with it. Per B3 the arm is recorded as not executable rather than the heuristic being silently substituted. It is moot in any case: its only job was to license the heuristic outside breast, and the heuristic failed its own control. **Not changed:** nothing in production; the flag was reported-only by design and is not emitted. A revived D126 would need a different regulator-selection rule (the drug's target-proximal regulators rather than its 25 largest shifts), which is a new decision with its own control, not an adjustment of this one | Done - D126 closed by B5.1; Part B complete, and with Part A closed at A1 the whole of SPEC_graph_metric_scoring is now resolved |

| D128-A0 | 11-15 | **A0 label-blind feasibility audit: implementation choices for the audit, fixed before running; Section 0 rows and D0.1/D0.2 NOT frozen (user decisions pending). No M or R value is computed and no outcome label is read.** (1) **Population:** the production lead rows, `results/lincs/layered_score_v2.tsv` (cohort x `pert_id`: 41 groups, 14 subgroups), joined to the production split tables (`drug_reversal_split_v2_consensus.tsv`, `stratum_reversal_split_v2_consensus.tsv`) for `source`, `passes_gate`, `line_match`, `transfer_conf`; gate failures stay in the table as `excluded`. LINCS compounds outside it (e.g. PLX-4720, SB-590885) appear only in the control-availability table. (2) **Target units:** the D121 units (`results/lincs/target_role/d121/units.tsv`, written by 79t2 `chembl_units`; `orient` / `action_flag` as 79t `orient()`), joined on `cmap_name`; `direction_assumed` / `mixed` -> M not scorable (P11), R unaffected (R uses no action sign). (3) **Source lines:** direct = the drug's profiled lines in the cohort's in-key lines (`disease_line_weights_long.tsv`; subgroups `stratum_line_weights_long_v2.tsv`), with their Celligner weights; borrowed = all the drug's lines (>= 3), as 79e; agreement with production `source` is reported. (4) **Footprint (counts only):** CollecTRI as the production object (`regulons_core.rds` collectri, export `data/processed/lincs/precursor_regulons/collectri.tsv.gz`); signaling prior = D126 cache `data/raw/omnipath/omnipath_signed_directed.tsv.gz` (SIGNOR arm counted as sensitivity). Hop 0 = a unit gene is a CollecTRI regulator; hops 1-2 = directed OmniPath edges to a regulator. A TF's path sign = product of edge signs over all its shortest paths; a `sign_disputed` edge counts as both signs; both signs present -> TF ambiguous. A gene's expected sign up to the drug's action = path sign x `tfmode`; resolved TFs that disagree -> gene conflicted; genes of ambiguous TFs count in totals, not in resolved. Footprint genes restricted to the 79c gene list, split landmark / inferred. Hub fraction = largest single TF's share of resolved genes. (5) **Floors (P10, proposed):** signaling unit >= 10 resolved genes and >= 2 anchored TFs; TF unit (hop-0 anchor) >= 10 resolved regulon genes; counts reported at x0.5 / x1 / x2. R pre-count: footprint genes with `adj.P.Val < 0.05` in the cohort's P2 contrast (groups `data/processed/step4_v2`, subgroups `data/processed/step10_v2`, 79t's contrast rule), reported against the same floors. (6) **Genotypes:** lines from DepMap 24Q4 hotspot / damaging matrices (BRAF, EGFR, KRAS, NRAS, ERBB2, ESR1, PIK3CA, RB1), `OmicsCNGene` for ERBB2 and RB1; patients from MC3 (FILTER PASS; BRAF V600; NRAS G12/G13/Q61; EGFR L858R, L861Q, G719X, exon 19 in-frame deletions, exon 20 insertions; KRAS G12/G13/Q61) and GISTIC2 thresholded (+2 amplification, -2 deep deletion), cancer type from the TCGA survival table. (7) **Profile audit:** every value of the specific and raw 79c matrices: |z| quantiles, share > 2 and > 4, landmark vs inferred; orientation checks (byte size, raw-vs-specific per-row correlation). (8) **External screens:** prior use found by grep of `scripts/` and `docs/DECISIONS.md` only; no PRISM / GDSC value is read. (9) Sandbox `scripts/d128/`, outputs `results/lincs/d128/a0/` (large tables `.tsv.gz`), threads 8 | The plan (Section 4 A0) asks for a feasibility matrix before any threshold is frozen; these choices make the audit reproducible without fixing Section 0 | Claude (implementation), user decisions pending |
| D128-A0 result (2026-11-15): **the audit ran; feasibility is recorded and nothing is frozen.** `scripts/d128/{config,d128io,a0_audit}.py`, `tests/d128/` (11 pass), outputs `results/lincs/d128/a0/`, log `logs/d128_a0.log`. No M or R value, no outcome label read. **Run history (reported, not hidden):** the first attempt was killed after 43 min without finishing - compute-bound on one core in the footprint loop, because `reach()` re-walked the same BFS for target genes shared across units and a pandas `.isin()` ran per reached TF; progress was invisible because stdout was piped through `tail`. Fixed by sign bitmasks, one cached BFS per gene at the largest hop arm (smaller arms filter on hop), regulon gene lists pre-restricted to the LINCS gene space, and a persisted reach cache keyed by the OmniPath/SIGNOR checksum (plan A1). **43+ min unfinished -> 141 s; 202 s for the full run.** **Population:** 12,283 cohort x pert_id pairs (9,288 group, 2,995 stratum) over 55 cohorts, 113,688 gate-passing (cohort, pert_id, line, unit) rows. Eligible for at least one of M / R: 3,931 of 9,288 group pairs, 1,270 of 2,995 stratum pairs; every other row carries a reason. Largest reasons: no anchored TF within 2 hops (28,893 rows), no protein target (8,220), M below floor (7,849), ambiguous action (3,327). **Units:** 497 D121 units - 399 signaling, 98 TF-anchored; M-eligible at the proposed floor 225, no anchored TF 226, ambiguous action 31, below floor 15. **ESR1 (the mandatory control):** the zero-hop anchor works - fulvestrant's ESR1 unit resolves **294 genes (40 landmark)** at hop 0, far above the floor of 10, which is what D126 could not reach. At 0-2 hops it expands to 38 TFs / 1,744 genes with MYC the largest contributor (32%). **Tamoxifen's ESR1 unit is `ambiguous_action` (MODULATOR), so under P11 only fulvestrant is M-scorable**: D0.2 decides whether the SERMs join it. **BRAF:** 15 TFs, 1,063 resolved genes (153 landmark), hub MYC 65% - hub dominance is the main risk to watch (E17). **Feature space (P4):** landmark genes are a median **13.1%** of a unit's resolved footprint; a landmark-only floor admits 185 units instead of 225. **Controls in genotype-matched lines (signatures):** A1 ESR1 67 (MCF7); A2 ERBB2 **12** (SKBR3: lapatinib 3, neratinib 3, afatinib 4, canertinib 2); A3 BRAF 104 (A375, HT29); **A4 EGFR 0 - confirmed not computable in context** (no sensitizing-mutant line; H1975 is T790M); B1 MEK 470; B2 CDK4/6 29 (MCF7, BT20, MDAMB231); B3 PARP 0 (no BRCA1/2-damaging LINCS line). **Patient strata (MC3 / GISTIC):** SKCM V600 209 / NRAS 124 / WT 121; LUAD EGFR-activating 52 / KRAS 146 / WT 317; THCA BRAF 287 / RAS 59; BRCA ERBB2-amp 133, RB1-loss 59. **Profiles (P9):** specific-arm |z| median 0.57 (landmark 0.86), q0.99 3.16 (landmark 5.59), share |z| > 2 4.7% (landmark 17.3%); tanh(z/2) saturates (f > 0.9) on 1.3% of values (landmark 7.5%), so the divisor 2 is not contradicted by the distribution. Orientation verified: raw-vs-specific same-row r 0.88 vs 0.20 shifted. **External screens (A0 action 7):** PRISM and GDSC are already read by `79j`, `79k`, `79m`, `09b`, `run_v2_drug.sh` and `d94/d94_decide.py`, and are cited in D94, D97, D102c, D119, D120, D122 and A43 - **PRISM is entangled with development and is not a clean independent label for A5**; GDSC appears only alongside it. **Two rule problems found and recorded, both pre-outcome:** (1) the ERBB2-amplification rule used a 99th-percentile cut and missed SKBR3 at CN 11.0 / percentile 98.7; changed to an absolute relative-CN >= 2.0 (the distribution is bimodal above it: q0.95 1.57, q0.98 3.61) and re-run, which is what made A2 executable. (2) **Unsettled, for the freeze:** a unit is typed `tf` when *any* of its genes is a CollecTRI regulator and is then floored on hop-0 genes only, so palbociclib (CCND1|CDK4) is `below_floor` with 0 hop-0 genes despite 1,758 resolved via signaling. The kind rule or the floor needs a decision. **Gaps recorded, not substituted:** 496 unit genes absent from OmniPath, 182 absent from all three sources, 86 population compounds without a D121 unit, no HRD table on disk, 1 line missing from the DepMap matrices. **Still open (blocks the freeze):** D0.1, D0.2, the unit-kind/floor question above, and whether A5's functional label can be anything other than PRISM | Section 4 A0 asks for the feasibility matrix before any threshold is frozen | Claude (audit), user decisions pending |
| D128-A0.1 | 11-15 | **Amendment of D128-A0: the unit-kind / floor rule is typed and floored PER GENE, then unioned (user approved 11-15, after the A0 audit flagged it; still label-blind, no M or R value computed).** **Rule as it was:** a unit counted as `tf` as soon as *any* of its genes was a CollecTRI regulator, and the whole unit was then floored on zero-hop genes alone; otherwise it was `signaling` and floored on >= 10 resolved genes and >= 2 resolved TFs. **Rule as it now is:** each gene of the unit is typed by how it actually anchors - a CollecTRI regulator contributes a zero-hop regulon footprint (`tf` subset), a non-regulator contributes a 0-2 hop signed-path footprint (`signaling` subset). Each subset is floored on its own genes with its own floor, and **the unit is M-eligible if either subset clears**. Genes are deduplicated across subsets before the unit's totals (plan 3.4: one count per unique gene per target unit), and a gene the two subsets disagree about is conflicting, not resolved. `unit_kind` is now `tf`, `signaling` or `mixed`, with per-subset gene counts and `cleared_by` reported so a mixed unit's basis is visible. **Why:** the old typing blocked wave B2 on an artefact - palbociclib (CCND1|CDK4, CCND1 being a CollecTRI regulator) was `below_floor` with 0 zero-hop genes although CDK4 resolved 1,781 genes through signaling, while 29 in-context signatures exist (MCF7 17, MDAMB231 9, BT20 3). Found before any score existed, so the correction cannot be outcome-driven. **Re-run deltas (same code, same inputs, 144 s):** units M-eligible **225 -> 238**, `below_floor` **15 -> 2**, unit_kind 98 tf -> 92 tf + 6 mixed; M-eligible rows **73,336 -> 80,666**, rows blocked by `M:below_floor` **7,849 -> 519**, M-admitted pairs 4,775 -> 4,940; the landmark-only arm gains most (units 185 -> 226 at floor x1), because per-subset floors drop conflicts imported from TFs the unit only reaches at 1-2 hops. **No unit lost eligibility (0), as the union rule requires.** **The 13 units admitted:** palbociclib's two CDK4/CDK6 units (cleared by `signaling`, 886 and 1,781 genes) and 11 TF-anchored units - regorafenib, bosutinib x2, vorinostat, ponatinib, imatinib x2, nilotinib x2, dasatinib x2 - that clear on their own regulon (10-18 genes) now that other TFs' conflicting expectations no longer suppress them. **The 2 still below the floor fail on data, not typing:** regorafenib::CHEMBL5122 (173 genes but only 1 anchored TF, so the >= 2 TF floor is unmet) and eptifibatide::CHEMBL2093869 (0 resolved genes). **Knock-on correction to a reported A0 number:** fulvestrant's ESR1 unit now resolves **478 genes (89 landmark)** on its own regulon, against the 294 reported in D128-A0, which had been intersected with the globally resolved set and so lost genes to conflicts from TFs reached at 1-2 hops from ESR1. The conclusion is unchanged and stronger: the zero-hop ESR1 anchor clears the floor of 10 comfortably. Cohort x compound pair totals are unchanged (3,931 group, 1,270 stratum), since the newly admitted units belong to pairs already eligible on another unit or on R. `tests/d128/` 13 pass (2 added for the union rule). Nothing frozen; D0.1, D0.2 and the A5 functional label stay open | A floor must test the evidence a unit actually has; typing a mixed unit by one of its genes discarded the other's footprint (D125-A0.1 precedent for a dated amendment) | User (approved), Claude (implementation) |
