A screen whose earlier use touched cohort, compound or parameter choice is development evidence, not an independent outcome label (plan A5 action 5). **CTRP is unused here:** grep over `scripts/`, `docs/` and `config/` returns only the D128 spec naming it as a candidate — no script, no decision, no prior result. **Source (`scripts/09c_download_ctrp.sh`, commit 09fdb1e):** the original Broad / CTD^2 `CTRPv2.0_2015_ctd2_ExpandedDataset.zip`, md5 `e764f6074a624d515d1b9d75e8b306e7`, from the CloudFront object the BHK lab CTRPv2 pipeline pins; the NCI CTD^2 path is retired (redirects to studycatalog.cancer.gov) and Zenodo refuses this network (403). Dabrafenib shows why the second number is the operative one: 0 lines in the strict intersection, 437 in CTRP. Fixed before any M or R value is computed.** **D0.1 — reference for R: keep the production contrast everywhere, including breast,** i.e. P2 unchanged (tumor vs GTEx where `signature_summary` status is ok, else adjacent, as 79t selects it). R is NOT given a special breast reference. **The expected outcome is recorded in advance, and is falsifiable:** on the P3 primary arm (CollecTRI), the 2026-11-12 precursor found ESR1 neither inverted nor lifted — rank 241/484 (Her2), 305 (LumA), 194 (LumB), median percentile 0.544 — so **R in the ER-positive breast cohorts is predicted to be uninformative, not inverted**; on the ARACNe sensitivity arm the same precursor DID invert (ESR1 ranked the anti-estrogens 1082/1082 Her2, 1124/1134 LumB, 930/1093 LumA, and highest in ER-negative Basal), so **inversion is predicted there**. Diagnosed cause: GTEx normal breast is itself estrogen-active, so ESR1 targets do not read as up in tumor. **Consequence: the mandatory anti-estrogen control is gated on M alone** (M carries no disease term and is unaffected); breast R is reported with this prediction beside it and is not read as evidence against a drug. Options (b) adjacent normal (LumA 62, LumB 22; Her2 skipped at 9) and (c) a within-tissue LumA/LumB vs Basal contrast are computed as **reported-only arms** for breast, never as the primary. **D0.2 — SERMs: fulvestrant is the primary positive set; SERMs are a second, separately reported set, breast-lineage lines only.** Fulvestrant is the only unambiguous ESR1 antagonist (ChEMBL `ANTAGONIST|DEGRADER`; 3 pert_ids, 18 MCF7 signatures; unit eligible, 478 resolved genes / 89 landmark on its own regulon per D128-A0.1). Tamoxifen, raloxifene, bazedoxifene, toremifene and clomifene are ChEMBL `MODULATOR` on ESR1 and therefore ambiguous under P11; they are scored as ESR1 antagonists **only in breast-lineage lines**, under the written tissue-context rule that SERMs antagonise ER in breast while acting as agonists in bone and uterus, and their results are reported as a separate set, never pooled with fulvestrant. Elsewhere `MODULATOR` stays ambiguous. **Tamoxifen's ESRRG unit must not stand in for ESR1** — it is a different receptor and its eligibility is an artefact of annotation, not evidence about ER. Control size: 18 signatures (fulvestrant alone) vs 67 (with the SERM set). **Gap, recorded not substituted: there is no agonist sign-flip control** — estradiol's unit is `ESR1:raise` and eligible, but it has 0 MCF7 signatures. **Scope:** this SERM rule governs the D128 control only; it does not change 79t production orientation, where these drugs remain `direction_assumed` | D0.1: a special breast reference chosen after seeing which one works would be the exploratory-tuning failure this plan exists to avoid; keeping P2 and predicting the result in advance makes a failure informative either way, and M is the better test of the method regardless. D0.2: option (b), fulvestrant alone, would rest the whole mandatory control on one compound, so a failure could not be attributed between the method and that drug's profiles; option (c), SERMs as antagonists everywhere, would corrupt the negative controls, which depend on anti-estrogens NOT scoring in ER-negative contexts | User |

The transform therefore neither flattens the bulk nor clips a meaningful tail, so the divisor stays 2. **P10 floors >= 10 genes / >= 2 TFs - UNCHANGED in value, corrected in application by D128-A0.1** (per-gene typing, per-subset floors, union): at x1 they admit **238** of 497 units and leave only 2 below the floor, both on data (one anchored TF; no resolved genes). x0.5 admits 239 and x2 admits 207, so the result is insensitive to the exact level and the floors are kept as proposed. **P11 action sign from 79t `orient()` - UNCHANGED, with the single exception D0.2 grants:** 31 units are `ambiguous_action` and stay unscorable for M; SERMs count as ESR1 antagonists in breast-lineage lines **for the D128 control only**, reported separately from fulvestrant, and tamoxifen's ESRRG unit may not stand in for ESR1. | D128-A1 | 11-15 | **Signed route index, implementation choices, fixed before running (Section 0 frozen; no drug response, no disease contrast and no outcome label is read at this stage).** (1) **Shared code:** the graph functions A0/A0.1 proved on the full input set are factored into `scripts/d128/graph.py` and imported by `a0_audit.py` and `a1_build_paths.py`; they are not restated. A latent bug found while writing the A1 fixtures is fixed here: the footprint memo was keyed on the network NAME, so two different graphs sharing a name could collide (harmless in production, where a name maps to one graph); the key now carries the graph checksum. (2) **No R export:** the CollecTRI regulons and the four breast ARACNe networks were already exported to `data/processed/lincs/precursor_regulons/` by `scripts/precursor/export_regulons.R` (2026-11-12), so `export_edges.R` is not written; the ARACNe sensitivity arm for the D0.1 breast prediction is already on disk. (3) **Expected signs are pre-action:** `target_gene_footprints` stores `action x path x tfmode` WITHOUT the action factor, which A2 applies. A1 therefore cannot depend on a drug's annotated direction, let alone its response. (4) **Three gene states, kept distinct:** `resolved` (one signed expectation), `conflicted` (resolved TFs disagree, expectation 0), `ambiguous_route` (reached only through an ambiguous-sign TF: counted in coverage, never signed). (5) **Both priors exported:** OmniPath primary and SIGNOR sensitivity in `target_tf_paths`, all hop arms 0-3, so P6's arms need no recomputation later | Plan Section 4 A1; reusing the audited code keeps one definition of a footprint across stages | Claude |
| D128-A1 result (2026-11-15): **pass gate met; the route index is built.** `scripts/d128/{graph.py,a1_build_paths.py}`, outputs `results/lincs/d128/a1/`, log `logs/d128_a1.log`, 1.6 s. **Fixtures (plan 6.1 `test_graph.py`): 14 hand-calculated cases pass** - zero-hop TF anchor, inhibitor-to-activator, two-hop sign multiplication, hop cap excluding a longer route, equal-length opposite paths staying ambiguous, shortest-path precedence over a longer opposite route, disputed edge ambiguous, cycle termination, shared gene counted once, shared gene with opposite expectations conflicted, multi-gene complex as one unit, isolated target yielding nothing (not zero), and sign inversion following the prior alone. 27 tests pass in `tests/d128/` overall. **Built:** `regulon_edges` 42,432 CollecTRI edges (1,178 regulators; 31,440 edges in the LINCS gene space); `target_tf_paths` 31,769 rows over **387 distinct target genes** (OmniPath 21,725, of which 8,795 ambiguous at the (target, TF) level; 25 zero-hop anchors); `target_gene_footprints` **367,942 (unit, gene) rows over 257 units** - 220,073 resolved, 80,059 conflicted, 67,810 reachable only through an ambiguous-sign TF. Units absent from the footprint table are the 226 with no anchored regulator and 14 with anchors but no measured regulon gene. **Ambiguity is preserved, not resolved away:** median per-unit TF-ambiguity fraction **0.091**, median gene-ambiguity fraction **0.247**. A quarter of a typical footprint therefore carries no signed expectation and is counted in coverage only. **ESR1 (the control's footprint): clean.** `fulvestrant::CHEMBL206` anchors at hop 0 with path sign +1 and resolves **478 genes (89 landmark), 0 conflicted, 0 ambiguous**; 405 genes expected up and 73 down BEFORE the action sign, so with fulvestrant's `lower` action M expects 405 down and 73 up in an ER-driven line. **Hub concentration, for E17:** vemurafenib/BRAF is 65% MYC and palbociclib/CCND1-CDK6 41% ESR1, against 23-29% SP1 for the ERBB2/EGFR units - hub dominance is unit-specific and must be read with each score. **No outcome dependence by construction:** expected signs are stored pre-action, and no drug response, disease contrast or label is opened at this stage | Plan Section 4 A1 | Claude |
| D128-A2 | 11-15 | **M/R engine: implementation choices. Every scoring parameter was already fixed by the Section 0 freeze, so only the items below were open; they are recorded here, and (noted as a deviation from the per-stage routine) after the engine ran rather than before - nothing in them can select an outcome, and the ESR1 control was run after them.** (1) **Pure functions:** `score.py` `score_mechanism()` / `score_rescue()` take typed arrays and return a `Score` carrying value, status, abstain reason, coverage split by feature space, excluded-unresolved and missing-response counts, and the per-gene contributions, so any score can be explained gene by gene. No file, label or global is read inside them. (2) **Zero is not NA:** a cancelled footprint returns 0.0 with status `scored`; an empty or under-covered one returns NaN with status `abstained` and a reason. Enforced by fixture. (3) **Missing response != 0:** a footprint gene absent from the profile is dropped from the numerator and counted in `n_missing`, never imputed as no change. (4) **R without an action sign:** R carries no action term, so a unit whose action is ambiguous under P11 is still R-scoreable; M and R coverages are tracked separately. (5) **Hub diagnostic (E17) is structural**, taken from A1's `path_ambiguity.tsv` (largest TF's share of the resolved footprint) rather than recomputed from weights. (6) **Null contamination found and fixed before the control ran:** the degree-matched-target null first returned a median exactly equal to the observation, because the candidate pool contained other drugs' units against the SAME target (every ESR1 drug has an identical footprint, hence an identical score). Candidates sharing any target gene with the real unit are now excluded, and the match is on resolved genes AND resolved TFs | Plan Section 4 A2 | Claude |
| D128-A2 result (2026-11-15): **pass gate met.** `scripts/d128/{score.py,nulls.py,a2_score.py}`; **51 tests pass** in `tests/d128/`, of which 24 are A2 fixtures. **Hand-calculated cases all match:** perfect agreement = +1, perfect disagreement = -1, a mixed 6/4 case = 0.2 tanh(1) = 0.15232, the action sign flipping the score, R opposing the disease direction (+1 / -1), and an R case = 0.4 tanh(1) = 0.30464. **Gate items:** every defined score lies in [-1, 1] (asserted in the runner as well as the fixtures); **a score of 0 is distinguishable from NA** (0.0 + `scored` vs NaN + `abstained` with a reason); a unit with no genes never gets a confident score (`no_usable_genes`); a duplicated gene raises rather than double-counting (plan 3.4); scaling all weights leaves the score unchanged; nulls are seeded and reproducible, preserve the sign balance, permute within feature space, and do not mutate the real target's arrays. **Smoke run:** fulvestrant x BRCA-LumA x MCF7, 3 rows, M and R both scored, 1.2 s | Plan Section 4 A2 | Claude |
| D128-A4 (ESR1 control) result (2026-11-15): **the mandatory anti-estrogen control is MET on the primary arm, with a small effect size stated plainly.** `scripts/d128/a4_controls.py`, outputs `results/lincs/d128/a4/`, 200 nulls per row, 9.9 s. Gated on M alone (D0.1). **Primary set, fulvestrant, MCF7, specific profiles (P5 primary): all three `pert_id`s positive - M = 0.0477, 0.0398, 0.0254 (mean 0.0376)**, each on the full footprint of **478 resolved genes (89 landmark, 0 missing)**. Against 200 seeded nulls per row: sign-shuffle percentile 100 / 100 / 93 (z 2.81, 2.92, 1.52), gene-label percentile 100 / 100 / 94 (z 2.69, 2.79, 1.56). **This is the result D126 could not reach** - it flagged 0 of 22 anti-estrogen rows because it anchored on the 25 largest regulator shifts, where ESR1 never appears; anchoring at the annotated target instead recovers the footprint and detects it above both nulls. **Effect size, stated honestly: M = 0.04 on a [-1, 1] scale is a weak signal** - averaged over 478 genes, expected and observed directions agree only slightly, consistently, and reproducibly across three independently profiled `pert_id`s. Whether that is worth anything beyond the production `layered_pct` is an A6 question this control does not settle. **Raw-profile arm (the P5 failure test): M falls rather than rises** - 0.0187, 0.0008, -0.0089 (mean 0.0035), so the plan's failure mode ('passes only on raw' = generic removal removes the target signal) **does not occur**; removing each line's generic response helps the target signal rather than hiding it, which supports the production choice. Curiously the raw arm still clears its own nulls (z 2.7-3.6): the generic component depresses the absolute value while the signed structure survives. **SERM set, reported separately and never pooled (D0.2), breast-lineage line only:** 11 of 12 (`pert_id` x unit) rows positive on the specific arm - tamoxifen 0.027-0.053, raloxifene 0.055 (27 signatures), bazedoxifene 0.038, toremifene 0.075-0.092; the exception is clomifene `BRD-K04218075` at -0.059 (one signature, null percentile 32). Tamoxifen carries two annotated ESR1 units (`ESR1|ESR2` and `ESR1`), both reported; its ESRRG unit is excluded per D0.2. **Breast R against the recorded D0.1 prediction - the prediction held, and the cohort ordering remains the known problem.** In the three ER-positive cohorts R is **uninformative exactly as predicted**: LumA 0.0098, LumB 0.0039, Her2 0.0009 (per-`pert_id` range -0.016 to 0.017), neither inverted nor lifted on the CollecTRI primary arm. But **R is highest in ER-negative BRCA-Basal, 0.0492**, five to fifty times the ER-positive cohorts - the same ordering the 2026-11-12 precursor found, and consistent with its diagnosis that GTEx normal breast is itself estrogen-active. **This is not evidence for fulvestrant in Basal**; it is the recorded reference problem reproducing, and nothing was adjusted in response. **Gap, as D0.2 requires it be stated wherever the control is reported: there is no agonist sign-flip control** - estradiol's unit is eligible but it has 0 MCF7 signatures | Plan Section 4 A4 action 1; D0.1, D0.2 | Claude |
| D128-A3 | 11-16 | **Cohort aggregation, uncertainty and baseline joins: implementation choices, fixed before running (Section 0 frozen by `d128-a0-freeze`; nothing below changes P1-P14).** A3 needs the first full A2 scoring run, so the A2 run parameters are declared here too. **(1) Comparison population - declared before any score is read.** A2 is run over **every** eligibility row of `results/lincs/d128/a0/eligibility.tsv.gz` that has a target unit and a source line (**113,688** rows = cohort x `pert_id` x source line x target unit; 41 groups + 14 subgroups), not only the 82,426 rows A0 predicted eligible. Reason: the engine's own abstentions are then produced by the engine and can be cross-checked against A0's prediction, which is a stronger audit than assuming A0 was right. The **ranking population** is narrower and is the declared comparison population: rows whose M (respectively R) is **scored**, ranked **within (cohort, level)** and never across cohorts. Percentiles are reported beside raw M and R. Rows A0 called `excluded` (`fails_production_target_gate`) are scored but are **flagged and excluded from every percentile**, exactly as production excludes them from the lead ranking. **(2) Aggregation order (plan 3.4):** source lines first, **within** a target unit - weighted mean of M (and of R) over the drug's source lines with the 79e / P14 direct-or-borrowed population and Celligner weights carried unchanged from A0 (A0 reproduced production's direct/borrowed call on **12,283 of 12,283** pairs; that check is re-asserted in A3 and the run fails if it no longer holds) - then target units, **unweighted mean over scoreable units** (P13). Abstained lines and abstained units are dropped from their mean, never counted as 0; the fraction of annotated units that stayed unscorable is kept as `unit_scored_fraction`. **(3) Uncertainty:** `line_heterogeneity.tsv` reports, per (cohort, `pert_id`, unit), the per-line M and R, their weighted sd, min/max, range, the weighted-vs-unweighted difference, the number of lines, the borrowed fraction, the best line match, the transfer confidence and the source line's genotype flags from `a0/source_line_context.tsv`, so spread from genotype mismatch can be separated from spread from noise. Single-line drugs get sd = NA, not 0. **(4) Null budget, declared in advance:** per **scored M row**, 200 sign-shuffle draws and 200 gene-label draws (the same seeded permutations as A2/A4, `SEED = 128`), and up to 20 degree-matched alternative target units. The two permutation nulls are computed by a vectorised shortcut that is **exact** where every expected sign is non-zero and every response is finite (the normal case: footprint genes are already restricted to the LINCS gene space); where it is not exact the per-draw `score_mechanism` path is used instead, and `tests/d128/test_scores.py` asserts the two agree element-wise. The degree-matched pool keeps the D128-A2 contamination exclusion (no candidate sharing a target gene with the observation) and adds one fix, declared here before running: **candidates are deduplicated by their resolved footprint** (gene set x expected sign), because the 497-unit pool holds many units that are the same target gene under different drug names and they are not independent draws. A degree-matched null with **fewer than 3 distinct footprints is reported as `degenerate`** and its z is NA rather than a number; this is a reporting rule, not a rescue. **Contingency, fixed now:** if the full run with this budget exceeds 3 h, the degree-matched null is cut to a seeded stratified subsample and the cut is stated in the result row. **(5) Gene-level contributions** are written for a seeded random sample of 2,000 scored rows plus every A4 control row, not for all ~82k (that would be ~40 M rows); per-row hub and coverage summaries are kept for all rows. **(6) Baseline joins - read, never recomputed.** `layered_pct`, `layered_S`, `minority_alteration`, `essential_target`, `dna_acting` and the D124b behaviour from `results/lincs/layered_score_v2.tsv`; `rev_pct`, `spec_mech_pct`, `spec_mech_pct_pregate`, `tier_lead`, `source`, `line_match`, `transfer_conf` from `drug_reversal_split_v2_consensus.tsv` / `stratum_reversal_split_v2_consensus.tsv`; `T` from `target_role/compound_target_role.tsv` on (cohort, `pert_id`); `target_role_pct` from `target_role/drug_target_role.tsv` on (cohort, `fda_ingredient`), which is a **drug-level** table and exists for group cohorts only - subgroup rows get NA and that is recorded, not imputed. Every join is `validate='one_to_one'` or `'many_to_one'` and `baseline_join_audit.tsv` records, per source, the rows in, matched, unmatched, duplicated and the **value-identity check** (the joined column must equal its source value exactly on every matched row; a mismatch fails the stage). **(7) Abstentions:** `abstentions.tsv` accounts for 100% of the 124,168 A0 candidate rows - scored, abstained with the engine's reason, or never entered with A0's reason - and cross-tabulates the engine's verdict against A0's prediction. **(8) No new lead score.** M and R are reported beside the production columns; nothing is combined (plan 3.4 item 5). | Fixed before running |
| D128-A3 landmark sensitivity | 11-16 | **Amendment of D128-A3, added by the user before the scoring run and before any landmark score is read. A POST-FREEZE SENSITIVITY ANALYSIS, not a parameter change: P4 (all 10,112 BING genes) stays the primary arm whatever this shows, unless the user decides otherwise.** **Why:** the A4 ESR1 control rests on 478 footprint genes of which only **89 are physically measured landmarks**; the other 389 are inferred by the L1000 model from those landmarks. A0 compared landmark vs all-gene **coverage** (the landmark floor admits 226 units, the all-gene floor 238); this compares the **scores**. **What runs:** every unit of the declared A3 comparison population is scored twice in the same pass - arm `all_bing` (frozen primary) and arm `landmark_only` (the identical footprint restricted to the 978 measured landmark genes, with the P10 floors applied to the landmark count, so a unit may clear one arm and abstain in the other). Output `results/lincs/d128/a3/landmark_sensitivity.tsv` plus a summary table. **PRE-STATED CRITERION (fixed here, before the result is seen). The two arms are called interchangeable only if ALL THREE hold:** (i) Spearman rho(M_all, M_landmark) **>= 0.90** across the population; (ii) sign-flip rate **<= 5%** of units scored in both arms; (iii) median |delta percentile| **<= 5** points. All three numbers are reported either way, the same three for R, and the count of units that fall below the floor in `landmark_only` but not in `all_bing`. **How it cuts, decided now so the interpretation is not chosen after the fact:** if the criterion **is met**, the inferred genes add no distortion and `all_bing` is kept for its coverage advantage, with this result as the justification; if it is **not met**, the inferred genes materially drive the scores, biological signal cannot be separated from L1000 imputation, `landmark_only` becomes the honest primary, and that is a finding about the **whole pipeline**, not only D128, and is reported as such. Also reported: the subset of units whose landmark fraction is far below the A0 median of 15.0%. **Conditional follow-on (user):** the 200 + 200 sign-shuffle and gene-label nulls are run across the whole population **only if the criterion is met**; if it is not, that compute is not spent and the reason is reported. The scoring run is therefore in two phases - both gene arms scored together in one pass with no nulls, then the gated null pass - which keeps the landmark arm inside the A3 scoring run as asked while leaving the null spend genuinely conditional. **Also reported, on the degree-matched null:** its `n_null` distribution over the whole population and how many units have fewer than 10 surviving candidates, because the A4 ESR1 control returned a blank z (one surviving candidate after the shared-target exclusion). If that null is thinly powered for most units, the specificity claim it supports is correspondingly weak and is reported that way. | Fixed before running |
| D128-A4 (actions 2-5) | 11-16 | **Implementation choices for the remaining A4 controls, fixed before running. Action 1 (the mandatory MCF7 ESR1 control) ran on 11-15 and is not repeated.** **(0) Files** - a naming deviation from the plan, recorded: actions 2 and 3 are `scripts/d128/a4b_controls.py`, action 4 is `a4_d127_compat.py` (as the plan names it), action 5 is `a4_mechanism_cases.py`. `a4_controls.py` is left untouched so action 1's output stays byte-reproducible. **(1) Control and negative sets are genotype RULES over `results/lincs/d128/a0/source_line_context.tsv`, not hand-picked line lists**, so membership is reproducible: *A2-HER2* (lapatinib, neratinib, afatinib, canertinib, tucatinib; anchor ERBB2) positive = `ERBB2_amp_candidate`, negative = not `ERBB2_amp_candidate`; *A3-BRAF* (vemurafenib, dabrafenib, encorafenib, PLX-4720, SB-590885; anchor BRAF) positive = `BRAF_V600`, negative = `KRAS_NRAS_hotspot` **and** BRAF-wild-type (the RAF-inhibitor paradox context); *A4-EGFR* (gefitinib, erlotinib, afatinib, osimertinib, dacomitinib; anchor EGFR) positive = `EGFR_sensitizing`, negative = `KRAS_NRAS_hotspot` **and** EGFR-wild-type. A unit enters a wave only if its annotated gene set contains the wave's anchor gene. **(2) Stated gaps, not substituted:** A0 already established that **EGFR wave A4 is not computable as designed** - no HCC827, PC9 or HCC4006 among the LINCS lines; NCIH1975 carries a sensitising EGFR mutation *and* T790M, so it is reported as a resistance-genotype line and never as the missing sensitive one. **HER2 wave A2 is executable but thin (12 signatures on SKBR3)** and is reported with that uncertainty attached. No other line is quietly put in their place. **(3) `confident positive`, defined before any control is read:** M scored (so the P10 floor cleared), M > 0, **and** both permutation nulls at percentile >= 95 (200 seeded draws each, the same generator as A2/A4 action 1). **(4) Pass and fail.** *Action 2* passes if the positive sets yield interpretable target-linked footprints - a scored M with its gene, landmark and ambiguity coverage reported - or a documented reason they cannot. A large M is **not** required and a small one is not a failure; what is required is that the number can be explained gene by gene. *Action 3* **fails** if the negative set's share of confident positives is greater than or equal to the positive set's share; both shares are reported whichever way it falls. *BRAF hub check:* the share of the signed numerator carried by MYC-regulated genes is computed and reported with no threshold attached to passing, but **if MYC-regulated genes carry >= 50% of the signed numerator the BRAF M is reported as hub-dominated and is not read as evidence of BRAF-specific mechanism**; genes carried by MYC and by no other anchored resolved TF are counted separately, and M is recomputed with and without them. **(5) Action 4 is descriptive only.** The 67 D127 instances (49 primary) are replayed with their exact compound (`cmap_name`) and cohort definitions from `results/lincs/d127/instances.tsv`; per P12 each `pert_id` is reported and the instance verdict uses their mean, with the per-`pert_id` spread kept. **Nothing is tuned on them and no threshold is set from them.** **(6) Action 5 case definitions** (fixed here): missing network path, below floor, ambiguous action, sign contradiction (>= 50% of the reached genes excluded because the routes disagree), inferred-only genes (zero landmark genes in the numerator), absent target expression (**log2(TPM+1) < 1.0** in every source line, DepMap 24Q4), cytotoxic signature (production `prolif_driven` or `prolif_effect_pct` >= 90 with M > 0), line mismatch (borrowed, or `line_match` < 0.1), unknown genotype, polypharmacology (the drug's own units disagree in sign). **(6a) Both gene arms.** Added before running, after the A3 landmark sensitivity result: the A3 criterion was **not met**, so every A4 control, the BRAF hub analysis included, is computed and reported on both `all_bing` (frozen primary) and `landmark_only`. A control that passes on inferred genes and not on measured ones is reported as exactly that. **(7) Out of scope, enforced:** A5 does not run and **no CTRP, PRISM or GDSC value is read** in this batch; A5 needs its own `d128-eval-freeze` design, which is not written. | Fixed before running |
| D128-A3 result (2026-11-16): **the stage ran and its join gate is met; the scores themselves are small, dominated by between-line spread, and nearly independent of the production lead score. A3 cannot say whether they add anything, and it is not designed to.** `scripts/d128/{aggregate,baselines,landmark,a3_aggregate,a3_null_power}.py`; A2 full run `results/lincs/d128/a2/`, A3 outputs `results/lincs/d128/a3/`, logs `logs/d128_a2_full.log`, `logs/d128_a3.log`. **Scale:** the declared population, 113,688 (cohort, `pert_id`, source line, target unit) rows, scored in **282 s** on 8 threads, twice (both gene arms) = 227,376 scored rows. M scored on **81,043** rows (`all_bing`), R on 77,337. Aggregated to **17,530** (cohort, drug, unit) rows and **10,131** (cohort, drug) rows - the 12,283 production pairs minus the 2,152 with no target unit at all. M is defined on **4,940** drug rows, R on 5,037. **Gate: met.** No join lost or duplicated a row (10,131 in, 10,131 out, four sources). **P14 re-asserted: 12,283 / 12,283 pairs reproduce production's direct/borrowed call.** The two independent copies of `layered_pct` (lead table vs split tables) are the same number to within **5e-4** - the lead table stores it rounded to 3 dp. The two copies of the target-role `T` differ on **16 of 7,661** rows, and the cause is documented, not a bug: `drug_target_role.tsv` is keyed on (cohort, `fda_ingredient`) and aggregates the compounds sharing an ingredient (FLUDARABINE PHOSPHATE, FLUTICASONE PROPIONATE each have two `cmap_name`s), so **`target_role_pct` is a drug-level value shared by both `pert_id`s** and is reported as such. `target_role_pct` and `T` are group-level only: **2,470 of 10,131 rows (every subgroup) are NA and are not imputed.** `abstentions.tsv` accounts for **100% of the 124,168 A0 candidate rows** in 18 categories, with the engine's verdict cross-tabulated against A0's prediction. **Effect size, stated plainly:** M median **0.0049**, IQR -0.0089 to 0.0194, max 0.324; 63% of scored rows positive. R median 0.0063. **Between-line spread exceeds the score:** over the 8,916 unit rows with more than one source line, the weighted sd of M across lines has median **0.0170** against a median |M| of **0.0147** (ratio 1.15), and the full range has median 0.053. At cohort level M is therefore smaller than the disagreement between the lines it is averaged over. 76.7% of unit rows are borrowed. **Independence from the lead score:** Spearman within the population, M percentile vs `layered_pct` **0.17**, vs `spec_mech_pct_pregate` -0.04, vs `target_role_pct` 0.16, vs `prolif_effect_pct` -0.12; R percentile vs `rev_pct` **0.43**, vs `layered_pct` 0.15; M vs R 0.22. So M measures something the lead score does not, and R overlaps substantially with VIPER reversal - **but A3 holds no outcome label, so it cannot say whether M's independent component is signal or noise. That question belongs to A5/A6 and is open.** **One engine-vs-frozen-floor mismatch, flagged not hidden:** P10 as frozen by D128-A0.1 floors each subset of a unit separately, while `score.py` applies only the >= 10-gene part to the merged footprint, so **55 drug rows (regorafenib, one of its units) are scored although the frozen floor would not admit that unit**. The rows carry `n_units_scored_below_a0_floor` and are **for the user to review**; the engine should be brought into line with the frozen floor before A5/A6. | Gate met; effects small; the added-value question is NOT answered by A3. Open for the user: the regorafenib floor mismatch |
| D128-A3 landmark sensitivity result (2026-11-16): **the pre-stated criterion is NOT MET, on all three parts, and not marginally. By the rule fixed before the result was seen, this is a finding about the whole pipeline, not only about D128.** Outputs `results/lincs/d128/a3/landmark_sensitivity{,_summary}.tsv`. Every unit was scored twice in one pass: `all_bing` (the frozen P4 primary) and `landmark_only` (the same footprint restricted to the 978 measured genes, floors applied to that count). **M, over the 10,141 units scored in both arms: Spearman rho 0.677 (needed >= 0.90), sign-flip rate 25.0% (needed <= 5%), median |delta percentile| 9.87 points (needed <= 5). R: rho 0.740, flips 21.0%, median |delta pct| 10.11.** It gets worse exactly where it should if imputation is driving the scores: on the quarter of units with the lowest landmark fraction (<= 12.5%), M's rho falls to **0.423** and the sign-flip rate rises to **37.3%**; below the A0 median landmark share of 15.0%, rho 0.551 and flips 30.0%. **567 units clear the floor on all BING genes and fall below it on measured genes alone; none goes the other way.** The median footprint is 957 genes of which the median landmark share is **14.3%**. A worked example: fulvestrant's ESR1 M in MCF7 is 0.048 / 0.040 / 0.025 on 478 genes (89 landmark) and **0.119 / 0.088 / 0.063 on the 89 measured genes alone** - a 2.5x larger score with wider nulls, so the sign-shuffle z falls from 2.8 / 2.9 / 1.5 to 1.9 / 1.4 / 0.8. **Consequence under the rule fixed in advance:** a quarter of units change the sign of their mechanism score depending on whether imputed genes are included, so biological signal cannot be separated from L1000 imputation, and `landmark_only` is the honest primary. **Nothing was changed in response:** P4 stays frozen and `all_bing` stays the primary arm until the user decides, and both arms are reported everywhere, A4 controls included. **The conditional follow-on was therefore NOT spent:** the 200 + 200 permutation nulls were not run over the whole population, exactly as the amendment said. **Degree-matched null power, measured structurally over the whole population (no null was run to get this):** of the 238 M-eligible units, **95.0% have fewer than 10 distinct candidate footprints** after the shared-target exclusion and the footprint de-duplication, **27.7% have fewer than 3 (degenerate)** and **9.7% have none at all**; median 5. Weighted by the population rows that use each unit: 94.2% under 10, 22.8% degenerate, 13.9% with none. The ESR1 blank z was not an accident - the whole degree-matched pool for ESR1 collapses to one distinct footprint (AR). **The specificity claim this null is meant to support is therefore weak nearly everywhere**, and only the two permutation nulls carry weight. | NOT MET. Open for the user: whether `landmark_only` becomes the primary arm pipeline-wide (this is a pipeline-level decision, not a D128 one) |
| D128-A4 (actions 2-5) result (2026-11-16): **BRAF passes its negative-control gate and is the only informative wave - but its M is MYC-hub-dominated, so it is not evidence of BRAF-specific mechanism. HER2 fails the negative rule on a positive set too small to carry it. EGFR is a stated gap. The D127 replay says M does not reproduce the biomarker discrimination the production score achieves.** `scripts/d128/{a4b_controls,a4_d127_compat,a4_mechanism_cases}.py`; outputs `results/lincs/d128/a4/`, log `logs/d128_a4.log`, 4.4 s, 200 seeded nulls per scored row, both gene arms. **Action 2 / 3, BRAF (wave A3, the richest):** 4 positive rows (vemurafenib and dabrafenib in A375 and HT29, 73 signatures) **M = 0.025-0.041, all positive, sign-shuffle percentile 89.5-96.5, 3 of 4 confident positives**; 7 negative rows (the RAF-inhibitor paradox context: BRAF-wild-type and RAS-active) M -0.030 to +0.036 with null percentiles 0.5-76 and **0 confident positives**. **The negative control passes: 0% against 75%.** One caveat kept in view - vemurafenib in A549 reaches M = 0.036, larger than either positive, and is excluded only by its null (percentile 57.5 on a single signature); the separation comes from the null calibration, which widens with few signatures, not from M alone. **THE BRAF HUB NUMBER (the check that was asked for): the BRAF unit's footprint is 1,063 sign-resolved genes, of which 692 - 65.1% - are MYC-regulated, and they carry 65.9-68.5% of the absolute signed numerator in every profile. In the four BRAF-V600 positive rows MYC-regulated genes carry 69%, 78%, 86% and 95% of the signed numerator. 610 of the 692 (57% of the whole footprint) are reached by MYC and by no other anchored resolved regulator. By the >= 50% rule fixed in advance, BRAF's M is HUB-DOMINATED: it is substantially a read-out of a MYC-driven proliferation program routed through the BRAF anchor, not a BRAF-specific mechanism measurement.** On landmark genes alone it is worse: 117 of 153 genes (76.5%) are MYC-regulated. M without MYC-regulated genes drops to 0.004-0.026 against 0.025-0.041 with them. **Action 2, HER2 (wave A2, declared thin in advance):** 4 positive rows on SKBR3 (10 signatures), M -0.007 to +0.004, **0 confident positives**; 47 negative (ERBB2-non-amplified) rows with **7 confident positives (14.9%)**. By the rule fixed in advance the negative set's share (0.149) exceeds the positive set's (0.000), so **HER2 FAILS action 3** - with the honest qualification that a 4-row positive set cannot support any verdict, as A0 warned. **Action 2, EGFR: the stated gap held.** The only line carrying a sensitising EGFR mutation is NCIH1975, which also carries T790M; its single afatinib row gives M = 0.0018 at null percentile 30. No substitute line was used. Its 19 negative rows produce 2 confident positives (10.5%). **Compounds with no D121 target unit and therefore no control row: canertinib, tucatinib, encorafenib, PLX-4720, SB-590885** - recorded, not worked around. **Action 4, the D127 replay (descriptive only, nothing tuned):** all 49 primary instances are computable. **M points the right way in 16 of 49 (32.7%) and its percentile in 25 of 49 (51.0%), against `layered_pct`'s 73.5% - and the reason is structural, not a near miss.** M carries no disease term, so for a drug whose source lines are the same in both cohorts the two sides are **exactly** equal: **15 of 49 instances are exact ties** (all 10 borrowed-in-both instances and 12 of the direct-in-both ones, including all 9 THCA BRAF-like vs RAS-like instances). Among the 34 instances where M can differ at all it points the right way **47.1%** of the time - chance. R: 36.7% over the 49. **M as built cannot discriminate a biomarker pair for a borrowed drug, by construction.** **Action 5, failures by mechanism** (`mechanism_case_reports/`, 10 cases over the 10,131 drug rows): missing network path **5,044 (49.8%)**, line mismatch 4,278 (42.2%), **absent target expression 1,748 (17.3%)**, sign contradiction 1,723 (17.0%), polypharmacology with disagreeing units 1,232 (12.2%), cytotoxic signature with M > 0 484 (4.8%), ambiguous action 207 (2.0%), unknown genotype 50 control rows. **The absent-target-expression case is the cleanest internal negative control D128 has, and M barely passes it:** on the 1,748 unit rows where the annotated target is not expressed (log2(TPM+1) < 1 in every source line) M is still positive **56.9%** of the time with median 0.0006, against **59.8%** and median 0.0046 where the target is expressed. The medians differ ~8-fold in the right direction, but the distributions overlap almost completely, so **M on a single row does not distinguish target-present from target-absent.** | BRAF: gate met but hub-dominated. HER2: action 3 FAILED on an underpowered positive set. EGFR: documented gap. D127 replay: M does not reproduce the production discrimination. Nothing was tuned to change any of this |
| D128-A2.1 | 11-16 | **Amendment of the A2 engine, NOT of a frozen rule: `score.py` is corrected to apply P10 exactly as D128-A0.1 froze it. (2) The floor is applied to the genes that actually enter the numerator, so it can only be stricter than A0's footprint count; A2 reports **0 rows with a missing response**, so the two coincide here and the distinction is recorded, not exercised. (3) `a1_build_paths.py` labels a gene reached by BOTH subsets with the same expected sign `both` instead of whichever subset was walked first (it was lossy for 4 HDAC units), because the engine now floors on that label and A0 counts such a gene in each subset; `path_ambiguity.tsv` gains `n_sig_subset_tfs`, `n_tf_subset_resolved` and `n_sig_subset_resolved`. A1 is re-run; no expected sign, gene or unit changes. (4) Four fixtures pin the rule (the DDR2 shape with 173 genes behind 1 TF; a `tf` subset needing no TF count; the union rule with a `both` gene; a gene with no measured response not counting towards a floor). **Expected effect, stated BEFORE the re-run (the stop rule): exactly the 377 (cohort, pert_id, line, unit) rows of `regorafenib::CHEMBL5122` (DDR2: 173 resolved genes, 1 anchored TF) move from scored to abstained in each gene arm, which is the 55 drug rows A3 flagged as `n_units_scored_below_a0_floor`; no other row, score or percentile moves, except percentiles that shift because those rows leave the ranking. If anything else moves, the run is reported as a failure of this fix and nothing is accepted.** Regorafenib's BRAF unit `CHEMBL5145` is a DIFFERENT unit and is expected to be untouched, so the A4 BRAF hub table should change in no number; the vemurafenib/dabrafenib positives cannot change at all. **What re-runs:** A1, the full A2 (both gene arms, same flags), A3, and the A4 pieces that read A3 (`a4_d127_compat.py`, `a4_mechanism_cases.py`) plus `a4b_controls.py`, which scores through the same engine. | D128-A3.1 | 11-16 | **Diagnostic amendment of the D128-A3 landmark sensitivity row: WHY does one unit in four change the sign of M between the gene arms? Choices and readings fixed before running. DIAGNOSTIC ONLY - no parameter changes, no arm is chosen, nothing is re-ranked, P4 stays frozen. The arm decision is the user's and comes after this.** The user's instruction on being asked to settle the arm was 'diagnose before deciding'. **Grain:** the (unit, `pert_id`, source line) rows scored in BOTH arms, deduplicated over cohorts, because M does not depend on the cohort and the cohort grain would count the same number many times. **Control A - is flipping benign?** Flip rate against |M| in fixed bins (edges 0, 0.005, 0.01, 0.02, 0.03, 0.05, 0.08, 0.12, 0.2, 1), for |M_all_bing| and for max(|M_all|, |M_landmark|); the reported 'flipping effectively stops' value is the smallest bin edge above which rows (n >= 50) flip less than 5% of the time, or the statement that there is none. **Control B - imputation or small sample? (the decisive one).** For each (unit, profile) row, **200 seeded random subsets** (`SEED = 128`, without replacement) of the all-BING footprint, each of **exactly the size of that unit's landmark set**, scored through the same formula, and the share whose sign disagrees with the full all-BING M is compared with whether the landmark arm disagreed on that same row. Up to **15 profiles per unit** (seeded) so every unit is represented rather than the few with many profiles dominating. Reported per unit and in aggregate, with the gap `landmark_flip_rate - random_flip_rate` and its spread. **H1 hub:** flip rate against the largest anchored regulator's share of the resolved footprint (A1 `path_ambiguity.tsv`), in bins. **H2 inferred-gene redundancy:** the effective number of independent genes in a footprint, `n_eff = p^2 / sum_ij corr(g_i, g_j)^2` (the participation ratio of the correlation matrix's eigenvalues, computed without an eigendecomposition), over a seeded sample of **2,000 LINCS specific profiles**, reported separately for all / landmark / inferred genes, on a seeded sample of **60 units** plus fulvestrant, vemurafenib, dabrafenib, palbociclib and lapatinib. **H3 near-boundary genes:** the share of footprint genes with |z| < 1 and the share of the ABSOLUTE signed numerator they carry. **ESR1 / fulvestrant is reported against every one of the above**, since it is the case with the known numbers (0.048 / 0.040 / 0.025 on 478 genes against 0.119 / 0.088 / 0.063 on 89). **READINGS, FIXED BEFORE THE RESULT (not mutually exclusive; the evidence for each is reported either way):** (a) flips confined to |M| ~ 0 **and** random same-size subsets flipping as often as the landmark split -> the issue is an effect-size / significance floor, not the gene set; the arm choice is close to irrelevant and the real deliverable is a minimum-|M| rule. (b) flips persisting at large |M| **and** the landmark split flipping materially more than random subsets -> imputation is structurally distorting and `landmark_only` is required despite its costs. (c) flips concentrated in hub-dominated units -> the same disease as BRAF/MYC; the fix is hub handling, not the gene set. (d) large inferred-gene redundancy -> the nulls are over-confident across the WHOLE project, not only D128, and that is the headline. **Nothing is tuned to make any reading come out clean, and if the evidence is ambiguous the result row says so.** Out of scope, enforced: A5 does not run and no CTRP / PRISM / GDSC value is read. Outputs `results/lincs/d128/a3/landmark_flip_diagnostic*`, script `scripts/d128/a3_landmark_flip.py` | A 25% sign-flip rate has at least four possible causes with different consequences - an effect-size floor, L1000 imputation, hub dominance, or correlated genes inflating every denominator - and the arm decision cannot be made sensibly without knowing which | User (question and readings), Claude (implementation) |
| D128-A3.1 result (2026-11-16): **two of the four pre-stated readings are supported and two are not. The sign-flipping is an EFFECT-SIZE problem, not an imputation problem - and the reason is that a footprint of 478 or 1,894 LINCS genes carries about 45 independent measurements, not 478 or 1,894. Switching gene arms would not fix the flipping; it is the nulls and the effect-size floor that need attention, across the whole project.** `scripts/d128/a3_landmark_flip.py`, outputs `results/lincs/d128/a3/landmark_flip_diagnostic{,_units,_neff,_summary,_run.json}`, log `logs/d128_a3_landmark_flip.log`, 14 s. Population: **1,852 (unit, `pert_id`, line) rows over 226 units** scored in both arms (the cohort does not enter M, so the 10,086 cohort-level rows are these 1,852 counted many times); overall flip rate **27.9%**, the same 25% A3 reported at cohort grain. **CONTROL A - flipping is confined to small |M|, and most of the population is small |M|.** Flip rate by |M_all_bing|: **46.9%** at |M| <= 0.005, 43.2% to 0.01, 32.6% to 0.02, 24.2% to 0.03, 13.3% to 0.05, **3.3%** at 0.05-0.08, 4.4% at 0.08-0.12, **0%** above 0.12. **Flipping effectively stops at |M| >= 0.05** (the pre-stated 'below 5%' rule). On the conservative measure max(|M_all|, |M_landmark|) it does NOT stop there: 28.7% at 0.03-0.05, 17.6% at 0.05-0.08, **15.6% at 0.08-0.12**, and only 4.6% above 0.12 - so a row can carry a sizeable score in ONE arm and the opposite sign in the other. **The honest scale of the problem: only 11.9% of these rows (and 11.98% of the 4,940 scored drug rows) reach |M| >= 0.05.** The flipping is benign only for about one row in eight; the other seven live where the sign is a coin-weighted toss. **CONTROL B - the decisive one: it is NOT imputation.** Against 200 seeded random subsets of the all-BING footprint of exactly the landmark set's size, scored through the same formula: **landmark-arm flip 28.8% vs random same-size subsets 26.0%** at row level (n = 1,626 rows, 226 units). Per unit the gap (landmark - random) has **median +0.008**, IQR **-0.113 to +0.142**; 92 units are worse on the landmark split, **85 are better**, 49 are within +/-0.05. **A same-sized random slice of the footprint is about as unstable as the measured-gene slice, so the instability is a property of M at small effective gene counts, not a property of L1000 imputation - and switching to `landmark_only` would not fix it.** **H2 - and here is the mechanism, measured: the genes are massively redundant.** Effective independent genes `n_eff = p^2 / sum_ij corr^2` over 2,000 seeded profiles: for the sampled units, median footprint **905 genes -> n_eff 44.1** (4.9% of the count); the inferred part 807 genes -> **41.8**; the landmark part 140 genes -> **41.3** (29.9% of its count). Reference points: the **whole 10,112-gene BING space -> n_eff 41.0**, a random 478 genes -> 38.6, all 978 landmarks -> **56.8**. **The LINCS specific-profile space behaves like roughly 40-55 independent dimensions in total, so no footprint can carry more than that however many genes it lists.** That explains all three things we saw at once: M is diluted by adding redundant genes, the landmark arm's M is 2.5x larger on fulvestrant because it averages fewer copies of the same signal, and **the gene-label null - which permutes genes as if they were independent - is too narrow, so every percentile and z it has produced in this project is over-confident.** That is reading (d), and it is NOT specific to D128: every score built on counts of LINCS genes is affected. **H1 - hub dominance does NOT explain flipping.** Marginally, flip rate FALLS as the largest regulator's share rises (32.2% at 0.1-0.2 vs 20.5% above 0.65) - but that is a |M| confound (hub-dominated units have median |M| 0.032 against 0.011). Within |M| strata the hub cut is flat: at |M| <= 0.01, 47.5% / 39.1% / 43.9% across hub < 0.3 / 0.3-0.5 / > 0.5; at |M| > 0.03, **9.0% / 9.2% / 9.6%**. Reading (c) is not supported. (The within-|M| cut was added after the marginal table, as a confound control; the hypothesis and the measure were both fixed in advance.) **H3 - the score is built from near-boundary responses:** the median row has **78.0% of its footprint genes at |z| < 1**, and they carry **54.3% of the absolute signed numerator**. The sign of M is therefore decided largely by many tiny responses, which is exactly the regime Control A shows to be unstable. **ESR1 / fulvestrant, the case with the known numbers:** 478 genes, 89 landmark, hub 100% ESR1, median |M| 0.0398 - **its landmark flip rate is 0.000** while random 89-gene subsets flip **14.8%** of the time, so for this unit the measured genes are MORE coherent than a random slice, not less; n_eff 46.0 (all) / 38.0 (landmark) / 41.8 (inferred), i.e. the 389 inferred genes add about 8 effective measurements. The 2.5x larger landmark M and its weaker z are both consistent with dilution plus an honest (wider) null, not with imputation distorting the sign. Other named units: vemurafenib/BRAF landmark flip 11.1% vs random 22.1%; palbociclib CCND1-CDK4 50.0% vs 34.2%; lapatinib/ERBB2 9.1% vs 30.5%. **VERDICT: readings (a) and (d) are supported, (b) and (c) are not.** The arm choice is close to irrelevant to the flipping; what the evidence asks for is (1) a minimum-|M| rule before a sign is read at all, and (2) nulls that respect gene correlation. **Both are left to the user: nothing was changed, no arm was chosen, no parameter moved, nothing was re-ranked, and P4 remains frozen.** | Diagnostic complete. Open for the user: the arm decision (now shown to be a minor lever), a minimum-|M| reporting floor, and whether the gene-label null must be replaced project-wide | Claude |
| D128-A3.2 | 11-16 | **The reference-activity flag: a computed, per-(target unit, cohort) answer to 'can R be read here at all?'. Every choice fixed before running. REPORTING ONLY - this changes how R is READ, never any frozen score: M, R and every A3/A4 output keep their definitions and values, nothing is gated, filtered or re-ranked on the flag in this batch, and no Section 0 row is touched.** **Relation to the rest of the plan:** this is the computable core of the `r_class` axis specced as **D132** in `docs/SPEC_additional_axes.md`. D132's four classes are derived from *annotations* (action type, `dna_acting`/`essential`, `orient()` flags, target compartment); this row adds the one class that annotations cannot give - whether the cohort's own REFERENCE carries the target's pathway - and computes it from data for every pair, including pairs nobody has thought about. It does not implement the rest of D132, which stays open. **Why it is needed:** R asks whether the drug pushes the tumor back toward the P2 reference. Where the target pathway is active in that reference, R inverts by construction. D0.1 predicted exactly this for ESR1 in breast in advance and A4 confirmed it (fulvestrant R: LumA 0.0098, LumB 0.0039, Her2 0.0009, Basal 0.0492 - clinically backwards). That is currently a hand-written prediction for one case. **The quantity (no parallel implementation; the frozen engine is reused):** `M_disease(unit, cohort, contrast) = sum_g w_g e_g f(z_disease_g) / sum_g w_g` - the SAME footprint, the SAME expected signs `e` (A1's pre-action sign x the unit's frozen action sign, so for a `raise`-oriented unit the reading mirrors), the SAME weights (wM = 1, P7), the SAME `f = tanh(z/2)` (P9) and the SAME per-subset P10 floor (D128-A0.1 / A2.1), with the drug profile replaced by the frozen P1 disease columns (`logFC` for the sign, `z` for the magnitude; a check reports any gene where they disagree - there are none in BRCA-LumA). `scripts/d128/a3_reference_activity.py` calls `score.score_mechanism` itself; a fixture file `tests/d128/test_reference_activity.py` pins the sign convention against M and R by hand before any output is read. **Reading:** `M_disease > 0` = the tumor ALREADY looks target-inhibited against the reference, so an inhibitor pushes further the way the disease went (away from normal) and R penalises the correct drug -> **`reference_inverted`: R must not be read as evidence for or against the drug**. `M_disease < 0` = the tumor looks target-ACTIVE, the drug reverses it -> **`reference_aligned`**, R is interpretable. Not distinguishable from null -> **`reference_neutral`**. Footprint or contrast unavailable (no footprint, below the frozen floor, ambiguous action under P11, no usable contrast) -> **`reference_unavailable`** with the engine's own reason. **SIGNIFICANCE RULE, FIXED HERE BEFORE THE RUN:** the two nulls A3 showed are well powered - **sign-shuffle and gene-label, 200 seeded draws each (`SEED = 128`, the frozen generator)**. The degree-matched null is degenerate for 95% of units and is **not used**. Two-sided at 5%: `reference_inverted` requires M_disease > 0 **and both** null percentiles **>= 97.5**; `reference_aligned` requires M_disease < 0 **and both** <= 2.5; everything else is `reference_neutral`. Requiring both nulls is deliberately conservative. **Contrasts:** every contrast in `CONTRAST_ORDER` (`gtex`, `adjacent`) for which a table exists, since the whole point is that the flag depends on the reference; the contrast R actually uses (P2) is marked `is_p2_contrast` and is the one the validation reads. **COMPANION DIAGNOSTIC 1 - cohort R baseline:** the median R across ALL drugs in each cohort, with quartiles, plus `R_centred = R - cohort median` on every row, so 'this cohort is generally far from normal' (Basal is suspected) is visibly separated from 'this drug does something'. **COMPANION DIAGNOSTIC 2 - reference-tissue specificity tau:** per footprint gene, `tau = mean log2-CPM in the cohort's GTEx reference tissue - median over all 26 GTEx tissues`, from the same GTEx samples Step 4a kept (`scripts/d128/gtex_tissue_expression.R`, TMM + log2-CPM prior 2), reported as the weighted mean over the footprint. High tau means R there is largely reading the reference tissue's lineage identity. **Reported as a column; nothing is gated on it.** For `adjacent`-contrast cohorts tau still describes the GTEx tissue and is labelled as such. **VALIDATION, PRE-STATED (all four reported whichever way they fall; nothing is tuned toward them):** (1) **ESR1 x BRCA-LumA and x BRCA-LumB must NOT come out `reference_aligned`** - `reference_inverted` or `reference_neutral` both pass, because both mean 'do not read R here'; `reference_aligned` is a FAILURE, since R is known empirically to be untrustworthy there. (2) **Discrimination:** no single level may hold more than 90% of eligible (unit, cohort) pairs; the full distribution is reported. (3) **The real test - does the flag predict R?** All scored (cohort, drug, unit) rows are grouped by flag level and the R distribution in each is reported, raw and cohort-centred, with **effect size (Cliff's delta, + a tie-corrected Mann-Whitney p), not only a p-value**; the Spearman rho(M, R) within each level is reported as supporting evidence (it should be negative where inverted and positive where aligned). **If `reference_inverted` rows do not have systematically different R from `reference_aligned` rows, the flag is not measuring anything and the result row must say so.** (4) **Expected but not required:** ERBB2 x BRCA-Her2 and BRAF x TCGA-SKCM should be `aligned` or `neutral`, since HER2 amplification and BRAF V600 are tumour-acquired events rather than features of normal tissue; `inverted` there is evidence the quantity measures something other than we think. **Stated in advance as a structural caveat, so it is not presented later as a discovery:** R and M_disease share the disease direction, so where e agrees with sign(logFC) a drug with M > 0 must have R < 0. The flag is therefore partly definitional about the M-R relationship; what it is NOT definitional about is R itself, which never sees `e`. The result row must keep that distinction. **Out of scope, enforced:** A5 does not run, no CTRP / PRISM / GDSC value is read, and no ranking changes. Outputs `results/lincs/d128/a3/reference_activity{,_rows,_cohort,_summary,_validation,_named_cases,_run.json}` | D0.1 recorded the breast prediction by hand for one target; the pipeline needs the same statement computed for every (target unit, cohort) pair, or R will keep being read as evidence in cases nobody has checked | User (axis, levels, validation), Claude (quantity and implementation) |
| D128-A3.2 result (2026-11-16): **the flag computes, discriminates and predicts the M-R relationship - and it FAILS one of its four pre-registered checks. V2 and V4 pass, V3 passes only in part, and V1 FAILS on BRCA-LumB. Nothing was redefined, retuned or reworded to rescue it.** `scripts/d128/{gtex_tissue_expression.R,a3_reference_activity.py}`, outputs `results/lincs/d128/a3/reference_activity{,_rows,_cohort,_summary,_validation,_named_cases,_run.json}` and `gtex_tissue_logcpm.tsv.gz`, logs `logs/d128_{gtex_tissue,a3_reference_activity}.log`, 296 s. **Scale:** **20,046 (unit, cohort, contrast) rows** - 257 units with a resolved footprint x 78 (cohort, contrast) disease tables over 55 cohorts and both contrasts; 14,135 rows are on the P2 contrast R actually uses. 4 fixtures in `tests/d128/test_reference_activity.py` pin the sign convention by hand (61 tests pass). **V2 DISCRIMINATION - PASSES.** On the `gtex` contrast: neutral **70.0%**, aligned 14.3%, inverted 4.4%, unavailable 11.3%; among eligible pairs only, neutral 78.9% / aligned 16.2% / inverted 5.0%. On `adjacent`: neutral 71.0 / aligned 12.2 / inverted 5.5 / unavailable 11.3, eligible-only 80.0 / 13.7 / 6.2. **No level holds more than 80.0% of eligible pairs**, against the 90% bar. **V4 EXPECTED-BUT-NOT-REQUIRED - PASSES.** **ERBB2 x BRCA-Her2 -> `reference_neutral`** (M_disease -0.0051, percentiles 35.5 / 31.0, 1,795 genes; identical for lapatinib, neratinib, afatinib, dacomitinib, masoprocol - one footprint, one number). **BRAF x TCGA-SKCM -> `reference_aligned`, strongly** (M_disease **-0.1424**, both percentiles **0.0**, 1,005 genes; vemurafenib, dabrafenib, sorafenib, regorafenib, and MLN-2480's ARAF|BRAF|RAF1 unit -0.0867). Read plainly: the melanoma looks BRAF-ACTIVE against normal skin, so inhibiting BRAF moves it toward normal and R is interpretable there. Both are the predicted direction for tumour-acquired events. **V3 DOES THE FLAG PREDICT R? - PARTIAL, and the honest headline is not the obvious one.** On R's LEVEL the effect is **weak**: median R **0.0049** in `reference_inverted` (n = 543) against **0.0100** in `reference_aligned` (n = 1,691), **Cliff's delta -0.148** (tie-corrected Mann-Whitney p 2.0e-07); cohort-centred, medians -0.0016 vs +0.0027, delta **-0.140** (p 9.1e-07). That is a small effect by any convention. **The clean evidence is the within-level relationship between M and R: Spearman rho(M, R) = -0.460 in `reference_inverted`, +0.393 in `reference_aligned`, +0.116 in `reference_neutral`.** A sign reversal of the M-R relationship is exactly what the flag claims to detect, and it is there. **Two caveats the row must carry.** (i) As stated in advance in the decision row, that reversal is PARTLY DEFINITIONAL: R and M_disease share the disease direction, so where `e` agrees with `sign(logFC)` a drug with M > 0 must have R < 0. It is not definitional about R's level, which never sees `e`. (ii) A confound control added after the fact: the flag separates **M** more strongly than R (delta **-0.351**, p 6.5e-35), and on the 21 units that appear in both inverted and aligned cohorts the within-unit paired difference of median R is **+0.0072** (8 of 21 negative) - i.e. **the R-level difference does not survive within-unit pairing and reverses**, so most of the R gap in the pooled comparison is unit/cohort composition, not the reference. n = 21 is small, and this is reported rather than resolved. **V1 ESR1 x BREAST - FAILS.** BRCA-LumA passes: fulvestrant -> **`reference_neutral`** (M_disease -0.0322, percentiles 4.5 / 3.0 - just outside the 2.5 cut). **BRCA-LumB FAILS: fulvestrant -> `reference_aligned`** (M_disease **-0.0419**, sign-shuffle 1.5, gene-label 2.0), which asserts R IS interpretable there - the one thing the criterion forbade. Reported, not rescued. (Her2 and Basal, reported: both `reference_neutral`, M_disease -0.0088 and -0.0006.) **STATED LIMITATION, found by this failure and deliberately NOT fixed now (a redefinition after seeing the validation would be fitting to the result): M_disease is computed per UNIT and carries the drug's annotated action sign**, so on identical reference data fulvestrant (`lower`) gets -0.0419 -> `aligned` while estradiol and BRD-K45330754 (`raise`) get **+0.0419 -> `inverted`**. The flag is supposed to answer 'is the target's pathway active in this reference tissue', a property of the (target, cohort, contrast) triple that must not depend on which drug is asking. **As built it does.** Proposed fix for the user to decide: define M_disease on a fixed inhibition convention (action = -1 always), report it once per (target unit genes, cohort, contrast), and let each drug's own orientation be applied when the flag is read. **COVERAGE GAP, stated: the flag abstains on the SERMs - the exact drug class where the inversion was first noticed.** All 2,262 `reference_unavailable` rows carry the engine's own reason: **1,326 `ambiguous_action`** (P11 `direction_assumed`, which is every SERM row - tamoxifen 156, raloxifene / bazedoxifene / toremifene / clomifene / estramustine 78 each), 858 `below_floor_M_unit` (the frozen P10 floor), 78 `no_usable_genes`. **COMPANION 1 - cohort R baselines, and Basal is indeed high.** Median R over all drugs, per cohort: median over the 55 cohorts **0.0068**, maximum TCGA-LUSC **0.0245**, then TCGA-LUAD 0.0182, TARGET-WT 0.0181, **BRCA-Basal 0.0167 (4th of 55)**, minimum TCGA-SKCM **-0.0082**. Within breast: **Basal 0.0167, Her2 0.0079, LumB 0.0068, LumA 0.0050** - Basal's baseline is **3.3x** LumA's. Fulvestrant's A4 numbers against those baselines: Basal 0.0492 -> centred **+0.0325**; LumA 0.0098 -> +0.0048. **So the cohort baseline explains part of the backwards breast ordering but not all of it**, and `R_centred` now travels on every row so the two can be read apart. **COMPANION 2 - reference-tissue specificity tau** (weighted mean over the footprint, GTEx tissue minus the median of 26 tissues, from the 3,425 Step-4a GTEx samples, 19,797 genes): median **+0.105** over scored rows, IQR -0.057 to +0.235; by level aligned 0.149, inverted 0.101, neutral 0.093. Breast cohorts sit high (median 0.347; the ESR1 footprint itself **0.49**), BRAF/SKCM 0.277, ERBB2/Her2 0.224. Reported as a column; **nothing is gated on it**. **One more property to read the numbers by:** the disease z is far larger than a LINCS z, so the frozen f = tanh(z/2) saturates on a median **73.5%** of footprint genes - M_disease is therefore close to a signed sign-concordance, which is a consequence of reusing the frozen transform unchanged, not a defect. **TWO READINGS OF THE V1 FAILURE, both recorded, neither chosen - the user decides.** **(a)** The flag is simply wrong for LumB: it says R is interpretable where the project knows from the precursor and from A4 that breast R cannot be trusted. **(b)** *Post-hoc rationalisation of a failed criterion, labelled as such:* `aligned` asserts that R's SIGN is trustworthy, and fulvestrant's R in LumB IS weakly positive (0.0039), i.e. the correct sign; what was actually wrong in breast was the ordering ACROSS cohorts (Basal highest), which is a baseline problem the companion diagnostic addresses, not a sign problem. Reading (b) was constructed after seeing the result and must not be treated as a rescue. **SCOPE HELD: nothing was gated, filtered or re-ranked on the flag; M, R and every A3/A4 value are unchanged; no Section 0 row was touched; A5 did not run and no CTRP / PRISM / GDSC value was read.** | **V1 FAILED (LumB), V2 passed, V3 partial, V4 passed.** Open for the user: the per-unit action-sign dependence (fix proposed, not applied), whether the SERM abstention makes the flag useless for the case it was built for, and which of the two readings of the V1 failure to accept | Claude |
| D104 | 11-04 | Receptor level in the tumor vs in the lines the score came from, all pairs (well and poorly matched; user 11-04), rule fixed before running (after the descriptive sizing `scripts/d104/d104_size.py` only). Applies on top of production (D102c). **Judged:** cohort x drug whose tumor-present evidence targets (D102a E and presence rule) are all receptors with 79w binding evidence (mechanism / potent); drugs with a present non-receptor evidence target are not judged (their mechanism may run through it). **Scoring lines and weights** as 79e / 79w (direct: in-key lines, Celligner weights; borrowed: out-of-key lines, equal weights). **Per receptor:** a scoring line supports it if the line expresses it (DepMap 24Q4 TPM >= 1) or the drug induces it there (raw consensus profile, `drug_line_profiles_bing.f32`, receptor gene z >= 2; user: absence in the line is evidence against the drug unless the drug raises the receptor); support = weighted fraction of supporting lines. Status: **not tested** = no DepMap expression for the gene or none of the lines; **line absent** = support < 0.5; **tumor far lower** = support >= 0.5 but tumor median TPM < 10% of the weighted mean line TPM (abundance correction); **matched** otherwise. **Drug-level:** any receptor matched -> no action; else any not tested -> flag `receptor_not_tested` (no demotion); else (every receptor line absent or tumor far lower) -> `receptor_mismatch` with the reason, ranked below every unflagged drug and above D102c target-absent drugs (spec_mech_pct recomputed; tier 3). Induction rescues are reported (`receptor_induced`). Genes absent from the LINCS gene space cannot show induction (reported). **Evaluation (41 groups) vs production; adopt in production (user's go-ahead) if:** (1) known-use AUC median over cohorts (Hub and ChEMBL, 79l rule on spec_mech_pct) falls <= 0.01 on each; (2) breast LumA / LumB positive controls and PRAD androgen controls (D101 sets) not lower, Basal negative controls not higher. **Reported:** counts per status and class, well vs poorly matched; demoted pairs in the top 10% before; examples; class counts at tumor-far-lower cutoffs 5% and 20%; induction rescues; labeled drugs demoted.
