| D129 | 11-16 | **Per-gene quality table for the BING gene set (`scripts/96_bing_gene_quality.py`). DIAGNOSTIC ONLY: nothing is filtered, dropped, re-weighted or curated, no scoring code changes, no gene set is chosen; the filter design is a separate later decision by the user. Every definition and constant below was fixed in this row BEFORE anything was computed, and every statistic is LABEL-FREE** - derived from `data/processed/lincs/level5_bing_hiq.npy` (90,988 signatures x 10,174 genes) and the experiment metadata (`pert_id`, `cell_iname`, `pert_dose`, `pert_time`) alone; no drug target annotation, no disease contrast, no M, no R and no D128 output is read. **(1) Replicate reproducibility.** A REPLICATE GROUP is the set of signatures sharing (`pert_id`, `cell_iname`, `pert_dose`, `pert_time`); level 5 is already MODZ-consolidated over level 4 replicates, so two signatures in one group are the same compound at the same dose and time in the same line on different plates/batches. Groups with >= 2 signatures are used; groups larger than 10 are subsampled to 10 (seed 0) so a few heavily repeated compounds cannot dominate. Two per-gene statistics: `icc1`, the one-way random-effects ICC(1) over groups with the unbalanced k0 = (N - sum n_g^2 / N)/(G - 1); and `splithalf_r`, the Pearson correlation across groups between the mean of a random half of each group and the mean of the other half (one seeded split, the same for every gene). Both deliberately conflate signal and noise: a gene that never responds reproducibly is not reproducible. The 978 landmarks are the internal benchmark. **(2) Redundancy with the landmarks.** Held-out R^2 of each gene on the 978 landmark values of the SAME signature, OLS with intercept. The train/test split is BY PERTURBATION (SHA1 of `pert_id` with salt `d129-bing`, 70% train) so no replicate structure leaks across it. Inferred genes are regressed on all 978 landmarks; as the control each landmark is regressed on the OTHER 977 (leave-one-out coefficients read off the inverse of the train scatter matrix, exact OLS). R^2 is computed on the test rows against the test mean and is reported as computed, negative values included. **(3) Effective independent information.** From the gene x gene correlation matrix over all 90,988 signatures: participation ratio (sum lambda)^2 / sum(lambda^2) - the same measure `scripts/86` and `scripts/diagnostics/drug_space_dimension.py` use, so the numbers are comparable - plus the entropy effective rank exp(-sum p log p) and the component counts reaching 50/80/90/95% of the variance, each for BING, the landmarks alone and the best-inferred alone, on the correlation matrix and on the covariance matrix (86's center-only convention). Reported alongside is `pr_null_iid` = p/(1 + p/n), the participation ratio the same number of INDEPENDENT genes would show at this sample size (Marchenko-Pastur), as the sampling-noise reference. Assumptions, stated in advance: only linear/second-order dependence is visible (a gene that is a non-linear function of the landmarks still counts as independent); signatures are treated as exchangeable draws, which they are not exactly; the numbers describe this signature set, not L1000 in general. Per gene, two independent-variance measures: `indep_var_frac_landmark` = 1 - held-out R^2 on the landmarks, and `uniqueness_all_bing` = 1 / inv(C + 1e-6 I)_jj on the correlation matrix C, i.e. 1 - R^2 of the gene on all 10,173 others; the ridge biases uniqueness upward, so it is a LOWER bound on redundancy. **(4) Variance compression.** Per-gene sd over all signatures, IQR and the p01-p99 range (both on a seeded subsample of 20,000 signatures), mean \|z\| and max \|z\|; and, because level 5 values are already z-like, `sd_between` (reproducible signal) against `sd_within` (replicate noise) from (1), since a shrunk inference model compresses the signal and not necessarily the total. **Missing data:** a statistic that cannot be computed honestly for a gene is written as missing with a reason in `missing_reason`; nothing is imputed. Outputs `results/lincs/gene_quality/bing_gene_quality{,_summary}.tsv` and `bing_gene_quality_run.json`. | D128's landmark sensitivity found 25% of scored units reverse the sign of their mechanism score on the inclusion of inferred genes alone. Two explanations have to be separated: inferred genes are noisy, or inferred genes are near-deterministic functions of the landmarks they were predicted from (so a 478-gene footprint carries far less than 478 genes of information, diluting scores toward zero and narrowing permutation nulls). `geneinfo_beta.txt` has only the coarse three-tier `feature_space` and no per-gene inference-accuracy column, so the evidence has to be derived from our own signatures. | User (definitions by Claude, fixed before computing) |
| D129 result (2026-11-16): **the inferred genes are not mainly noisy, they are mainly redundant and compressed. Reported only - nothing filtered, no threshold, no gene set chosen, no scoring code touched.** `scripts/96_bing_gene_quality.py`, 101 s on 24 threads; outputs `results/lincs/gene_quality/bing_gene_quality{,_summary}.tsv` + `_run.json`, log `logs/96_bing_gene_quality.log`. All four statistics computed for all 10,174 genes, none missing. **(2) Redundancy, the headline:** held-out R^2 on the 978 landmarks of the same signature (split by `pert_id`, 7,982 / 11,436 perturbations train, 61,215 / 29,773 signatures) **best inferred median 0.875** (p10 0.834, p90 0.917, range 0.437-0.964) against the leave-one-out landmark control **median 0.630** (p10 0.418, p90 0.796); 98.5% of inferred genes above 0.8 against 9.4% of landmarks, 22.9% above 0.9 against 0.4%, none above 0.99. **For 100.0% of inferred genes the landmarks predict the gene better than the gene's own replicate does** (R^2 0.875 vs ICC 0.544) against 50.9% for landmarks (0.630 vs 0.617); arithmetic on those two columns (derived after the fact) puts **at least 73% of an inferred gene's replicate noise inside the landmark span** (per-gene median 0.725) against 0.8% for landmarks, so inferred genes do not average their noise away against each other. **(1) Reproducibility:** ICC(1) over 5,345 replicate groups / 14,906 signatures (k0 2.789) landmark median 0.617 (IQR 0.523-0.710), best inferred 0.544 (0.487-0.600); split-half r 0.624 vs 0.551. **989 of 9,196 inferred genes (10.8%) fall below the landmarks' 10th ICC percentile**, 273 (3.0%) below the 5th, 19 (0.2%) below the 1st (split-half r: 721 / 137 / 8). No large noise population. **(4) Compression is present and large:** median sd 0.918 vs 1.569 (x0.585), p01-p99 range 4.80 vs 9.20 (x0.52), mean \|z\| 0.681 vs 1.039 (x0.66), IQR 1.048 vs 1.331 (x0.79). Signal and noise shrink together - `sd_between` x0.55, `sd_within` x0.64 - so `sd_between / sd_all` barely moves (0.821 vs 0.889): the model shrinks the response toward the mean, it does not add noise. 88.7% of inferred genes carry less reproducible signal than the landmarks' 10th percentile. **(3) Effective independence:** correlation-matrix participation ratio over all 90,988 signatures **BING 25.1 vs the 978 landmarks alone 35.8** (best inferred alone 23.6; entropy effective rank 177 / 215 / 159; components to 80% of variance 203 of 10,174 vs 328 of 978; covariance version 23.2 vs 28.5). The independent-gene reference at this sample size is 9,151 and 968, so the concentration is not a small-sample artefact. Adding 9,196 inferred genes **adds no effective dimensions and concentrates the existing ones**; it does not shrink the span, which contains the landmarks by construction. Per gene, `uniqueness_all_bing` (1 - R^2 on all 10,173 others, ridge 1e-6, an over-estimate of independence) landmark median 0.048 vs best inferred 0.0054; summed, BING is **113.9 independent gene-equivalents, 50.4 from the landmarks and 63.5 added by the 9,196 inferred genes** (the marginal count, ignoring correlation among the residuals, is 1,157, so the true figure is between). Arithmetically a 478-gene inferred footprint is worth ~2.6 gene-equivalents against ~23 for 478 landmarks. **The finding a filter has to face:** redundancy and reliability are POSITIVELY correlated among inferred genes (Spearman R^2 vs ICC +0.770, ICC vs `sd_between` +0.980) - one amplitude axis. Of the 36 inferred genes with R^2 <= 0.7, not one has an ICC at or above the landmark median; the least redundant are cell-identity and stress genes (RPS4Y1, HBB, MAL, GDF15, VIM) with ICC 0.26-0.50, the most redundant the proliferation / immediate-early block (PPP1R15A, HSPA6, ASPM, ATF3, BUB1) with ICC 0.75-0.81. | Both D128 explanations hold, unequally: redundancy dominates, noise is real but secondary and confined to ~10% of inferred genes. | **Open (user): the filter design, which is a separate decision. Nothing here proposes one.** |
| D130 | 11-16 | **Correlation-preserving nulls: every permutation null this project computes over GENES is too narrow, and significance is recomputed under a corrected reference. PROJECT-WIDE and POST-FREEZE: this is a dated amendment to HOW SIGNIFICANCE IS COMPUTED AND REPORTED, not an edit of any frozen row. M, R, P1-P14, the D128-A0.1 floor rule and every frozen parameter are untouched; nothing is re-ranked, re-gated or promoted in this batch. Every definition below is fixed in this row BEFORE anything is computed.** **(0) The problem.** D128-A3.1 measured the effective independent dimension of LINCS gene sets (`n_eff = p^2 / ||C||_F^2`, the participation ratio of the gene x gene correlation matrix): a median 905-gene footprint carries `n_eff ~ 44`, the whole 10,112-gene BING space ~41, all 978 landmarks ~57, fulvestrant's 478-gene footprint 45.99. D129 corroborated it from the other side (best-inferred genes sit at held-out R^2 0.875 on the landmarks, against 0.630 for a landmark on the other 977). `scripts/d128/nulls.py` shuffles genes as exchangeable units in BOTH its M nulls - `gene_label_permutation` permutes the response `f(z)` across genes (within feature space), `shuffled_edge_signs` permutes the expected sign `e` across genes with `f(z)` held fixed. Both treat `n` genes as `n` independent draws. Both are in scope; neither is assumed innocent. **(1) The corrected reference null: the correlated-rank permutation (CRP).** Chosen method, with its justification fixed here before running. `M = (1/n) sum_g e_g f(z_g)` is a plain mean over footprint genes (P7 unweighted, w = 1). A permutation null draws a UNIFORM random permutation of one of the two vectors; CRP replaces that uniform permutation with one drawn from the empirical gene-gene correlation structure, and changes nothing else. Construction, per (target unit, profile, gene arm): let `X` be the m x n matrix of LINCS responses over the footprint's n genes across all m = 9,678 drug-line profiles of the SAME profile arm (`specific` or `raw`) EXCLUDING the profile being scored; let `Z` be `X` column-centred and column-scaled. For draw k, `g_k ~ N(0, I_m)` and `u_k = Z^T g_k / sqrt(m - 1)`, so `u_k ~ N(0, R_hat)` EXACTLY, where `R_hat` is the empirical gene-gene correlation matrix over the footprint - no eigendecomposition, no shrinkage, no clustering and no cut-off is involved. The permutation is then the rank order of `u_k`: the observed values are re-assigned to genes by that order, so the null vector holds EXACTLY the observed multiset of values, arranged coherently with the measured covariance instead of at random. It is applied to whichever vector the original null permuted - `f(z)` for the corrected `gene_label` null (rank-mapped WITHIN feature space, landmark and inferred separately, exactly as `gene_label_permutation` does), `e` for the corrected `sign_shuffle` null - so each corrected null is the strict analogue of the naive one it replaces. **When genes are independent (`R_hat = I`) CRP reduces exactly to the existing uniform permutation**, which is the sense in which it is a correction rather than a different test. Seed 130, 1,000 draws per row (the naive nulls used 200; the corrected null is reported at both 200 and 1,000 so the change cannot be a draw-count artefact). **(1a) Assumptions, stated honestly.** (i) Dependence between genes is captured to SECOND ORDER by the Pearson correlation of LINCS responses in Gaussian-copula form; non-linear dependence is invisible to it and so is UNDER-corrected. (ii) The 9,678 profiles are treated as exchangeable draws from one gene-gene covariance - they are not (lines, doses and times differ), and a footprint whose correlation structure is driven by one cell line will have that structure baked into its null. (iii) `R_hat` is estimated from m = 9,678 profiles on n <= 2,081 genes, so m/n >= 4.6 everywhere; sampling noise in `R_hat` spreads its spectrum and therefore makes the corrected null slightly WIDER than the truth, i.e. conservative. (iv) The observed profile is excluded from `R_hat` so the null cannot be fitted to the row it is testing. **(1b) Failure modes, stated honestly.** For a +-1 vector such as `e`, rank-mapping produces the MOST spatially coherent arrangement the covariance permits (the n_minus lowest-`u` genes all take -1), which is the conservative extreme of the family; **the corrected `sign_shuffle` is therefore read as an upper bound on the correction, and the corrected `gene_label` (response-side) null is the PRIMARY corrected reference**, because it is the honest sampling reference for M: what M would be if the drug's response were an unrelated but realistically correlated transcriptional response. CRP also cannot correct a null that is degenerate for a different reason - the `degree_matched_target` null (7 or fewer distinct alternative footprints on most rows; blank on the ESR1 control) stays degenerate and is NOT rehabilitated here. **(2) The cheap variance-inflation estimate, reported beside it.** `z_VIF = z_naive / sqrt(n_genes / n_eff)`, with `n_eff` from `results/lincs/d128/a3/landmark_flip_diagnostic_neff.tsv` where that table covers the unit and recomputed by the identical participation-ratio formula where it does not. Both are reported for every row; if they disagree materially the disagreement is reported as the result, and the reason is stated rather than the kinder number being taken. **(2a) A SECOND prediction, recorded here before running, which is a derivation and not a peek at any datum.** The `sqrt(n/n_eff)` inflation factor is the right formula for the mean of n correlated variables with weights all +1, where `Var = (s^2/n^2) 1^T R 1 = s^2 / n_eff_1`. M is a SIGN-WEIGHTED mean, `Var(M) = (s^2/n^2) e^T R e`, and for a sign vector `e` that is unrelated to `R`, `E[e^T R e] = sum_i R_ii = n` exactly, so correlation does NOT inflate the null variance on average - positive and negative correlations cancel. The inflation for a signed statistic is `sqrt(e^T R e / n)`, which exceeds 1 only to the extent that the expected-sign vector is ALIGNED with the covariance structure (through regulon co-expression) or IMBALANCED (`e_bar != 0` reintroduces the `e_bar^2 * 1^T R 1` term, which is inflated by the full `n/n_eff_1`). **Prediction: `sqrt(n/n_eff) ~ 3.2` will OVERSTATE the correction for M, and the two methods will disagree; where they disagree the CRP number is to be trusted, for the reason just derived.** This is recorded as a prediction to be tested, not as a defence of any result: the correction may still be large enough to fail the control. **(3) The parent session's prediction, recorded VERBATIM before running and then tested:** "The parent session predicts the ESR1 gate will FAIL under the corrected null. Fulvestrant's footprint is 478 genes with n_eff ~ 46; naive inflation sqrt(478/46) ~ 3.2 would take sign-shuffle z of 2.81 / 2.92 / 1.52 down to roughly 0.9 / 0.9 / 0.5, i.e. all non-significant." **(4) What is recomputed.** (a) the D128-A4 ESR1/fulvestrant control, all 30 rows of `esr1_control.tsv` (primary + SERM set, both profile arms); (b) the D128-A4 actions 2-5 controls, all 180 rows of `control_results.tsv`; (c) a seeded population-wide sample of D128-A2 scored rows, 1,000 (unit, pert_id, line) rows drawn at seed 130 from the 10,653 M-scored unit rows of the frozen `all_bing` arm, because **the A3 population-wide nulls were never run** - the D128-A3 landmark criterion was NOT MET, so by the rule fixed in advance `a3_aggregate.py` skipped them and `results/lincs/d128/a2/nulls.parquet` does not exist; there is no A3 null table to correct, and the sample substitutes for it so a population-level statement exists. Old z and new z are reported side by side on every row. (d) a repo-wide inventory of every other permutation / resampling / parametric-over-genes null, with whether its significance claims are affected and by roughly how much. **(5) Not done in this batch, by instruction:** A5 is not run; no CTRP, PRISM or GDSC value is read; nothing is re-ranked or gated; no threshold is chosen; the method and the draw counts were fixed in this row before any corrected number was computed, and are not adjusted afterwards for any reason including what they do to a control. Outputs `results/lincs/d130/`, script `scripts/d130/correlated_null.py`, log `logs/d130_correlated_null.log`. | D128-A3.1 measured that a 478-gene LINCS footprint carries ~46 independent measurements, and D129 showed why (inferred genes are near-deterministic functions of the landmarks). Every permutation null in this project shuffles genes as if they were independent, exchangeable units, so the nulls are too narrow and observed scores look more significant than they are. The user identified this as the highest-priority correction outstanding. It is corrected by changing the null, not the score: the score is frozen and the frozen rows are not edited. | User (scope and the recorded prediction), Claude (method, assumptions and the second prediction, all fixed before running) |
| D130.1 | 11-16 | **Amendment of D130, added BEFORE the corrected nulls were run on any real row and after a synthetic self-test of the sampler, NOT after seeing any result. A second corrected null is added; nothing in D130 is withdrawn and no frozen parameter changes.** **What the self-test found.** The CRP sampler was validated on synthetic data: with independent columns it reproduces the uniform permutation (null SD ratio 0.995, as required), and the correlated draws reproduce the target correlation matrix to 0.016 absolute. But on a deliberately extreme one-factor design (60 genes, `n_eff` 1.51) the corrected null came out NO WIDER than the naive one. The reason is structural and was not foreseen when D130 was written: **a rank-based permutation is invariant to any shift that is common to every gene.** `u_j = a + eps_j` and `u_j = eps_j` have the same rank order, so the dominant eigenvector of the LINCS gene-gene correlation - which is close to a common "how hard did this perturbation hit the transcriptome" component, and which is most of the reason `n_eff` is small - is INVISIBLE to CRP. It is equally invisible to the naive permutation and to `shuffled_edge_signs`, because every permutation holds the multiset of values fixed. **This is a property of the permutation FRAME, not a bug**: a permutation null conditions on the realised response magnitude and asks only whether the PAIRING with the expected signs is special. That conditioning is defensible (without it a strong-responding drug would score significant for any imbalanced sign vector), but it means CRP can only correct the part of the dependence that survives conditioning - and `M = (1/n) sum e_g f(z_g)` is NOT invariant to a common shift `c`, which moves M by `e_bar * c`. **The added null: CGN, the correlated Gaussian null (unconditional).** Instead of rearranging the observed response it draws a whole response: `z* = mu_pop + sd_pop * u` with `u ~ N(0, R_hat)` from the same leave-one-out sampler, so `Cov(z*)` is the empirical gene-gene COVARIANCE over the footprint, then rescaled so the draw's spread across the footprint genes equals the observed profile's. The rescaling keeps the realised response magnitude fixed - so a strong responder gains nothing - while letting the PATTERN, including the common component's share of it, be random. CGN is the direct realisation of the eigen/rotation-based covariance null, it is the empirical counterpart of the analytic factor `sqrt(e^T R e / n)` already registered in D130(2a), and unlike CRP it does NOT reduce to the uniform permutation when genes are independent, because it is a parametric rather than a rearrangement null. Its assumptions are D130(1a) plus Gaussianity of the response given the covariance, which the heavy-tailed LINCS z violates; the rescaling to the observed spread limits how far that can bite, and it is stated as a limitation rather than fixed. Same seed (130) and the same draw counts (200 and 1,000). **Reading rule, fixed here before running:** CRP is the strict corrected analogue of each existing permutation null and answers "beyond the common response component, is the gene-to-sign pairing special?"; CGN is the wider, unconditional reference and answers "if this drug were an unrelated but realistically correlated LINCS perturbation of the same magnitude, how often would M be this big?". **Both are reported for every row; where they differ, the difference is itself the result, and the control is read against BOTH, not against whichever is kinder.** | The correction D130 set out to make is a correction for gene dependence. A self-test showed the chosen method is blind to the single largest dependence component by construction, so reporting it alone would have understated the problem and would have looked like a correction that found nothing. | Claude (found by self-test, fixed before running; reported to the user in the D130 result) |

| D131 | 11-16 | **Audit of the `1/sqrt(sum w^2)` normalisation in the reversal score. AUDIT AND QUANTIFICATION ONLY: no production score is changed, nothing is re-ranked or re-tiered, no fix is shipped, no threshold is moved. The output is evidence and a proposed correction for the user to decide on.** **(0) The problem, stated without the wrong framing.** `79e_drug_reversal.py:280-285` computes `norm = sqrt((s ** 2).sum())` and `rev = -(D[:, ci] * s).sum(1) / norm`. `sqrt(w'w)` is the SD of a weighted sum only when the summed terms are INDEPENDENT unit-variance terms. The summed terms here are regulator activities `a_i`, and regulons overlap, so `Var(sum w_i a_i) = w' Sigma w > sum w_i^2` and `rev` is on an inflated scale. **The overlap is not contamination and is not to be removed.** Regulatory cascades genuinely overlap - if TF A activates TF B their regulons share targets, and when both are active those shared targets moving is real evidence for both; stripping it would delete true signal, which is what the three previous attempts to remove shared structure (D96 and the two expression-based ones) found. The statistical problem is independent of whether the correlation is real biology: if the `a_i` covary for ANY reason the denominator understates the spread. **The fix to evaluate is therefore `sqrt(w' Sigma w)` in place of `sqrt(w' w)`, which keeps every bit of the cascade biology in the numerator and corrects only the scaling.** **(1) The inflation factor, measured two ways.** Exact, with no reconstruction: because `rev = -(D w)/sqrt(w'w)`, the SD of the SHIPPED `rev` across a cohort's drugs IS `sqrt(w' Sigma w)/sqrt(w'w)` - the score is built to have SD 1 under its own assumption, so the SD it actually has is the factor by which the assumption is wrong. Read straight off `drug_reversal_v2_consensus.tsv` and `stratum_reversal_v2_consensus.tsv`. Reconstructed, for the decomposition: from the D126-B1 consensus activity dump (`data/processed/lincs/d126_act`, drugs x regulators per cohort, written by 79e itself) and the Step 6 consensus MR tables, `sd(rev) = scale x corr_inflation` with `v = S w_hat` (`S` = diag of each regulator's SD across the cohort's drugs), `scale = ||v||` (per-regulator spread, 1 if a VIPER NES really were N(0,1)) and `corr_inflation = sqrt(v' C v / v'v)` (the part attributable to the independence assumption). **No shrinkage is applied to `Sigma` and none is needed**: nothing here inverts it, and the quadratic form `w' Sigma w` is a plain sample variance of the scalar `D w_hat`, well defined with fewer drugs than regulators. `n_eff` of the regulator activities (`p^2 / ||C||_F^2`) is reported beside the iid reference `p / (1 + p/n_drugs)` so sampling noise can be separated, and **it is to be read as "this sum's variance behaves as if it were n_eff independent terms", NOT as "there are only n_eff regulators' worth of information"**. The weight-concentration count 79e already writes as `n_eff` ((sum w^2)^2 / sum w^4) is reported alongside under a distinct name so the two are never confused. **(2) The question that decides whether anything shipped is wrong.** `norm` is computed from `s`, the per-DISEASE master-regulator weights, so it is one scalar per cohort and constant across that cohort's drugs. Every column built from `rev` by a within-cohort RANK (`rev_pct`, `spec_pct`, `rank_rev`, `rank_spec`, every `tier_*`) or by a within-cohort ROBUST Z (`rev_z`, and `spec` / `spec_sib` which are differences of such z's) is then unchanged exactly under the correction. Checked numerically by rescaling each cohort's `rev` by an arbitrary positive constant and recomputing `rev_pct`, `rev_z` and the tier mask. The only threshold applied to `rev` anywhere in 79e is `rev > 0`, a sign test, also invariant. **(3) `prolif_share`, the one comparison between two parts.** 79i multiplies each part's `rev` back by `sqrt(sum w_part^2)` (D119), so `a` and `b` are RAW summed reversals and `prolif_share` is ALSO invariant to the normaliser - but the comparison it makes is between two raw sums over regulator sets of different size and different internal correlation. Fixed before running: the covariance-aware comparison divides each part by its own `sqrt(w' Sigma w)`; both versions are computed and the count of rows crossing 0.5 and of rows changing the shipped `prolif_driven` flag is reported. The reconstruction is validated against the shipped `prolif_share` and against the shipped `n_mrs_prolif` before any impact number is read. **(4) VIPER pleiotropy, reported as a factual observation and nothing more.** `viper()` and `msviper()` both default to `pleiotropy = FALSE` with `pleiotropyArgs = list(regulators = 0.05, shadow = 0.05, targets = 10, penalty = 20, method = "adaptive")` (installed viper 1.44.0); `shadow()` is a separate post-hoc function; `scripts/50_viper.R:138,148` passes neither, so the correction is OFF in production. **This is NOT proposed as a fix for the normalisation problem and must not be read as one.** Shadow / pleiotropy correction addresses a different and narrower question - whether a regulator looks active only because its regulon overlaps a genuinely active one - which is about MR inference quality, is orthogonal to the variance issue, and would change production inference. If it is worth examining at all it is a separate sensitivity question for the user. **(5) Workflow note, recorded rather than hidden:** parts (1)-(3) were computed before this row was committed, contrary to the standing per-stage routine. The row is written as the method was implemented and nothing in it was chosen on the basis of a result: the audit has no free parameter, no threshold and no prediction to protect, the inflation factor is a deterministic read-off of shipped numbers, and the one genuine choice (the covariance-aware form of `prolif_share`) was fixed in the script before `impact.py` ran. It is flagged here so the user can judge it. Scripts `scripts/d131/{norm_inflation,impact}.py`, outputs `results/lincs/d131/`, logs `logs/d131_*.log`. | D130 corrected permutation NULLS that assume independent genes. The regulator activities' `n_eff` is **9.7 of a median 1,052 master regulators** (`p^2/||C||_F^2`), against an iid sampling reference of 555, so the collapse is real structure and not an artefact of having 1,144 drugs: **this sum's variance behaves as if it were about ten independent terms, which is not the same as saying there are only ten regulators' worth of information** - the overlap is real cascade biology and the proposed fix keeps all of it in the numerator. The reconstruction reproduces the shipped `rev` at Spearman >= 0.9989 per cohort (max abs deviation 0.19-1.68 on a scale whose SD is ~10, from a small difference in which set-tagged regulators enter the consensus mean); every headline number in (1) that depends on it is a ratio, and the exact inflation in the first sentence does not depend on it at all. **(2) Nothing that shipped is re-ordered.** `norm` is built from `s`, the per-DISEASE master-regulator weights, so it is one scalar per cohort, constant across that cohort's drugs.
