| D133 | 11-17 | **Is the D131 activity-dimensionality collapse an artefact of REGULON TARGET OVERLAP? AUDIT AND QUANTIFICATION ONLY: no score is changed, nothing is re-ranked or re-tiered, no threshold is moved, no production file is touched. The output is evidence about what `n_eff` of the drug-side regulator activities means.** **(0) The claim under test.** D131 reports, per cohort, `n_eff_activity = p^2 / ||C||_F^2` (participation ratio of the drug x regulator activity correlation matrix `C` across the cohort's drugs), median ~9.7 against an iid sampling reference `p/(1+p/n_drugs)` of ~550 (e.g. PCPG-PHEO: p = 1,052 regulators, 1,149 drugs, n_eff 9.70, iid 549.2). The user's objection is correct and is the hypothesis here: this cannot mean biology has ten mechanisms, because different master regulators hit different genes. **The alternative:** VIPER activity for a regulator is a weighted enrichment over its target set, so two regulons that share most targets give near-identical NES whether or not the two TFs do anything different. With ~1,050 regulons over a ~10,113-gene LINCS universe, sharing is extensive. If that is the explanation, the finding is not "drug response is 10-dimensional" but "the instrument cannot tell these 1,052 regulators apart" - a different and more serious conclusion. **(1) Data and the provenance check, done before this row was written.** Activities: `data/processed/lincs/d126_act/<cohort>.{f32,regulators.txt,drugs.tsv}`. Confirmed by reading `79e_drug_reversal.py`: the `ACT_DIR` branch (lines 269-272) dumps `D`, and `D` is built at line 258 from `A`, which `load()` takes from `read(PROFILE, net)` with `PROFILE` defaulting to `specific` - the primary, generic-drug-response-REMOVED arm (`79c`). `A_raw` is read separately into `D_raw` and is set to `None` under `CONS`, so the raw arm is not in the dump. The dump is written AFTER `consensus(D0, regs0, m)` (line 267), so its columns are consensus regulators. Confirmed the file geometry: `regulators.txt` is written with `"\n".join(...)` and has no trailing newline, so PCPG-PHEO is 1,052 regulators (not the 1,051 `wc -l` reports) x 1,149 drugs = the `.f32` size exactly, and matches D131's `n_mrs`. **(2) How the consensus-regulon correspondence is resolved** (getting this wrong invalidates the test, so it is stated explicitly and gated). `consensus()` (79e line 204) strips the `<set>|` prefix, takes `ub = np.unique(base)` and returns `Dc/cnt`: the activity of consensus regulator X is the UNWEIGHTED MEAN of `NES_{s|X}` over exactly the sets `s` that appear in `m` for that cohort, where `m = mr[(mr.group_id == g) & mr.regulator.isin(regs)]` - i.e. the sets where X is both tested in the disease and scored on the drug side. So the object to compare against is NOT one set's regulon: it is the multiset of X's per-set regulons. Fixed here: (a) `cnt_X` and the set membership are reconstructed from the same two set-tagged MR tables 79e reads (`results/step6/aracne_project_v2_tf[_strata]/group_mr.tsv` tagged `aracne`, `results/step6/collectri_v2[_strata]/group_mr.tsv` tagged `collectri`), intersected with the same two scored-regulator lists (`data/processed/lincs/drug_viper_specific_v2_tf/<net>.regulators.txt`, `drug_viper_specific_collectri/ALL.regulators.txt`), with the cohort's network from `config/group_networks.tsv` else `net_of(parent cohort)` exactly as 79e resolves it; (b) each per-set regulon is taken from the rds 79d actually scored (`data/processed/step5/regulons_aracne_project_v2_tf.rds[[net]]`, `results/step5/regulons_core.rds[["collectri"]]`) and restricted to the LINCS gene universe with 79d's own rule, `names(tfmode) %in% rownames(P)` over `drug_line_profiles_genes.tsv`; (c) target set `T_X` = union over the present sets of those restricted targets; (d) weight vector `w_X = (1/cnt_X) * sum_s u_{s|X}` where `u_{s|X}[g] = tfmode_g * likelihood_g` over the restricted targets, L2-normalised per set - linear in the per-set vectors because the consensus is linear in the per-set NES, and each set enters with equal footing because each per-set NES is a standardised score. **GATE (must pass or the whole test is void and is reported as void):** the reconstructed `ub` must equal the dumped `regulators.txt` element-for-element and in order, for every cohort tested. **(3) The overlap measures, both reported, neither tuned.** Unweighted `J(X,Y) = |T_X and T_Y| / |T_X or T_Y|` (Jaccard of target sets) and weighted signed cosine `K(X,Y) = <w_X,w_Y> / (||w_X|| ||w_Y||)`. The weighted one is the faithful one, because VIPER enrichment is weighted and because two regulons with the same targets and opposite `tfmode` give anti-correlated NES; `J` cannot see sign and is reported as the plain-language version. **(4) Activity correlation** `C` = Pearson over the cohort's drugs, columns mean-centred, constant columns dropped, exactly as D131 computes it. **(5) The decisive diagnostic: ZERO-overlap pairs,** `|T_X and T_Y| = 0` exactly - a fact about the annotation, not a threshold, and no threshold is introduced anywhere in this row. Reported: the count, the full distribution of their `C` (mean, mean |C|, SD, 1/5/25/50/75/95/99th percentiles) against the same statistics for the top overlap decile and for all pairs. **(6) Decomposition and the headline number.** "Explained" is fixed as two quantities, both reported: (a) linear `R^2` of `C_ij` on `K_ij`, and separately on `J_ij`, over all pairs; (b) nonparametric residual `r_ij = C_ij - E[C | K]`, with `E[C|K]` the within-bin mean over **50 equal-count bins of K, fixed now** (a smoother, not a threshold; the bin count is not varied to move a reading). Residual dimensionality: `C_res` has off-diagonal `r_ij`, diagonal 1, and `n_eff_res = p^2 / ||C_res||_F^2` - the same functional D131 uses. `C_res` is not guaranteed positive semi-definite; it is reported as the same statistic on a residual structure, not as a correlation matrix. The linear-fit version is reported alongside. **(7) Secondary, costs nothing, and is the cleanest form of the same question:** `n_eff` computed on a maximal pairwise-ZERO-overlap subset of regulators (greedy, ascending overlap-degree, ties by name, seed 0) - what the dimensionality would be if the instrument had no annotation redundancy at all. **(8) The cascade check - the user's actual point.** `data/raw/omnipath/omnipath_signed_directed.tsv.gz`; regulator names and OmniPath nodes are both gene symbols, so no ID mapping is needed and none is invented. Connected = a directed edge X->Y or Y->X (reported separately: undirected path length <= 2). Comparison is MATCHED on overlap: within each of the same 50 K-bins, mean residual `r` and mean `|r|` for connected vs unconnected pairs, pooled with bin-size weights, with a permutation p-value from 10,000 within-bin label shuffles, seed 0. **(9) Cohorts, and why.** Five, not 55: PHEO-kinase-signaling (lowest measured D131 inflation, 4.097), PCPG-PHEO (4.127 - the cohort whose exact numbers the question quotes: 1,052 regulators, 1,149 drugs, n_eff 9.70), TCGA-LIHC (10.212, the exact median), UCEC-EEC (10.258, the other near-median), TGCT-SEM-C1 (highest, 15.457). This spans the full measured inflation range end to end, covers five different ARACNe networks (PCPG_PHEO, LIHC, UCEC_EEC, TGCT) and both the group and stratum levels, which is what the overlap structure can vary with; ~553k pairs per cohort is cheap and 55 cohorts would add networks, not information. **(10) Readings, fixed in advance.** (i) Zero-overlap pairs near-uncorrelated AND `n_eff_res` rises into the hundreds (>= 100, i.e. within an order of magnitude of the ~550 iid reference) -> the collapse is annotation redundancy, the instrument cannot resolve these regulators, and the D131 `n_eff` gloss must be restated. (ii) Zero-overlap pairs substantially correlated AND `n_eff_res` stays low (< 30) -> real shared structure in drug response; ~10 dominant axes is a genuine statement about these drugs. (iii) Both -> report the decomposition with proportions and do NOT round it to either story. **(11) A third mechanism, named now so it is not mistaken for either.** Neither overlap nor cascade: a drug signature with strong gene-gene correlation makes even disjoint regulons co-vary, because every NES is an enrichment on the same ranked profile. This test cannot separate that from "real shared drug-response biology" - in part it IS that biology - so a zero-overlap correlation will be reported as "structure the regulon annotation does not explain", and the cascade check is what distinguishes the part of it that is wired signalling. **(12) Predictions, recorded before running.** Parent session: both effects present, overlap explaining a substantial but incomplete share; zero-overlap pairs clearly correlated but much less than high-overlap pairs. This session: zero-overlap pairs will have a clearly non-zero mean `|C|`; overlap will explain only a modest share of total `C` variance (linear `R^2` well under 0.3) because the great majority of pairs have near-zero overlap while `C` is dominated by a few global components; `n_eff_res` will rise but land in the tens, not the hundreds - i.e. reading (iii), weighted toward shared structure rather than annotation redundancy. **(13) Phrasing discipline, carried from D131:** `n_eff` means "this sum's variance behaves as if it were `n_eff` independent terms", never "there are only `n_eff` regulators' worth of information". **Constraints:** no A5, no CTRP/PRISM/GDSC, no overlap threshold tuned to make a reading come out, nothing written outside `scripts/d133/`, `results/lincs/d133/`, `logs/d133_*.log` and the three docs. Scripts `scripts/d133/`, outputs `results/lincs/d133/`. |
| D133.1 | 11-17 | **Amendment of D133, from the user, recorded BEFORE anything was computed (at the time of writing only the provenance checks of D133(1)-(2) had been run: no overlap, no correlation, no `n_eff`). It addresses a circularity in D133 as first written, and it is the right objection.** **(0) The confound.** ARACNe regulons are LEARNED FROM CO-EXPRESSION. If two TFs are co-active across tumours, ARACNe will give them overlapping target sets - so "target overlap" is partly a CONSEQUENCE of the correlated biology rather than an independent annotation of it. D133 as briefed therefore cannot separate "activities correlate because the regulons share targets" from "the regulons share targets because the underlying biology is correlated, and that same correlation then reappears in the activities". That is circular, and it undercuts the clean overlap-versus-biology split the whole test rests on. **(1) The fix: run the same test on a second, NON-LEARNED regulon set.** CollecTRI is literature-curated, not inferred from expression, so its overlap structure comes from curation and not from the correlation being measured. Path verified on disk: `data/processed/lincs/precursor_regulons/collectri.tsv.gz` (columns `regulator, target, tfmode, likelihood`; targets are ENSG, as in the rds). It is used as a CROSS-CHECK only; the authoritative definition stays the one `79d` scored, `results/step5/regulons_core.rds[["collectri"]]` restricted to the LINCS gene universe, and any disagreement between the two is reported rather than silently resolved. **(2) What has to be built, and the gate that makes it trustworthy.** The `d126_act` dump is CONSENSUS activity, so a set-alone arm does not exist on disk at cohort level: the per-cohort drug aggregation (`79e` lines 243-266: direct = Celligner-weighted mean over the drug's lines inside the cohort's strict tissue key, borrowed = unweighted mean over the drug's other cancer lines) must be reproduced from `data/processed/lincs/drug_viper_specific_v2_tf/<net>.f32` and `drug_viper_specific_collectri/ALL.f32` using the same `drug_line_profiles_index.tsv`, the same `stratum_drugs_v2.tsv` / `disease_drugs.tsv`, the same line weights and the same tissue keys. **GATE, stronger than the D133(2) gate and replacing it as the primary one:** the reconstruction's CONSENSUS arm must reproduce the dumped `<cohort>.f32` to float32 precision, and its regulator list must equal `<cohort>.regulators.txt` element-for-element. If it does not, the arm is void and is reported as void; nothing is patched to make it match. **(3) Fairness of the comparison, stated rather than smoothed over.** CollecTRI and ARACNe differ in size, coverage and weighting (on LINCS genes: 472 CollecTRI regulators scored against ~1,000-1,200 per ARACNe network), so their `n_eff` values are NOT comparable as absolute numbers. Fixed now: (a) each arm is compared against its OWN iid sampling reference `p/(1+p/n_drugs)`, and the reported comparison is the RATIO `n_eff / n_eff_iid`, not the raw figure; (b) the primary comparison is additionally run on the COMMON regulator subset - the consensus regulators of that cohort for which both sets are present (`cnt_X = 2`) - so that `p`, the drugs and the regulator identities are identical across arms, and only the regulon definition differs; (c) the all-regulators version of each arm is reported alongside. **(4) Readings for the second set, fixed in advance.** (i) **Both regulon sets collapse similarly** (ratio `n_eff/n_eff_iid` within a factor of ~1.5 of each other on the common subset) -> the collapse is not an artefact of ARACNe's inference procedure; it reflects either real coordinated biology or a property of the gene space itself, and the conclusion is robust to how the regulons were built. (ii) **ARACNe collapses but CollecTRI does not, or much less** -> the collapse is substantially circular: regulons learned from co-expression inherit that co-expression and re-express it as correlated activities, and the D131 `n_eff` figure is then partly an artefact of the regulon-inference step and must be restated with that attribution. (iii) **CollecTRI collapses more than ARACNe** -> report it; that would point at curation bias (well-studied TFs sharing annotated targets), not at either of the above. **(5) Reporting discipline for the cascade check (D133(8)), tightened.** An OmniPath edge establishes a DOCUMENTED REGULATORY RELATIONSHIP, not causality in these data. Two regulators can correlate because one drives the other, because both sit downstream of a shared driver, or because both track a cellular state. The matched-overlap comparison supports exactly one claim - "correlated beyond what shared targets predict" - and the result will be written that way; it will not be written as if it demonstrated that a cascade is operating. **(6) Everything else in D133 stands:** audit and quantification only, nothing re-ranked, no threshold tuned, same five cohorts, same readings, same phrasing discipline on `n_eff`. **(7) Prediction for the added arm, recorded before running (this session):** CollecTRI will collapse too, but less than ARACNe on the common subset - reading (ii) in part - because co-expression-learned regulons must inherit some of the correlation they are being used to measure, while a curated set cannot; the residual CollecTRI collapse is then the part that is either real coordinated drug response or a property of the L1000 gene space. |
| D133 / D133.1 result (2026-11-17): **the collapse is NOT an artefact of regulon target overlap. Reading (ii) of D133(10) holds, and the parent session's prediction and this session's prediction are both WRONG in the same direction: overlap explains much less than either of us predicted.** Scripts `scripts/d133/{export_regulons.R,overlap_dimensionality.py}`, outputs `results/lincs/d133/`, log `logs/d133_overlap.log`, 22 s. Five cohorts spanning the full D131 inflation range: PHEO-kinase-signaling (4.10), PCPG-PHEO (4.13), TCGA-LIHC (10.21, the exact median), UCEC-EEC (10.26), TGCT-SEM-C1 (15.46); networks PCPG_PHEO, LIHC, UCEC_EEC, TGCT. **(0) The gate passed exactly, so the consensus correspondence is confirmed rather than assumed.** The reproduction of `79e`'s per-cohort drug aggregation plus `consensus()` matches the shipped `d126_act` dump with **max |recon - dump| = 0.000e+00** and element-identical regulator lists in all five cohorts; the resulting `n_eff` reproduces D131's `n_eff_activity` to four decimals (PCPG-PHEO 9.6966, TGCT-SEM-C1 7.9688, TCGA-LIHC 10.3572). D131's number is independently confirmed; what it means is what changes. **(1) Overlap IS strongly predictive where it exists - and it almost never exists.** Mean activity correlation by Jaccard band (PCPG-PHEO, consensus): zero shared targets 0.009, (0, 0.01] 0.024, (0.01, 0.05] 0.215, (0.05, 0.10] 0.486, (0.10, 0.20] 0.657, (0.20, 0.50] 0.777. So two regulons that really do share targets really do give near-identical NES, exactly as the hypothesis says. But **54-63% of all regulator pairs share ZERO targets**, and only ~0.3% reach Jaccard > 0.05. **(2) The decisive diagnostic: zero-overlap pairs are NOT near-uncorrelated.** Pairs with literally no shared target have mean |correlation| **0.214-0.263**, against an all-pairs mean |correlation| of 0.247-0.288 - i.e. essentially the same. Mean signed correlation ~0 (-0.017 to +0.009), SD 0.26-0.32, 5th/95th percentiles about -0.45/+0.51, 1st/99th about -0.61/+0.67: roughly a tenth of disjoint pairs correlate beyond |0.45|. **(3) Decomposition.** Linear `R^2` of correlation on the weighted signed cosine **0.160-0.259** (on Jaccard 0.094-0.222); the 50-equal-count-bin nonparametric version **0.193-0.355**. Overlap predicts about a fifth to a third of the pairwise structure; **70-80% of it is not overlap.** **(4) THE HEADLINE NUMBER: removing the overlap-predicted component barely moves the dimensionality.** Consensus `n_eff` -> residual `n_eff`: PHEO-kinase 9.75 -> **12.72**, PCPG-PHEO 9.70 -> **12.68**, TCGA-LIHC 10.36 -> **14.48**, UCEC-EEC 9.83 -> **12.47**, TGCT-SEM-C1 7.97 -> **10.04** (linear-fit residual 9.65-13.84, the same picture). A rise of 26-40%, against an iid sampling reference of 531-582. The pre-registered bands were "into the hundreds (>= 100)" for annotation redundancy and "< 30" for real shared structure: **every cohort lands at 10-14.5, far inside the second band.** **(5) The cleanest form of the same test.** A maximal set of regulators that pairwise share *literally zero* targets (greedy, seed 0) is 40-53 regulators per cohort; their activity `n_eff` is **10.8-14.6** against an iid reference of **38.7-50.7**, mean |correlation| 0.18-0.22. With annotation redundancy removed by construction, dimensionality is still about a quarter of its sampling reference. **(6) D133.1, the circularity arm: the non-learned regulon set collapses too, and the comparison refutes the overlap hypothesis a second way.** On the common regulator subset (identical `p`, identical drugs, only the regulon definition differs; `p` = 258-296), `n_eff / n_eff_iid` is **ARACNe 0.030-0.037** vs **CollecTRI 0.073-0.098**: CollecTRI collapses **2.1-2.7x less**, consistently, in all five cohorts. So part of the ARACNe collapse IS the circularity the user named - regulons learned from co-expression inherit that co-expression. But CollecTRI's own ratio of ~0.08 is still a **~12-fold** collapse below its own sampling reference (17.8 effective terms out of 275 regulators, reference 222). Inference-circularity accounts for a factor of about 2.3 of a 30-60-fold collapse; **it does not account for the collapse.** By the D133.1(4) bands this is neither (i) (factor 2.1-2.7, outside the ~1.5 band) nor cleanly (ii), and it is reported with proportions rather than rounded to either. **(7) And the cross-check that kills the overlap hypothesis on its own terms:** CollecTRI regulons overlap **more** - only 23-26% of CollecTRI pairs are disjoint, against 65-75% of ARACNe pairs - yet their activities correlate **less** (mean |r| 0.133-0.172 vs 0.189-0.281). If target overlap drove activity correlation this would have to run the other way. **(8) Cascade check (D133.1(5) wording observed throughout).** OmniPath direct-edge pairs among a cohort's regulators: 471-516 pairs. Raw mean correlation **+0.120 to +0.129**, against an all-pairs mean of +0.041 to +0.067 - connected pairs ARE more correlated than pairs in general. **Matched on overlap bin, they are LESS correlated than unconnected pairs: residual difference -0.071 to -0.094, permutation p < 0.0001** (10,000 within-bin shuffles, seed 0); undirected path <= 2 (3,177-3,518 pairs) the same, -0.058 to -0.081, p < 0.0001. In the set-alone arms the matched difference is ~0 and not significant for ARACNe (+0.004 to +0.033, p 0.04-0.82) and small-positive for CollecTRI (+0.009 to +0.022, p 0.04-0.36), i.e. not consistent either way. **What this supports, stated exactly:** a documented regulatory relationship buys no extra activity correlation beyond what shared targets already predict. An OmniPath edge is a documented relationship, not causality in these data - two regulators can correlate because one drives the other, because both sit downstream of a shared driver, or because both track a cellular state - and nothing here shows a cascade is operating. The connected pairs sit in high-overlap bins where the expected correlation is ~+0.20 and reach only ~+0.13, which is what the negative residual is. **Reported, NOT pre-registered (exploratory):** split by edge sign, activating edges carry the raw signal (+0.146 to +0.161 raw, 367-401 pairs) and inhibitory edges almost none (+0.009 to +0.044, 101-118 pairs), while the residual deficit is present for both (-0.078 to -0.097 and -0.046 to -0.087). **(9) Which reading holds: (ii).** Zero-overlap pairs substantially correlated AND residual `n_eff` stays low. The structure in drug response is real shared structure, not annotation redundancy; ~10 dominant axes is a genuine statement about these drugs, and it survives (a) removing the overlap-predicted component, (b) restricting to literally disjoint regulons, and (c) swapping the learned regulon set for a curated one. Overlap is not nothing and is not rounded away: it explains a fifth to a third of pairwise correlation, and where regulons genuinely overlap (Jaccard > 0.05) correlation runs 0.49-0.78. **(10) Does the D131 `n_eff` statement need restating? The number does not; the attribution does.** D131's `n_eff_activity` is confirmed exactly. What D133 adds is that the collapse cannot be explained away as "our regulons are the same regulon": it is not annotation redundancy, and only a ~2.3x slice of it is the regulon-inference circularity. **(11) The one thing this still cannot separate, stated plainly (D133(11)).** Neither overlap nor cascade: every regulator's NES is an enrichment on the SAME ranked drug profile, so a drug signature with a strong dominant component makes even disjoint regulons co-vary. This test cannot distinguish that from real coordinated drug-response biology - in part it IS that biology. So the user's original worry survives in a different and weaker form: not "the instrument cannot tell these regulons apart" (it can - disjoint regulons stay correlated), but "every regulator reads the same dominant axis of the drug profile". Separating the two needs a different test (e.g. dimensionality of the gene-level drug signatures themselves, against which the regulator-level `n_eff` could be compared), which is not run here. **(12) Phrasing, unchanged:** `n_eff` means "this sum's variance behaves as if it were `n_eff` independent terms", never "there are only `n_eff` regulators' worth of information". **(13) Predictions, scored.** Parent session predicted both effects present with overlap explaining a substantial but incomplete share - half right: zero-overlap pairs are clearly correlated (right) but overlap's share is small, not substantial, and the residual `n_eff` hardly moves. This session predicted linear `R^2` well under 0.3 (right: 0.16-0.26) and residual `n_eff` in the tens rather than the hundreds (right: 10-14.5), but predicted CollecTRI would collapse "much less" than ARACNe, which overstates a 2.1-2.7x difference inside a 30-60-fold collapse. **(14) Cross-check reported, not resolved (D133.1(1)):** the CollecTRI rds that `79d` scores and `data/processed/lincs/precursor_regulons/collectri.tsv.gz` agree on all 31,440 rds regulator-target pairs; the precursor file holds 10,992 further pairs (42,432 total), i.e. it is a superset. The authoritative rds definition was used throughout. **Nothing was changed, re-ranked, re-tiered or gated.** |
| D134-S0 | 11-17 | **Feasibility audit for `docs/SPEC_target_recoverability.md` (D134) stage S0. FEASIBILITY ONLY: NO retrieval is computed, no similarity between two different compounds' profiles, no ranking, no AUROC, no null. No production score is changed, nothing is re-ranked or re-tiered, no threshold is chosen, no outcome label (CTRP / PRISM / GDSC) is read. The output is counts, and it is written so that it cannot be fitted to a result, because no result exists yet.** **(0) What S0 is for.** Spec Section 2 gives ANNOTATION-level counts (193 target units, 82 with >= 2 compounds, 30 with >= 5, 111 singletons). Those are necessary but not sufficient: two compounds sharing a target are only comparable if they were profiled in the SAME cell line, three close structural analogs are not three independent compounds, and a compound that produced no transcriptional response cannot be retrieved at all. S0 converts the annotation counts into DATA-level counts and reports the attrition at each step. If the testable set does not survive, S2 is not run. **(1) Scope and keys, fixed here.** Target unit = the `tid` of `results/lincs/target_role/d121/units.tsv`, rows with a non-empty `tid` only (23 of 497 rows have none and are dropped). That reproduces Section 2 exactly: 474 rows, 307 compounds, 193 units, 111 singletons, 82 / 59 / 30 units with >= 2 / 3 / 5 compounds. Compound identity = `cmap_name` (spec Section 3 excludes other `pert_id`s of the same `cmap_name` as self-retrieval, so a compound is one `cmap_name`). **(2) Profile universe.** The production drug x line consensus set `data/processed/lincs/drug_line_profiles_index.tsv` with `drug_line_profiles_bing.f32` (raw) and `_specific.f32` (generic drug response projected out), 9,678 (`pert_id`, `ModelID`) rows x 10,112 genes, built by `scripts/79c_drug_profiles.py` as the mean over a compound's ACTIVE signatures (`is_hiq == 1` and per-signature `tas >= 0.4`) across dose and time - the spec Section 3 primary aggregation. Matrices are memory-mapped and read in chunks, never loaded whole. A compound is PRESENT in line L if any of its `pert_id`s has a row for L; per-compound-per-line quantities are the MAX over its `pert_id`s there. Recorded in advance: this universe ALREADY carries a strength floor (`tas >= 0.4` per signature), so S0 also reports the pre-filter (`is_hiq`-only) cell count to show what that floor has already cost. **(3) Candidate pool per line, for the chance baseline.** Primary, as spec Section 3 writes it: every distinct `cmap_name` with >= 1 profile in that line (the whole profiled set, 2,848 compounds over 73 lines). Sensitivity: the pool restricted to the 307 target-annotated compounds. Both are reported; neither is chosen here. **(4) Shared-line feasibility (the real constraint).** For target unit T, an ordered-free PAIR {A,B} of distinct compounds of T is SHARED-LINE TESTABLE iff there exists a line L in which both A and B have >= 1 profile. Per T: total annotation pairs C(n,2), shared-line pairs, distinct compounds appearing in >= 1 shared-line pair, distinct lines carrying >= 1 such pair. REVISED TESTABLE SET = units with >= 2 compounds surviving that requirement. **(5) Analog collapse (independent-evidence check).** SKELETON = first 14 characters of `inchi_key` in `results/lincs/drug_annotations.tsv` (the InChIKey skeleton hash; already present, no new dependency), taken per `pert_id` and reduced to the compound; a compound whose `pert_id`s disagree takes the skeleton of its most frequent, and the disagreement is counted and reported. A compound with no `inchi_key` (2 of 402 profiled target-annotated `pert_id`s) is given its OWN singleton skeleton and FLAGGED as un-collapsible - reported missing, never imputed into another skeleton. EFFECTIVE COMPOUND COUNT of T = distinct skeletons among its compounds; reported alongside the raw count, and again after the shared-line requirement. **(6) Signature strength, per (compound, line) profile.** Four measures, all per-profile, none comparing two compounds: `nz2` = number of genes with |z| >= 2 (the spec Section 5.2 suggestion), `nz4` = |z| >= 4, `mean_abs` = mean |z|, each on the raw arm with the specific arm reported alongside; and `tas_max` / `tas_mean` over the active signatures the profile was averaged from (TAS IS available, in `level5_bing_hiq_sigs.tsv.gz` from `siginfo_beta.txt`, so it is used as well as - not instead of - the gene count). Distribution reported overall and per target unit. **NO FLOOR IS CHOSEN HERE.** A grid of CANDIDATE floors is costed: `nz2` >= 0 / 50 / 100 / 200 / 400 / 800 and `tas_max` >= 0.4 / 0.5 / 0.6 / 0.7, each reporting how many profiles, compounds, shared-line pairs and testable units it removes. The floor is fixed at S1, after S0 is read. **(7) Chance baseline.** Per (line L, unit T) with >= 2 of T's compounds profiled in L, the spec Section 4 analytic per-query chance: (compounds in L carrying T, excluding the query itself) / (candidate compounds in L - 1), under both pools of (3). Distribution reported; any (L, T) at chance >= 0.05 is FLAGGED as a cell where retrieval would be close to uninformative. The 0.05 is a reporting flag only - it gates nothing and is not a threshold decision. **(8) Bottom line.** After shared-line, analog collapse and the candidate strength floors, the number of units that remain testable, with how many effective compounds, in how many lines, is reported as a single attrition table. Illustrative floor points are labelled illustrative; the verdict on whether S2 is worth running is stated plainly, and a negative verdict is an acceptable outcome of an audit. **(9) Honesty rule.** Any count that cannot be computed honestly is reported MISSING with its reason, never imputed. Any Section 2 number S0 cannot reproduce is reported as a revision with the reason. **(10) Outputs** `results/lincs/d134/`: `feasibility.tsv` (per unit), `strength_profiles.tsv` (per compound x line), `chance_by_line_target.tsv`, `attrition.tsv`, `d134_s0_run.json`. Script `scripts/d134/s0_feasibility.py`, threads set explicitly to 8. | Pre-registered before running (this row is committed and pushed before the script is executed) |
| D134-S0 result (2026-11-17): **the spec's premise survives, narrowly and with one of its four confound controls measured to be far weaker than it is written. 82 annotated testable targets become 71 after the shared-line requirement, 71 after analog collapse, and 67 / 49 / 35 under progressively stricter strength floors. S2 is worth running; control 5.1 as specified is not the control the spec thinks it is.** Script `scripts/d134/s0_feasibility.py` (threads 8, 2.8 s), outputs `results/lincs/d134/` (`feasibility.tsv`, `strength_profiles.tsv`, `chance_by_line_target.tsv`, `attrition.tsv`, `d134_s0_run.json`). No retrieval, no between-compound similarity, no ranking, no AUROC, no null, no outcome label, nothing re-ranked, no threshold chosen. **(1) Section 2 reproduces exactly** on the key fixed in D134-S0(1): 474 of 497 unit rows have a non-empty `tid`, giving 307 compounds, 193 units, 82 / 59 / 30 units with >= 2 / 3 / 5 compounds and 111 singletons. **Two revisions.** (a) Distinct gene targets are **377, not 387**. (b) The coverage line ("median 4 active cell lines (IQR 1-8); 206 testable compounds in >= 3 lines, 163 in >= 5") is **NOT REPRODUCED** under any of three readings: distinct lines per `cmap_name` gives median 3 (IQR 1-7), 131 of 247 in >= 3 lines and 91 in >= 5; `n_active_lines` summed over a compound's `pert_id`s gives median 3 (IQR 1-7.5), 137 and 96; per `pert_id` it gives median 2 (IQR 1-5), 152 and 100 of 333. Reported unreproduced rather than guessed; the figure retrieval can use is the first. **Coverage is roughly 35% thinner than Section 2 states.** **(2) Shared-line feasibility is the real constraint, and it costs 11 targets.** All 307 compounds have at least one consensus profile (1,445 compound x line profiles over 73 lines, of which 37 carry a testable pair). Requiring that a pair share a cell line cuts annotation pairs **1,061 -> 651 (-39%)**, compounds in testable units 247 -> **229**, and **testable units 82 -> 71**. The 11 lost are all small and nearly all non-oncology: CACNA1C/D/F/S (4 compounds, 7 lines, never two in the same one), NR3C2, ACE, HTR4, SLC12A3, HTR1D, the GRIN complex, ADRB1, HTR1A, the RAR/RXR complex, F10. The pair loss is much larger than the unit loss: the surviving 71 units keep a median of 4 effective compounds (IQR 2.5-5) in a median of 6 lines, but their internal pair counts are thinned by 39%. **(3) Analog collapse costs essentially nothing, and that is a finding about the control, not about the data.** 307 compounds carry 302 distinct InChIKey skeletons. Only four groups collapse: {alitretinoin, isotretinoin, tretinoin}, {betamethasone, dexamethasone}, {fluocinolone-acetonide, fluocinonide}, {levonorgestrel, norgestrel}. Shared-line pairs fall 651 -> 647 (4 pairs) and **no unit drops below 2 effective compounds: 71 -> 71**. **The reason is a measured limit of spec control 5.1 as written.** The first 14 characters of an InChIKey hash the molecular SKELETON, so two compounds share it only when they have the same connectivity and differ in salt, stereochemistry, isotope or charge. Structural analogs in the ordinary sense - two statins, two tinibs, two taxanes - have DIFFERENT first blocks and are not collapsed. The compound set is already keyed by `cmap_name`, so near-duplicates are largely gone before the rule applies. **The effective-compound count is therefore a near-duplicate check and a LOWER BOUND on analog redundancy, and Section 5.1's pre-stated reading ("if analog-excluded AUROC drops substantially, retrieval is measuring chemistry") cannot be run as specified: excluding 4 pairs of 651 cannot move an AUROC.** A real analog control needs a structural fingerprint (Morgan / Tanimoto or equivalent) and a similarity cut-off - a new dependency and a threshold, both of which belong to S1. Two compounds (`BRD-K08177763`, norethynodrel) have no `inchi_key`; each was given its own singleton skeleton and flagged, never imputed. Fifteen compounds have `pert_id`s whose keys disagree and took the most frequent. **(4) Signature strength: the universe already carries a floor, and the extra floors are expensive.** TAS is available (`siginfo_beta.txt` via `level5_bing_hiq_sigs.tsv.gz`) and was used alongside the gene count. Recorded for the record: `79c` already keeps only signatures with `tas >= 0.4`, which took 35,793 high-quality (`pert_id`, line) cells down to 9,678 - **73% of the cells were already removed before S0 saw the data**, so every count here is conditional on that. Over the 1,445 target-annotated profiles: `nz2` (genes at |z| >= 2 on the raw arm) median 445 (IQR 238-984, p05 100, max 5,810); `nz4` median 40; mean |z| median 0.79; `tas_max` median 0.57 (IQR 0.47-0.69, max 0.89). On the `specific` arm `nz2` median falls to 259, so removing the generic drug response removes about 42% of the |z| >= 2 genes. **Candidate floors, costed not chosen:** `nz2` >= 50 / 100 / 200 / 400 / 800 leaves **70 / 70 / 67 / 49 / 35** testable units (and 53 / 52 / 48 / 35 / 15 with >= 3 effective compounds); `tas_max` >= 0.5 / 0.6 / 0.7 leaves **60 / 48 / 35** units (44 / 30 / 14 with >= 3). Floors up to `nz2` >= 200 are nearly free; beyond that the testable set halves. **A caution the floor will collide with:** the best-powered targets are the weakest responders. NR3C1 (22 effective compounds) has median `nz2` 246 and median `tas_max` 0.46, HTR2A (13) 295 and 0.50, DRD2 (10) 372 and 0.49 - all below the overall medians and barely above the inherited 0.4 TAS floor, so a strength floor strict enough to matter will preferentially delete the targets the map most depends on. This is stated now, before S1 picks the floor. **(5) Chance baselines are low, with 16 cells where retrieval is meaningless.** 563 (line, unit) cells carry >= 2 of a unit's compounds (37 lines, 71 units; 267 cells have >= 3 compounds, 57 have >= 5). Against the full profiled pool of the line (median 17 candidate compounds, p95 782, max 1,132): chance median 0.0034, IQR 0.0017-0.0096, p95 0.031, max 1.0; **16 cells at >= 0.05**, driven by lines with almost nothing profiled in them (EGFR in ACH-000940 / ACH-000929 / ACH-000962 sits in a pool of 2-3 compounds, chance 1.0 - retrieval there is arithmetically guaranteed and carries no information). Against the target-annotated pool only (median 4 candidates): chance median 0.022, p95 0.125, 115 cells at >= 0.05. Cells are concentrated: the top line carries 59 of 563. **The 0.05 flag is a reporting flag; it gates nothing and no cell was dropped.** **(6) THE BOTTOM LINE.** With no strength floor: **71 testable units, 229 compounds, 37 lines; median 4 effective compounds and 6 lines per unit; 53 units at >= 3 effective compounds, 29 at >= 5.** At an illustrative `nz2` >= 200: 67 units, 202 compounds, 33 lines, 48 at >= 3. At an illustrative `tas_max` >= 0.6: 48 units, 103 compounds, 24 lines, 30 at >= 3. **Verdict: S2 is worth running.** The attrition is real but not fatal - the 82 was never going to be 82, and 71 units with a median of 4 independent compounds over 6 lines is a domain map worth having. The spec's own framing survives too: this still says nothing about the 111 singletons, which remain untested. **(7) What S1 must now fix, which S0 has made unavoidable.** (a) The strength floor, with the NR3C1 / HTR2A / DRD2 caution in (4) on the table. (b) A replacement for control 5.1: either a fingerprint-based analog definition or an explicit statement that the analog control cannot be run and Section 5.1's pre-stated reading is withdrawn. (c) Whether the 16 high-chance cells are excluded or merely reported. (d) Whether the candidate pool is the full profiled set or the annotated subset - the two differ by a factor of about 6 in chance and the spec does not say. | Closed. S1 (freeze) is next and inherits (7)(a)-(d). Section 2 of the spec needs the two corrections in (1); Section 5.1 needs the measured limit in (3) |
| D135-S0 | 11-17 | **Feasibility and QC audit for `docs/SPEC_drug_genetic_concordance.md` (D135) stage S0. FEASIBILITY AND QC ONLY: NO CONCORDANCE IS COMPUTED. No drug profile is correlated with any perturbation profile, no positive control is run, no AUROC, no null, no statistic that compares a drug with a knockout. No production score is changed, nothing is re-ranked or re-tiered, no outcome label (CTRP / PRISM / GDSC) is read, no threshold is chosen. Every quantity is an annotation count, a presence count, or a PER-PROFILE statistic; the output is written so that it cannot be fitted to a result, because no result exists yet.** **(1) The testable object.** A genetic perturbation acts on one GENE, so the cell of this test is a (drug `cmap_name`, target GENE symbol, line `ModelID`) triple, not the (compound, `tid` unit) pair of D134. Target scope = the genes of `results/lincs/target_role/d121/units.tsv` rows with a non-empty `tid` (474 of 497 rows; 307 compounds, 193 units, **377** distinct genes, the figure D134-S0 corrected from 387). The predicted direction is carried from `orient` (lower / raise) and `actions` at the (compound, unit) level and inherited by each gene of the unit; it is RECORDED here and never used, because nothing is compared at S0. **(2) Drug universe, unchanged from D134-S0(2):** `data/processed/lincs/drug_line_profiles_index.tsv` with `drug_line_profiles_bing.f32` / `_specific.f32` - 9,678 (`pert_id`, line) rows, 2,848 compounds, 73 lines, 10,112 genes, built by `79c` as the mean over ACTIVE signatures (`is_hiq == 1` and per-signature `tas >= 0.4`). A compound is PRESENT in line L if any of its `pert_id`s has a row for L; per-(compound, line) quantities are the MAX over its `pert_id`s. Every count below is conditional on the `tas >= 0.4` floor the universe already carries. **(3) Perturbation universe.** `data/raw/lincs2020/siginfo_beta.txt`, `pert_type` in {`trt_xpr` (CRISPR, primary), `trt_sh.cgs` (shRNA consensus, secondary), `trt_sh` (raw shRNA, reported only)}, `cmap_name` in the 377 target genes, `cell_iname` mapped to `ModelID` through `results/lincs/cell_coverage.tsv` with `Non-Cancerous` lines dropped, exactly as `76b` / `79m`. `trt_oe` (overexpression) is counted as well, BEYOND the spec's Section 2 table, because Section 1 predicts agonists against overexpression and the table omits it. Counts are reported at each filter: all signatures; signatures on a mapped cancer line; signatures on one of the 73 drug-matrix lines; `is_hiq == 1`. **`is_hiq` is REPORTED as a stratum, not applied** - it is a quality floor and S1 chooses floors. **(4) Gene space.** All per-profile quantities are computed on the 10,112-gene drug-profile space (`drug_line_profiles_genes.tsv`), because that is the space any S2 comparison must use; gctx rows (entrez) are mapped to it through `level5_bing_genes.tsv`, and genes that fail to map are reported, never imputed. `feature_space` (978 landmark, 9,134 best inferred) is carried per gene, because an L1000 best-inferred value is a model output and not a measurement. Only **48** of the 377 target genes are landmark and **279** are on the space at all. **(5) Extraction.** `scripts/d135/s0a_extract_perturbations.py` reads the gctx files in 10,000-column blocks (as `79m`) and keeps only the selected signature rows; the 6.5 GB and 11.8 GB matrices are never loaded whole. `h5py` exists only in the `celligner` env, so extraction runs there (as `76b` / `79m` do) and the analysis, `scripts/d135/s0b_feasibility.py`, runs in `repurposing`. Threads set explicitly to 8 in both. Signature-level matrices are extracted only for signatures on the 73 drug-matrix lines, which is where any test could live; coverage counts outside those lines are reported from siginfo alone. **(6) PAIRED CELL - the real testable scope, and the number the spec's Section 2 does not give.** For perturbation arm P, a PAIRED CELL is a triple (drug D, gene G, line L) with: G an annotated target gene of D; D has >= 1 drug profile in L; >= 1 signature of arm P perturbing G exists in L. Attrition is reported step by step - 377 genes -> genes with any signature of P -> genes with a signature in a drug-matrix line -> (G, L) perturbation cells -> (G, L) cells in which some annotated drug of G is profiled -> paired (D, G, L) cells - with distinct drugs, genes and lines at each step. The spec's headline (208 targets, 8-12 shared lines) is a COVERAGE number; the paired count is expected to be smaller and the gap is reported as attrition, not hidden. **(7) Perturbation strength, per signature, nothing compared:** `nz2` = genes at |z| >= 2 on the 10,112 space, `nz4`, `mean_abs`, together with siginfo's own `tas`, `cc_q75`, `nsample` and `is_hiq`. Per (G, L, arm) the value is the MAX over that cell's signatures. Distributions are reported per arm. CANDIDATE floors are COSTED, NOT CHOSEN: `nz2` >= 0 / 50 / 100 / 200 / 400 / 800, `tas` >= 0.1 / 0.2 / 0.3 / 0.4, and `is_hiq == 1`, each reporting the paired cells, genes, drugs and lines it would remove. **No floor is adopted at S0; that is S1.** **(8) Target-mRNA QC - the measurement this stage exists for.** For a signature perturbing G, where G is on the gene space, the QC statistic is the z of G's OWN mRNA in that signature. The full distribution is reported, with pass rates at three descriptive cut-offs - z <= -1 (primary descriptive), z <= -2 (strict), z < 0 (sign only) - for the three knockdown arms, and the mirrored cut-offs for `trt_oe`. Rates are reported SEPARATELY PER ARM and split again by `landmark` vs `best inferred`. **Pre-stated reading, fixed before the numbers are seen: a shRNA failure and a CRISPR failure do not mean the same thing.** shRNA degrades the transcript, so reduced mRNA is the direct expected readout and a failure is evidence that the knockdown did not work. CRISPR disrupts the GENE: a frameshift transcript that escapes nonsense-mediated decay can leave mRNA near normal while the protein is gone, so a CRISPR perturbation can be fully effective with no mRNA movement. **A CRISPR mRNA-QC failure is therefore NOT counted as a failed perturbation**, here or at any later stage; it is reported as a limit of the QC. If the CRISPR pass rate is low, the conclusion is that this QC does not transfer to CRISPR and S1 must choose another check (a strength floor, or the DepMap gene effect of the same gene in the same line) - NOT that the CRISPR data are bad. **No cell is excluded on QC at S0.** **(9) Strata of spec Section 6, per paired cell.** (3) ESSENTIAL: `data/raw/depmap_24q4/CRISPRInferredCommonEssentials.csv` (1,523 genes; 60 of the 377 targets). (8) TARGET NOT ACTIVE: `OmicsExpressionProteinCodingGenesTPMLogp1.csv` log2(TPM+1) for (G, L), reported as a distribution with descriptive cut-offs >= 1 and >= 2, plus mutation status from `OmicsSomaticMutationsMatrixDamaging.csv` and `...Hotspot.csv`; 376 of 377 genes have an expression column and GUCY1B2 has none - reported MISSING, never imputed. (7) POLYPHARMACOLOGY: distinct annotated target genes and distinct `tid` units per drug, from `units.tsv`. (1) KINETICS: distinct `pert_time` among the ACTIVE drug signatures the profile averages (`level5_bing_hiq_sigs.tsv.gz`), per (drug, line); only cells with >= 2 available timepoints could ever probe the kinetics excuse, and because the production profile already averages over time this is availability-in-principle - re-deriving per-time profiles is S1 work. (5) WEAK PERTURBATION: (7) above. (6) WEAK DRUG RESPONSE: the drug profile's `nz2` and `tas_max`, as D134-S0(6). (9) THIN OVERLAP: paired cells per line. (4) shRNA SEED: the arm itself. **(2) NON-CATALYTIC SCAFFOLDING ROLES IS NOT COMPUTABLE:** no curated list of targets with scaffolding function exists in the repo and none will be invented, so that stratum is reported MISSING with its reason (D134-S0(9) honesty rule). (10) ANNOTATION ERROR is irreducible and is not a stratum. **(10) THE BOTTOM LINE S0 must deliver:** the number of paired cells, and the number of paired cells OUTSIDE EVERY COMPUTABLE EXCULPATORY STRATUM - gene not common-essential, target expressed in that line, drug carrying exactly one annotated target gene (a <= 2-gene variant reported beside it), perturbation above an illustrative strength floor, drug profile above an illustrative strength floor - reported for CRISPR BOTH with and without the mRNA-QC condition, per (8). Illustrative combinations are labelled illustrative and none is adopted. Per spec Section 6 this test can establish presence but not absence, so the count of cells capable of a CLEAN POSITIVE is the number that decides whether S1 / S2 is worth running, and a verdict that it is not is an acceptable outcome of an audit. **(11) Spec Section 2 is re-derived, not assumed** (standing instruction after two errors were found in the D134 spec). Any discrepancy is reported as a revision, naming the filter that reproduces each published figure, rather than reproducing the published number. **(12) Outputs,** `results/lincs/d135/`: `perturbation_sigs.tsv.gz` (per signature: strength and target-mRNA QC), `perturbation_coverage.tsv` (per gene x arm), `paired_cells.tsv` (per drug x gene x line x arm with every stratum), `attrition.tsv`, `strata_summary.tsv`, `d135_s0_run.json`. Scripts `scripts/d135/s0a_extract_perturbations.py` (env `celligner`) and `scripts/d135/s0b_feasibility.py` (env `repurposing`), threads 8. | Pre-registered before running (this row is committed and pushed before either script is executed) |
| D135-S0 result (2026-11-17): **the test is feasible, but it is far narrower than the spec implies, two of the spec's three Section 2 rows are computed under different filters and one cannot be reproduced at all, and the headline QC finding is not about CRISPR vs shRNA - it is about which genes the L1000 platform actually measures. 2,350 paired CRISPR (drug, target, line) cells exist; 46 to 121 of them sit outside every computable exculpatory stratum. S1 and a positive-control S2 are worth running, with a narrowed claim.** Scripts `scripts/d135/s0a_extract_perturbations.py` (env `celligner`, 61 s, threads 8) and `scripts/d135/s0b_feasibility.py` (env `repurposing`, 20 s, threads 8); logs `logs/d135_s0a.log`, `logs/d135_s0b.log`; outputs `results/lincs/d135/` (`perturbation_sigs.tsv.gz`, `perturbation_coverage.tsv`, `perturbation_qc.tsv`, `paired_cells.tsv`, `attrition.tsv`, `strata_summary.tsv`, `d135_s0_run.json`, `d135_s0a_extract.json`). **No concordance, no drug-to-perturbation comparison of any kind, no positive control run, no AUROC, no null, no outcome label, no threshold chosen, no production score changed, nothing re-ranked.** **(1) Spec Section 2 is reproduced only in part, and its three rows do not share a filter.** The CRISPR row (208 targets / 837 signatures / 8 lines) reproduces EXACTLY under `is_hiq == 1` restricted to the drug-matrix lines. The shRNA-consensus row (209 / 1,627 / 10) reproduces only with NO `is_hiq` AND NO drug-line restriction - it is an any-cancer-line count; under the CRISPR row's own filter it is **174 / 515 / 9**. The raw-shRNA row (207 / 6,118 / 12) is **NOT REPRODUCED** under any of sixteen readings (two cell-line mappings x four quality filters x line restriction); the closest are 207 / 6,136 / 13 (cancer lines, `is_hiq`) and 207 / 6,109 / 11 (drug lines, `is_hiq`). Reported unreproduced rather than guessed. **The table under one consistent filter** (`is_hiq`, drug-matrix lines) is CRISPR 208 / 837 / 8, consensus shRNA 174 / 515 / 9, raw shRNA 207 / 6,109 / 11; without `is_hiq` it is 279 / 4,396 / 8, 209 / 1,602 / 9, 210 / 11,782 / 11. **A second correction: the drug matrix spans 73 cell lines, not 87** (63 for the 307 target-annotated compounds). 87 is close to the 86 lines the high-quality compound set has BEFORE `79c` applies its `tas >= 0.4` floor, so the spec's 87 appears to predate that floor. So of the spec's four Section 2 figures, one reproduces, one needs a different filter, one does not reproduce and one is wrong. **(2) Paired-cell attrition - the real testable scope, which Section 2 does not give.** CRISPR: 377 target genes -> **280** have any `trt_xpr` signature (7,226 signatures, 21 lines) -> **279** on one of the 73 drug-matrix lines (4,396 signatures, **8** lines) -> **1,936** (gene, line) perturbation cells -> **1,049** of those have at least one annotated drug profiled in the same line (255 genes, **7** lines) -> **2,350 PAIRED (drug, gene, line) cells, 201 drugs, 255 genes, 7 lines.** Consensus shRNA: 209 -> 209 (1,602 signatures, 9 lines) -> 1,376 -> 901 -> **2,250 paired cells, 215 drugs, 178 genes, 9 lines.** Raw shRNA: 210 -> 210 (11,782 signatures, 11 lines) -> 1,499 -> 972 -> **2,415 paired cells, 217 drugs, 183 genes, 10 lines.** Overexpression (counted beyond the spec's table, because Section 1 predicts agonists against it): 128 genes, 1,861 signatures, 10 lines, 947 perturbation cells. **Power is concentrated exactly as limitation 9 predicts:** five lines carry 389-428 of the 2,350 CRISPR cells each, the sixth 254 and the seventh 22. **(3) Perturbation strength, and an asymmetry nobody has written down.** Per signature on the 10,112-gene space: CRISPR `nz2` (genes at |z| >= 2) median **51** (IQR 28-112, p95 578, max 7,159), `nz4` median 4, `tas` median **0.14** (p95 0.39); consensus shRNA `nz2` median **14** (IQR 6-29), `tas` median 0.10; raw shRNA `nz2` median **88** (IQR 49-178), `tas` median 0.24; overexpression `nz2` median 59, `tas` 0.19. **The drug side of this comparison already requires per-signature `tas >= 0.4`, and the genetic side mostly cannot clear that bar: only 247 of 2,350 CRISPR paired cells (10.5%), 94 of 2,250 consensus-shRNA cells (4.2%) and 1,061 of 2,415 raw-shRNA cells (43.9%) have a perturbation that would.** Candidate floors, COSTED NOT CHOSEN, on paired cells: CRISPR `nz2` >= 50 / 100 / 200 / 400 / 800 leaves 1,853 / 1,258 / 750 / 450 / 237 cells over 242 / 212 / 151 / 105 / 52 genes; `tas` >= 0.2 / 0.3 / 0.4 leaves 1,197 / 535 / 247; `is_hiq >= 1` leaves 859 cells over 152 genes. Consensus shRNA is far more fragile: `nz2` >= 50 / 100 leaves 418 / **175** of 2,250 cells. Raw shRNA barely moves: `nz2` >= 100 leaves 2,139 of 2,415. **The arm the spec designates as the seed-effect mitigation is also the weakest-responding arm, by a factor of six on `nz2`.** **(4) TARGET-mRNA QC - the measurement S0 existed for, and the pre-stated CRISPR caution turned out not to be the binding limit.** Per signature, where the perturbed gene is on the gene space: **CRISPR** 3,268 of 4,396 signatures, target z median -0.17, pass **22.5%** at z <= -1 (14.2% at <= -2, 58.1% at z < 0). **Consensus shRNA** 1,402 of 1,602, pass **19.0%** / 9.8% / 59.3%. **Raw shRNA** 10,629 of 11,782, pass **28.0%** / 14.5% / 62.7%. **Overexpression** 1,689 of 1,861, pass 15.7% at z >= +1. **Those headline rates are nearly meaningless on their own, because the determining variable is the L1000 feature space, not the technology.** Split by it: on LANDMARK genes (directly measured) CRISPR passes **70.4%** (776 signatures, 28 genes, target z median **-2.65**), consensus shRNA **54.8%** (436 signatures, 48 genes, median -1.17) and raw shRNA **54.8%** (4,446 signatures, median -1.18). On BEST-INFERRED genes the perturbed gene's own z is centred on zero (CRISPR median +0.01, shRNA +0.02) and pass rates collapse to **7.6% / 2.8% / 8.8%** - at or below what a shrunken null would produce, i.e. the value carries essentially no information about whether the perturbation worked, because it is a model output imputed from the landmarks and not a measurement of that gene. **The reading, with the two arms kept separate as D135-S0(8) required.** (a) **shRNA:** on the 48 landmark targets the transcript-degradation readout works and about 55% of knockdowns show it; a failure there is real evidence that the knockdown did not take, and that is a usable per-signature filter for those genes. (b) **CRISPR:** the pre-stated worry was that CRISPR could be effective at the protein level with mRNA barely moving (frameshift transcripts escaping nonsense-mediated decay), so a CRISPR mRNA failure must never be scored as a failed perturbation. **That caution stands and is still the right reading, but it is NOT what limits the measurement here: on directly measured genes CRISPR passes MORE often than either shRNA arm (70.4% vs 54.8%), the opposite of the feared ordering.** NMD-escape does appear in the tail - 29.6% of landmark CRISPR signatures do not reach z <= -1 - but it is a minority effect. (c) **What each rate can and cannot be used for:** the landmark rates (CRISPR 70.4%, shRNA 54.8%) can be used as a perturbation-quality filter, for **28 CRISPR genes and 48 shRNA genes only**; the inferred-gene rates can be used for NOTHING except to document that this QC does not exist for the other ~231 targets. Applying the QC across the board would delete about 90% of inferred-gene perturbations for a reason that is an artefact of imputation, which is precisely the error D135-S0(8) pre-registered against. **No cell was excluded on QC here.** S1 should apply mRNA QC only on landmark targets and use a strength floor or the DepMap gene effect of the same gene and line for the rest. **(5) How the paired set distributes over the exculpatory strata (CRISPR, of 2,350 cells).** Common-essential target **20.1%** (29 genes); target not expressed in that line (log2(TPM+1) < 1) **37.2%**; drug carrying more than one annotated target gene **90.7%** (more than one target unit 43.0%); weak perturbation at the illustrative `nz2` < 100 **46.5%**; weak drug profile at `nz2` < 100 5.7%; only one drug timepoint available **69.5%**; target mRNA not on the gene space at all (QC impossible) 20.1%; mRNA QC failing at z <= -1 54.8%; target damaging-mutated 0.09%, hotspot-mutated 0.34%. Expression was missing for 0 paired cells (the one gene without a DepMap column, GUCY1B2, never reaches a paired cell). **Limitation 2 (knockout removes scaffolding functions an active-site inhibitor leaves intact) is NOT COMPUTABLE** - there is no curated list of targets with non-catalytic roles in the repo and none was invented - so it is missing from every count below and the clean-positive numbers are an **upper bound**. **(6) THE BOTTOM LINE: how many cells could yield a CLEAN POSITIVE.** Cumulative, in a fixed order, CRISPR: 2,350 paired -> drop common-essential targets **1,878** -> drop targets not expressed in the line **1,003** -> drop drugs with more than one annotated target gene **80** -> drop weak drug profiles **70** -> drop weak perturbations (`nz2` >= 100, illustrative) **46**. **46 cells, 24 drugs, 14 genes, 6 lines.** Relaxing the single-target rule to <= 2 annotated genes gives **121 cells, 42 drugs, 25 genes**; dropping both illustrative strength floors gives **80 cells, 32 drugs, 18 genes**. Requiring in addition that the target be landmark AND pass mRNA QC leaves **18-23 cells over 3 genes**. Consensus shRNA: 2,250 -> 1,692 -> 1,154 -> 140 -> 124 -> **4** at `nz2` >= 100 (124 with no perturbation floor) - the arm is destroyed by its own weakness, not by the strata. Raw shRNA: 2,415 -> 1,797 -> 1,247 -> 144 -> 128 -> **122**. **The dominant single cut is polypharmacology: 1,003 -> 80, a 92% loss**, and it is as much definitional as biological - 153 of the 307 compounds carry >= 2 annotated genes, and a multi-target drug contributes one paired cell per gene. **What the clean CRISPR 46 actually contains** is reassuringly recognisable: ABL1 (nilotinib, ponatinib), BRAF (vemurafenib, dabrafenib), EGFR (erlotinib, gefitinib, AZD-9291), TYMS (fluorouracil, floxuridine), ESR1 (fulvestrant, clomifene), VDR (3 vitamin-D analogues), PPARG (pioglitazone, rosiglitazone), AGTR1, DPP4, FKBP1A, PDE5A, HRH2, PTGFR, ACE. **(7) A design problem in the spec's own positive controls, found as coverage and not computed.** All three control families exist in the paired set: HDAC1/2/3/6 (104 CRISPR cells, 5 drugs, 7 lines), PSMB (112 cells, 4 drugs), TOP2A (39 cells, 8 drugs, 6 lines). **But TOP2A is 100% DepMap common-essential and the PSMB cells are 57-61% common-essential**, so two of the three controls sit INSIDE the essential stratum that Section 6 limitation 3 marks as exculpatory - the stratum where a knockout gives a death / arrest signature rather than a mechanism signature. If those controls pass, the method may be validated by shared cytotoxicity rather than by mechanism, and that pass would not license reading concordance for non-essential targets. HDAC (25% essential) is the only control family mostly outside it. S1 must state in advance what a TOP2A or PSMB pass licenses. **(8) VERDICT: run S1 and the positive-control S2, with a narrowed claim.** CRISPR is the arm with the scope: 2,350 paired cells, and 46-121 of them capable of a clean positive over 14-25 genes and 6-7 lines. That is enough to establish PRESENCE of target signal for a dozen or so well-known drug-target pairs and it is the right size for a per-target map; it is nowhere near enough to make a population statistic over 2,350 cells meaningful when 91% of them are excused in advance. The spec's Section 6 corollary therefore binds harder than the spec assumed. The consensus-shRNA arm should be treated as reporting-only alongside raw shRNA unless S1 lowers its floor deliberately, because at any serious strength floor it has 4 clean cells. **Nothing was computed that compares a drug with a perturbation; feasibility is the whole of this result.** **(9) What S1 must now fix, which S0 has made unavoidable.** (a) mRNA QC restricted to landmark targets, with a stated replacement (strength floor or DepMap gene effect) for the other ~231. (b) The perturbation strength floor, knowing it costs the consensus-shRNA arm 92% of its cells at `nz2` >= 100 and CRISPR 46%. (c) Whether the secondary arm survives (b) at all. (d) Polypharmacology: excluded, modelled, or reported as a stratum - it is the single largest cut and the current single-gene rule is strict. (e) What a positive control that is itself a common-essential gene licenses. (f) Kinetics (limitation 1) needs per-timepoint drug profiles, which the production consensus does not keep, and 69.5% of CRISPR cells have only one timepoint available in the first place - so S1 should either re-derive per-time profiles or withdraw the kinetics stratification. (g) Section 2 of the spec needs the corrections in (1). | Closed. S1 (freeze) is next and inherits (9)(a)-(g) |

Full rebuild spec: [`SPEC_rebuild_v2.md`](SPEC_rebuild_v2.md).

## 2. Assumptions

What the results rely on. "Impact" = how much results could change if the assumption is wrong. "Check" = what we
did or could do about it.

| # | Assumption | Impact | Check |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | Purity (stroma/immune content) is a nuisance to remove, but it may carry real mechanism (immune evasion, fibrosis) | High | Sensitivity run without ESTIMATE adjustment: proposed, not run |
| ~~A2~~ | ~~limma treats tumors and normals as equally variable; tumors are more variable~~ | Resolved (D69) | Tested 2026-10-20: tumors 1.2-1.8x more variable; v2 models the arms separately (voomByGroup) |
| A3 | Gene-gene correlation in healthy tissue represents "chance" for tumors (normal-split null); the null is unadjusted for covariates while the signature is adjusted | High | v2 (D73): the null uses exactly the Step 4a normals the signature used (bug fixed 10-28); a covariate-adjusted null was tested (no false calls, more master regulators) and not adopted under the pre-set rule. FDR-cut lists are unstable between nulls while NES rankings agree (rho >= 0.98), so the drug side uses NES weights (D74) |
| A4 | GTEx is a fair healthy reference despite post-mortem collection and source being collinear with tumor vs normal; adjacent normals carry field effects | High | GTEx vs adjacent agreement median ~0.6 (18 groups) |
| A5 | Partial GTEx tissue matches add cell-type differences to signatures (pancreas acinar vs ductal, uterus myometrium, adult adrenal for NBL) | High | Flagged per group |
| A6 | Regulator activity can be read from its targets' mRNA; misses non-transcriptional activation (kinases) | Medium | PROGENy / CPTAC phospho proposed |
| A7 | The expressed-regulator filter drops active but low-mRNA regulators | Medium | None yet |
| A8 | CollecTRI is context-free; unsigned edges (~1.5k of 64.5k) are treated as activation | Medium | Supporting on the disease side (rarely significant: median 0 FDR < 0.10 regulators per group, Step 7 10-29); part of the drug-side consensus (D75) |
| A9 | ARACNe: co-expression = regulation; one network per cancer, shared by its subtypes and strata | Medium | Edges losing > 50% strength after purity / quality / plate: median 1.9% per network (full set), 4.0% (TF-only; max UCS 12%); BRCA_LUM vs BRCA tests the subtype part (D68 / D81: not material, 2026-11-01); all shared networks (D82, 2026-11-01): identity-only edges 0-7% per network but 35-89% for lineage regulators (BRCA ESR1 51%, GATA3 45%); subtype-specific edges up to 17% (SARC) |
| A10 | Subtype labels (PanCanAtlas PAM50, IDH/codel, marker-based ALL lineage; METABRIC CLAUDIN_SUBTYPE; CPTAC PAM50) are correct | Medium | Taken as given |
| A11 | RSEM expected counts behave like counts for voom | Low | Standard practice |
| A12 | Patients with missing covariates (< 2%) are missing at random | Low | Dropped |
| A13 | GTEx age = middle of the 10-year bin | Low | |
| A14 | The diagnosis sample represents the patient; metastasis/recurrence handled by an indicator | Low | |
| A15 | BH FDR is valid under positive dependence between regulators | Low | |
| A16 | Filter thresholds (>= 10 targets, >= 50% present, logCPM >= 1, >= 30 patients, >= 10 normals) are judgment calls | Low | Sensitivity analyses advisable |
| A17 | The long-minus-short gene index measures RNA quality | Medium | Agrees only partly with GTEx RIN / ischemic time; treated as a plate-linked technical axis. 10-18: computed jointly, TCGA normals score higher than GTEx and tumors lower; not used in Step 4 (D50) |
| A18 | The recurring module is biology (PI reading), not a technical axis | Medium | Plate-linked above chance; RNA quality, GC bias and multi-mapping ruled out; cause unknown. v2 (D79): subgroups are defined from the clustering without it (decision changed in 5 of 41 groups); treated as a nuisance axis pending PI confirmation of D26 |
| A19 | Regulator modules (correlation >= 0.7) are meaningful units, and module scores capture patient differences | Medium | Mean-member and PC1 scores agree (r 0.998) |
| A20 | Comparing replication patients with their cohort's average tumor is equivalent to the TCGA comparison with healthy tissue once scores are standardized | Medium | Colon: TCGA modules replicate (rho 0.89-0.94); breast axes replicate in gene content |
| A21 | Array (GSE39582, METABRIC) and RNA-seq scores are comparable after standardizing within each cohort | Medium | Same patterns found on both platforms |
| A22 | CMScaller calls on TCGA/replication data are correct, and the CMS1/3 vs 2/4 naming describes the colon split | Low | Consistent across three cohorts |
| A23 | READ clusters reflect biology despite their plate association | High (for READ) | v1 only: READ clusters did not reproduce in v2 and are not carried over (D79) |
| A24 | Breast axes are continuous biology, not technical; principal-component signs are arbitrary (high/low are labels only) | Medium | v1 only: breast axes are not carried into the v2 subgroups (D79); technical screen (D37), replication (D40) |
| A25 | Cutting axes into thirds captures the axis ends; the cut point is arbitrary and patients appear in several axis strata | Low | v1 only (breast axes not used in v2, D79) |
| A26 | One healthy reference (the parent's GTEx tissue) suits every stratum of a cancer | Low | Same as A4 |
| A27 | Stratum signatures are dominated by the shared cancer-vs-normal signal (0.97-0.99 similar to the parent); stratum differences are a thin layer | High (for drug ranking) | Addressed by specificity (D43) and, in v2, sibling specificity with proliferation removed (`spec_sib_mech`); LGG-noncodel and TGCT-SEM subgroups barely differ in drug response (reported as one list) |
| A28 | ~~(v2) The part of the adjacent-vs-GTEx difference shared across organs is lab/protocol + average post-mortem effect; some may be real biology of tumor-adjacent tissue (field effect, tumor cells, wound response) and is removed with it~~ (lab subtraction dropped, D57) | High | Acceptance checks C5 (known biology), C6 (proliferation retained) |
| A29 | ~~(v2) One lab correction per gene (median of 10 organ units) fits organs without adjacent normals; organ-specific lab effects remain~~ (lab subtraction dropped, D57) | Medium | C4 (held-out unit) |
| A30 | ~~(v2) Uncertainty of the lab correction is not carried into the moderated t, so z is slightly over-confident~~ (lab subtraction dropped, D57) | Low | z used as ranks |
| A31 | ~~(v2) Ischemic time and ventilator death capture GTEx post-mortem variation linearly; other agonal factors not modelled~~ (superseded by A33) | Medium | Ischemic effect agrees poorly across tissues (rho 0.13) |
| A32 | (v2) The 71 genes without a mappability value behave like normally mappable genes | Low | Flagged |
| A33 | (v2) GTEx collection effects are additive per gene in log ischemic time and death circumstance; other agonal / handling factors are unmodelled | Medium | Held-out variance (script 34): autolysis, fixative time, RIN and batch add nothing beyond M1 |
