| D137 result (2026-11-17): **the mechanism trace does not discriminate on ARACNe; false-positive rate 3 of 5.** The PROCEDURE Section 7 negative controls were run for the first time, on cohort-matched ARACNe regulons (`regulons_aracne_project_v2_tf.rds` -> `data/processed/lincs/trace_aracne/aracne_regulon_edges.tsv.gz`, 6,045,968 edges, 41 networks, 68.5% in the LINCS gene space). Pre-stated before any number: a control "closes" when no link L1-L7 is `breaks` or `not evaluable`, and the fraction that closes is the procedure's false-positive rate. **3 of 5 closed (0.60), and BOTH real pairs broke** - on ARACNe the trace is worse than uninformative. Closed: NC1 regorafenib/RET/LUAD/HCC515 (RET log2TPM 0.029, i.e. target absent; concordance 52.3%, 76.0th percentile), NC3 MEK-162/MAP2K2/COADREAD/HT-29 (random cohort shuffle, seed 20261117; concordance 57.5%, **99.9th percentile** - the strongest apparent mechanism of anything tested), NC4 estramustine/ESR2/BRCA_LUMB/MCF7 (alkylator annotated to a nuclear receptor; 57.4%, 73.4th). Behaved correctly: NC2 vemurafenib in BRAF-WT/KRAS-G13D MDA-MB-231 (breaks at L5), NC5 midostaurin/KDR/LIHC (L3 `not evaluable`, empty footprint). Real pairs: fulvestrant/ESR1/BRCA_LUMA concordance 50.4%, mean signed f(z) **-0.018**, 39.7th percentile, breaks at L6; vemurafenib/BRAF/SKCM 45.6%, **-0.023**, 26.5th percentile, breaks at L5. **Both flip sign versus CollecTRI** (+0.025 / 64.1st and +0.034 / 82.4th). Machinery verified by replay on the CollecTRI footprints with the same code, which reproduces the published values exactly, so this is the regulon and not a bug. **The two networks nominate almost disjoint gene sets for the same target**: fulvestrant/ESR1 shares 6 genes between a 478-gene CollecTRI and a 131-gene ARACNe footprint; vemurafenib/BRAF shares 159 of 1063 vs 1223 (87.4% sign agreement where they do overlap). Two further findings, both structural and independent of any response data: (a) **the "~65% MYC dependent" BRAF result was substantially a CollecTRI regulon-size artefact** - CollecTRI's MYC has 881 genes against a median regulon of 9, while ARACNe's median is ~100, and on SKCM the BRAF footprint's hub is MEF2D at 20.4% over 11 regulators (8.0 effective); (b) **TOP1, TOP2A, TYMS, DHFR, GART, RRM1 and TUBB have zero OmniPath route rows**, so a classical chemotherapeutic acting through its own effector cannot be traced at all. Checked and NOT supported: that ARACNe's ESR1 regulon is noise for want of variance in a subtype cohort (ESR1 sd 1.282 in BRCA_LUMA, 95.2nd percentile of all genes). Outputs `results/lincs/trace_aracne/` (`control_selection.json`, `links_L1_L4.json`, `links_L5_L7.json`, `verdicts.json`, `false_positive_rate.json`, `network_comparison.tsv`, `trace_records.tsv`); `scripts/trace/aracne/` | **Closed as a negative.** Neither the ARACNe nor the CollecTRI traces count as positives: Section 7 says the controls come first and they now give 0.60. Open (user): whether to repair the procedure (the footprint is a property of the network, not the drug, so L4 needs a network-agnostic definition or an explicit two-network agreement requirement), or to stop tracing and report the negative. |
| D138 (2026-11-17, user): **ARACNe regulon sets only; CollecTRI retired.** Answers D137's open question and the earlier scope question (2026-11-16). Consequences inventoried: (a) the lead production ranking is `fda_ranking_v2_consensus.tsv`, where `REGULON=consensus` is the per-regulator **mean of ARACNe v2 TF and CollecTRI** (`79e:80`), so **no ARACNe-only FDA ranking exists** - the ARACNe-only arm stops at `drug_reversal_v2_tf.tsv` / `stratum_reversal_v2_tf.tsv` and the ranking stages (`79i`, `79t`, `79t3`) have not been run on it; (b) **D128 is CollecTRI-only by construction** (`scripts/d128/config.py` `NETWORK = "collectri"`, pinned as P3), so its A1-A4 and the whole target-mechanism score would need rebuilding on ARACNe; (c) 25 scripts reference CollecTRI, including `79h_prolif_regulators.R` (the `ALL`/`collectri` proliferation set the trace's L6 uses). | Decided by the user. Open: whether to re-run the production ranking ARACNe-only, and what replaces D128's P3. |
| D138.1 result (2026-11-17): **ARACNe-only does NOT repair the network-dependence found in D137 - the instability is internal to ARACNe.** The same footprint was rebuilt once per ARACNe cohort network, changing nothing else, and the identical L5 replayed (`scripts/trace/aracne/t4a_network_stability.py`, `results/lincs/trace_aracne/network_stability.tsv`). **fulvestrant/ESR1 in MCF7, 41 networks:** footprint size 3-2295 genes, pairwise **Jaccard 0.011** (10th-90th 0.000-0.048), mean signed f(z) -0.2524 to +0.0492 and **positive in only 5 of 41**, generic percentile 1.3-72.0. **vemurafenib/BRAF in A375, 41 networks:** Jaccard 0.112, mean f(z) -0.0374 to +0.0986, **positive in 34 of 41**, generic percentile 16.1-97.5 - fairly robust, except that its own cohort-matched network **SKCM is the worst of the 41** (-0.0232, 26.6th percentile) against a median +0.0327 at the 78.4th. Tested and **rejected** as a rescue: that low-variance cohorts give noise regulons. Restricting ESR1 to the 17 networks where ESR1 is above the 75th percentile of expression variance leaves only 3 of 17 positive (median -0.0563, 18.4th percentile); **all-BRCA, the best-powered ESR1 regulon of all (sd 99.8th percentile, 251 genes), gives -0.0773 at the 18.3rd percentile.** r(variance percentile, mean f(z)) = +0.449 for ESR1 and -0.117 for BRAF (for BRAF the covariate is the wrong one - BRAF is not a regulator, so the relevant variance is of the TFs it reaches). **This reproduces a behaviour recorded since D83:** the ARACNe ESR1 list reads anti-estrogens in MCF7 as ESR1 *activation* (D83: +2.2 to +2.7 in all 10 profiles, CollecTRI -2.4; D85: the consensus cancels to ~0; D88, D89, D90, D92 the same). It is a known, repeatedly observed property of the ARACNe ESR1 regulon, not a new artefact of the trace. | **Closed as a negative.** Under ARACNe-only, a single cohort network cannot carry a mechanism claim for a specific TF: for ESR1 every well-powered network reads the drug backwards, and for BRAF the cohort-matched network is the outlier. Open (user): aggregate across ARACNe networks (D95 tested a metaVIPER-style combination for production scoring and rejected it on dominance), or stop claiming gene-level mechanism and report the negative. |
| D138.2 note (2026-11-17, user question): **the `n_present >= 10 AND frac_present >= 0.5` regulator filter is not what is limiting us; recommend keeping it.** Measured on the ARACNe v2 TF arm. **Disease side** (`50_viper.R:118`, `results/step6/aracne_project_v2_tf/regulator_filter.tsv`, 50,961 regulator x group rows, 41 groups, 1,496 regulators): 84.2% kept; `not expressed` drops 6,226 (12.2%) and `too few targets present` 1,806 (4.0% of the 44,735 expressed rows). **`frac_present >= 0.5` fails ZERO rows** - it never binds on the disease side. **Drug side** (`79d_drug_viper.R:92`, targets pruned to the LINCS gene space, `results/lincs/drug_viper_regulators_v2_tf.tsv`, 40,009 regulator x network rows, 38 networks): 94.0% kept, 2,389 dropped (6.0%) - 1,056 on size alone, 783 on fraction alone, 550 on both. What is dropped is small and uninformative: median 15 total targets, 8 in the LINCS space, frac 0.485, and **78% (1,855 of 2,389) are KRAB zinc-finger regulators**; only 12 regulators are never kept in any network. **No mechanism-relevant regulator is lost anywhere it exists**: ESR1 kept 23/23, MYC 38/38, MEF2D 38/38, HSF1 38/38, ETS1 38/38, SP3 38/38, RARA 38/38, THRA 38/38, MEOX1 14/14, MEOX2 13/13, E2F1 37/38. Relaxing to `n>=10, frac>=0.3` admits +777 rows (median 23 genes), to `n>=5, frac>=0.3` +2,228 (median 9 genes). **Implementation gotcha:** `kept` is only the first gate - `viper()` is then called with `minsize = MIN_TARGETS = 10` (`79d:96`, `50_viper.R:138/148`), so relaxing the filter alone admits nothing below 10 targets; `minsize` must move with it. **What actually limits ESR1** is not this filter: ARACNe builds an ESR1 regulon in only 29 of 41 networks, and the separate `expressed` gate drops 4 more, leaving 24; the size/fraction criteria drop none of them. | Recommend keep. Removing it changes no trace result and does not touch D138.1's cross-network instability. If more regulators are wanted, the levers are ARACNe network construction and the `expressed` gate, not this filter. |
| D138.3 result (2026-11-17, user question "what was the biological basis for < 10 targets"): **none was recorded, and the floor is measurably too LOW rather than too strict.** The only recorded justification is D16 (10-02): "avoid scoring regulators that are absent or barely measured" - an estimator-reliability rationale, owned by the PI, with no biological claim and no empirical basis. Two facts confirm it was a convention: **VIPER's own default is `minsize = 25`**, so our 10 is MORE permissive than the package default; and D86 dropped the `>= 50%` half of the rule outright when it conflicted with an experiment ("the 50% rule is dropped because tumor and curated regulons keep few landmark targets"), which a biological constraint would not permit. **Measured** (`scripts/trace/aracne/t5a_minsize_test.R`, `results/lincs/trace_aracne/minsize_reliability.tsv`): the 202 BRCA_LUMA regulators with >= 150 LINCS-space targets, each regulon subsampled to k targets, real VIPER on the real 1,221 MCF7 compound profiles, Spearman of the k-target activity against the full-regulon activity across compounds, 10 subsamples per k. Median r (and share of regulators above r = 0.8): **k=3 0.368 (0.5%), k=5 0.460 (1.2%), k=8 0.549 (4.1%), k=10 0.603 (6.4%), k=15 0.685 (16.5%), k=25 0.783 (43.7%), k=50 0.886 (79.9%), k=100 0.959 (99.2%)**. **At our floor of 10 a regulon reproduces its own full-regulon activity at r = 0.60 and only 6.4% of regulators clear r = 0.8**; at VIPER's default of 25 it is 0.78 and 43.7%. **Caveat tested, and it is real but small:** genuinely-small regulons are built from somewhat stronger edges than a random subset of a large one (BRCA_LUMA median likelihood 0.312 for size 1-9 vs 0.231 for 150+, median |tfmode| 0.982 vs 0.937), so the subsample curve overstates the penalty - by roughly 35% in edge likelihood, not enough to move r = 0.60 to r = 0.8. **Second caveat:** this measures self-consistency (does a k-target regulon say what the full regulon says), not accuracy against ground truth. Cost of raising the floor on the drug side (40,009 regulator x network rows, 94.0% kept now): floor 15 keeps 90.0% (-1,604), floor 25 keeps 81.9% (-4,858), floor 50 keeps 61.7% (-12,929); ESR1 survives in 23/23, 22/23, 21/23, 19/23 networks respectively. | Answers D138.2's open question in the opposite direction: **do not remove or relax the floor.** Open (user): whether to raise it toward VIPER's default of 25, which needs its own pre-registered rule against the existing benchmarks (`79n` knockdown benchmark, the anti-estrogen controls, known-use AUC) in the D83/D90/D91 style, since it would drop 12% of scored regulator x network rows. |
| D139-S0 result (2026-11-17, user: "test VIPER's default of minsize=25; are the small regulon sets really affecting the drug scoring?"): **no - raising the floor to 25 is safe but changes essentially nothing, so it is not worth a production re-run.** Computed exactly rather than re-run, which two verified facts allow. (1) **aREA NES is independent across regulators except through `eset.filter=TRUE`**, which restricts the gene universe to genes appearing in the regulon set: raising the floor 10 -> 25 in BRCA_LUMA shrinks that universe only **9,146 -> 9,133 genes (99.9% retained)**, giving median |dNES| 2.0e-3 with **0.02% of regulator x profile cells moving more than 0.1** and median per-regulator r = 1.00000; with `eset.filter=FALSE` the NES is **exactly** invariant (max difference 0.000). So raising minsize is, to within 0.02% of cells, exactly dropping the small regulons from the score sum. (2) `rev = -(D . s)/||s||` is **linear in D**, and a drug's D is a weighted mean of its profiles' activities, so per-profile rev averages to drug-level rev. Measured over 33 groups with an ARACNe drug-activity matrix, `SCORE=weighted` (every tested regulator, weight = disease-side group NES): **drug-side floor 25** drops a median **11.5% of regulators** (6.7-41.9%) carrying **8.9% of weight mass** and **6.9% of contribution mass**, yet **Spearman rev(10) vs rev(25) across profiles is 0.9991 median, 0.9888 minimum, with only 1 of 33 groups below 0.99** (TCGA-THCA 0.9888; then TCGA-PRAD 0.9910, TCGA-ACC 0.9956, TCGA-UCS 0.9962 despite dropping 41.9% of its regulators). **Disease-side floor 25** drops 5.1% of regulators / 2.8% of contribution for Spearman 0.9997 median, 0.9972 minimum, 0 of 33 below 0.99. Reconciles with D138.3 rather than contradicting it: a 10-target regulon IS individually unreliable (r = 0.60 against its own full regulon), but small regulons are a minority of the weight mass and their errors are incoherent, so they wash out of a ~1,000-regulator weighted sum. Demonstrated for `rev`; `spec_mech` is derived from it, so the conclusion carries but is not separately measured. Outputs `results/lincs/trace_aracne/minsize25_effect.tsv` (`scripts/trace/aracne/t6a_minsize25_effect.py`). | **Closed: do not re-run production for this.** The small regulons are neither helping nor hurting the drug score, so the floor is not a lever on anything - including D138.1's cross-network instability, which is therefore not caused by small regulons. Open (user): whether to raise the floor to 25 anyway as hygiene at the next full rebuild, which costs nothing measurable and makes every retained activity estimate defensible (r 0.78 vs 0.60). |
| D139-S1 result (2026-11-17, user: "if we increase the min size further, would it increase our activity estimate reliability?"): **no - it raises the reliability of the regulators you KEEP but leaves the SCORE's reliability flat, and eventually hurts it.** Split-half: each regulon split into two random halves, VIPER run on each, so NES_A and NES_B are independent estimates of the same regulator on the same profiles; 2,000 profiles over 52 cell lines, 3 networks, 5 splits (`scripts/trace/aracne/t7a_floor_reliability.R`). **Individual regulator reliability at its OWN size** (Spearman-Brown corrected, no subsampling, so this supersedes D138.3's subsample estimate): size 10-24 median r **0.515** (2.8% above 0.8), 25-49 **0.682** (18.2%), 50-99 **0.791** (46.5%), 100-199 **0.873** (76.5%), 200+ **0.924** (94.4%). Note this is **worse** than D138.3's subsample curve (0.603 at k=10), so the "small regulons are built from stronger edges" caveat does NOT rescue them - measured at their real size they are worse, not better. **Score reproducibility vs floor** (median over 5 splits): BRCA_LUMA 0.9916 (floor 10) / 0.9921 (25) / 0.9921 (50) / 0.9918 (75) / 0.9905 (100) / 0.9884 (150) / **0.9620 (200, only 80 regulators and 9.4% of weight left)**; COADREAD 0.9958 / 0.9963 / 0.9968 / 0.9966 / 0.9965 / 0.9963 / 0.9956; SKCM 0.9888 / 0.9883 / 0.9897 / 0.9881 / 0.9902 / 0.9899 / 0.9902. **The score sits at ~0.99 from floor 10 to floor 150 and only breaks when coverage collapses.** Mechanism: the score averages ~1,000 regulators, so its reliability comes from aggregation, not from any member; removing unreliable regulators removes their signal along with their noise. The best floor is 25-50 and it buys +0.0005 to +0.001 - negligible. | **Closed: raising the floor is hygiene, not a reliability gain.** There is no floor at which the activity estimate behind the drug score becomes materially more reliable; the ~0.99 ceiling is set by aggregation, and the remaining 0.01 is not recoverable by filtering regulators. Consistent with D139-S0 (floor 25 moves the score by rho 0.999). |
| D140 (2026-11-18): **frozen signed PKN built for Experiment F (SPEC §F-MATH 3); first implementation step.** `scripts/systems_validation/fault_isolation/build_signed_pkn.py` -> `results/systems_validation/pkn/{signed_pkn.tsv, signor_signed_pkn.tsv, pkn_excluded_edges.tsv, pkn_manifest.json}`. **Primary source is `omnipath_signed_directed.tsv.gz`**, because that is already the project's signalling prior (`scripts/d126/b1_config.py` PRIOR; `scripts/d128/config.py` OMNIPATH, "primary signaling prior (P6)"). **A first build from `omnipath_raw.tsv.gz` was discarded**: it gives a different and far more activation-biased network (70,389 edges, **90.5% activating**) against the curated 15,646 edges at **70.2% activating**, and would have silently diverged from D126/D128. Result: **15,646 edges over 5,264 gene nodes, 10,980 activating / 4,666 inhibiting, 1,174 sign-disputed carried as a flag rather than dropped**; top out-degree hubs are PRKACA, SRC, PRKCA, MAPK1, CDK1 (max 250). SIGNOR exported as a **separate** arm (9,984 edges, 4,281 nodes), never merged, matching `d128/config.py` where SIGNOR is the P6 sensitivity prior. Excluded and recorded with reasons rather than silently dropped: **13,743 sign-unknown** and **1,408 sign-conflicted** directed edges (from the raw export; never assigned a sign), **651 complex-node edges** (OmniPath writes complexes as underscore-joined symbols such as `ABI1_EPS8_SOS1`; they are not genes, cannot match TF activity measurements, and in the discarded raw build they produced fake top hubs like `UBA52_UBE2E2`). ARACNe is not read by this script at all, per the spec's requirement that co-expression links must not silently become directed causal interactions. **Smoke-tested end to end:** BRAF reaches a large subgraph within 3 hops; CARNIVAL 2.20.0 `runVanillaCarnival` with CBC returned an optimal solution (objective 2.20) in 4.2 s on the real PKN. | Done. Note for Stage 2: the smoke solution contained **561 nodes and 2,292 edges from only 5 measurements**, which is the hub-dominance problem the spec's complexity penalty is meant to control - the ILP weighting must be frozen before any biological run. Open (unchanged): the SPEC §4.8a CPLEX question blocks F from biology, and §2.6(c) the dossier/ranking relationship. |
| D141 (2026-11-18, user decision after CPLEX access was refused - the IBM site required a site number and business justification): **alternative-optima enumeration built in-house by no-good cuts; validated 4/4.** `scripts/systems_validation/fault_isolation/enumerate_optima.py` + `gate_f2_enumeration.sh`. Method, standard integer programming and no new mathematics: take the LP CARNIVAL itself writes (`generateLpFileCarnival`), solve with CBC, then append the cut `sum_{x_i=1} x_i - sum_{x_j=0} x_j <= |{x_i=1}| - 1`, which forbids exactly that assignment, and re-solve. Every solution attaining the optimum z* is an alternative optimum; the first strictly worse objective **proves** the optimal set exhausted. Cuts are applied to NODE binaries only (`nU*`, `nD*`) because two solutions reaching the same nodes through redundant edges are the same mechanistic explanation. **Bug found and fixed during validation, worth recording:** a `startswith(("nU","nD"))` filter also captures CARNIVAL's **continuous distance variables `nDs1, nDs2, ...`**; cutting on a non-binary variable is not a no-good cut but a silent over-constraint - the observed symptom was the optimum jumping 0.8 -> 2.0 and enumeration stopping after one solution, i.e. **reporting a provably ambiguous case as unique**, the exact error the tool exists to prevent. The fix parses the LP's declared `Binaries` section and cuts only on those. **Gate F2 (`results/systems_validation/synthetic/gate_f2_enum_*.json`): 4/4 fixtures recover the known count** - a single chain gives 1, two interchangeable sources 2, three sources 3, and two independent binary choices 2x2 = **4**; all `status: exhausted`. Output per run: `n_alternative_optima`, `core_nodes_in_every_solution` (what all explanations agree on), `nodes_in_some_solution`, and the median pairwise Jaccard between explanations. | Done; F-MATH's ambiguity endpoint is now computable without CPLEX. Stated limits, carried in the script docstring: it enumerates optima of the LP CARNIVAL wrote and cannot reveal explanations that formulation excludes; `--max-solutions` truncation reports `exhausted: false` and the count is then a **lower bound**; each cut makes the next solve harder, so runtime grows with the number of solutions found. |
| D142 result (2026-11-18, user: "output the results from the layered scoring function and then the work we are completing now ... to have a better understanding of what limitations each presents"): **the layered score and the mechanism avenues are statistically unrelated - they agree on nothing.** `scripts/systems_validation/integrate/build_candidate_table.py` -> `results/systems_validation/integrated/{candidate_table.tsv, coverage_summary.tsv}`. Built on the SPEC section 0 entry set (not a known use, gate-eligible, source line matched): **1,399 drug x cohort pairs, 29 cohorts, 274 compounds**. The avenues are placed side by side, never merged into a score, and each carries a status with a stated reason when it cannot speak. **Coverage - what each avenue can say anything at all about:** reversal 100.0%, layered 83.4%, O 78.3% (D134 recovery or D135 CRISPR), **F 39.1%** (main loss: `no_single_gene_target`, then `target_absent_from_PKN`), **S 33.5%** (`<3 timepoints and no washout data exists`). Layered AND F AND O all evaluable for **540 pairs (38.6%)**; adding S leaves **276**. **Agreement between the two scoring avenues, on the 1,167 pairs where both exist: Spearman(layered_pct, rev_pct) = -0.0002, Spearman(layered_pct, spec_mech_pct) = +0.0548, and the top-50 overlap is 0 of 50.** They are not noisy versions of each other; they are unrelated measurements. The disagreements are interpretable: strong-layered/weak-reversal rows are driven by target-role evidence without a matching transcriptional response (Estradiol in CESC-SCC 100.0 vs 6.0; Pioglitazone in BRCA-Basal 99.6 vs 6.6; Methylprednisolone in CESC-AD 100.0 vs 15.2), while strong-reversal/weak-layered rows are mTOR and JAK inhibitors whose target is not a driver in that cohort (Sirolimus in TCGA-COAD 3.8 vs 96.6; Temsirolimus 3.8 vs 93.0; **Vemurafenib in BRCA-Her2 6.6 vs 96.5**, where BRAF is correctly not a Her2 breast driver yet the transcriptional response still scores high). | Reported, nothing merged. This is the empirical answer to the dossier/ranking question of AUDIT section 2.6(c): the two outputs cannot be reconciled by weighting because they are uncorrelated, so a combined score would be an arbitrary choice of which to believe. The honest product is the side-by-side table with explicit not-evaluable reasons. Open (user): which avenue, if either, should drive a recommendation. |
| D143 result (2026-11-18, user question "only ~35% of tested regulators exist in the prior knowledge network?"): **the gap is a study bias, not a filtering artefact, and it constrains what Experiment F can ever claim.** Of 1,420 distinct ARACNe regulators, **468 (33.0%)** are nodes in our `signed_pkn.tsv`. **Our filtering is not the cause**: the unfiltered `omnipath_signed_directed` contains 471 (33.2%), so dropping complexes, self-loops and sign-conflicted edges cost **3 regulators**. Including every directed OmniPath interaction, unsigned ones too, reaches only **601 (42.3%)** - and those extra 133 appear solely on **unsigned** edges, which CARNIVAL cannot use because its constraints are sign-consistency constraints. SIGNOR is worse at 389 (27.4%). **The dropped regulators are not the inactive ones.** Pooled over 41 cohorts, retained vs dropped: median |NES| **1.003 vs 0.970**, share significant at FDR<0.05 **12.8% vs 11.2%** - essentially identical. What separates them is curation: median OmniPath curation effort **16 vs 0**, and **819 of 952 dropped regulators (86%) have zero curation effort anywhere in OmniPath**. Only **35.7% of total |NES| mass** survives, and among significant regulators only **37.6%** are in the PKN. Consequences, written into `docs/FREEZE_F2_driver_recovery.md` and `f2_registration.json` before any F-3 run: (a) F is structurally incapable of nominating an under-studied regulator however active it is; (b) recovering a known driver is weaker evidence than it appears, because the PKN encodes curated biology and a textbook driver has by construction the best-connected route to the best-curated TFs - annotation leakage at the **network** level, which SPEC §10.3 addresses only at the label level; (c) the degree-matched decoy control must additionally match **OmniPath curation effort (+/-50%)**, since a less-curated decoy set tests fame rather than degree. A fifth pre-stated failure condition was added: **if recovery rate correlates with curation effort, F is reported as recovering literature, not causality.** | Recorded before F-3 ran; changes no frozen parameter or success criterion, only the interpretation and the decoy-matching rule. |
| D144 result (2026-11-18): **F-3 inverse CARNIVAL recovers no driver that was not already handed to it - 0 of 4 genuine recoveries.** Run under the frozen registration (commit 9cbc91e), full 15,646-edge curated PKN, inverse mode with no driver supplied, CBC, betaWeight 0.2, enumeration capped at 25. | cohort | driver | optima | status | call | core nodes | | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | | TCGA-SKCM | BRAF | 1 | exhausted | **NOT RECOVERED** | 53 | | BRCA-Her2 | ERBB2 | 1 | exhausted | **NOT RECOVERED** | 87 | | TCGA-LUAD | EGFR | 1 | exhausted | **NOT RECOVERED** | 40 | | BRCA-LumA | ESR1 | 1 | exhausted | STRONG | 87 | **The single STRONG call is circular and does not count.** ESR1 is itself a transcription factor and was therefore **in the measurement set** given to the model (nes +3.22, fdr 2.1e-02); it was returned as a cause after being supplied as an observation, which SPEC §10.3 forbids counting as recovery. BRAF, ERBB2 and EGFR are kinases/receptors, absent from the measurements, and so were the only three cases requiring genuine upstream inference - all three failed. **Two things did work as designed:** the optimum was **unique and proven exhausted in all four cohorts** (pre-stated failure condition 2 does not fire - these are not arbitrary picks from a pile of equally good explanations), and the frozen complexity penalty held solutions to **40-87 nodes** against the 561-node blow-up of the unconstrained D140 smoke test. | **Reported as a null result on the primary test, not iterated** (the freeze forbids tuning after seeing this). The failure is not attributable to ambiguity or to over-large solutions, both of which were controlled. Candidate explanations to separate next, in order: (a) the PKN study bias of D143/R-1 - the curated map may simply not carry a signed route from these kinases to these TFs; (b) the measurement set is small (30-74 TFs) and drawn from the 37.6% of significant regulators that are in the PKN at all; (c) inverse mode with ~4,500 candidate perturbation nodes may be under-determined. The F-4 decoy controls are now less informative than planned - with nothing recovered, there is no positive rate for decoys to be compared against. |
| D145 (2026-11-18): **two structural findings about the full-ARACNe PKN, both recorded before any F-3b result.** The full regulator set does contain the upstream layer the TF-only set lacks - SKCM has 4,585 regulators including BRAF (62 targets), ERBB2 (81), EGFR (133), AKT1, MAPK1, PIK3CA, MTOR, KRAS, NRAS - which corrects my earlier claim that ARACNe cannot supply upstream nodes (user, 2026-11-18). But: **(1) selectivity.** In ARACNe the driver reaches the measured TFs only by reaching nearly everything: BRAF reaches 12 of 42 measured TFs through **561 nodes** in the curated PKN (2.1% selectivity) versus 10 of 42 through **4,808 nodes** unpruned in ARACNe (0.21%) - **~10x less selective**, with no pruning level in between (per-regulator top-k gives 0 measured TFs reached at k=5/10/20 and 5 at k=50). Global top-N pruning to match the curated 15,646 edges was tested and **rejected as rigging**: it leaves 3 of 42 measured TFs and strips EGFR of all outgoing edges. **(2) sign imbalance.** The ARACNe PKNs are **96-97% activating** (curated: 70%), because `tfmode` is a co-expression correlation sign and co-expressed genes mostly move together. Against that, the damage to be explained is mostly downward: **45% of SKCM, 66% of Her2 and 77% of LUAD measured TFs are DOWN**, each needing a route with an odd number of inhibitions from a network where 3-4% of edges inhibit. | Both written into `docs/FREEZE_F3b_aracne_arm.md` before results. Pre-stated reading: if F-3b fails, **sign imbalance is the leading explanation and must be reported ahead of any claim about co-expression versus curation** - the two are confounded in this arm. |
| D146 result (2026-11-19): **the F-3 null is robust to the complexity penalty - pre-stated failure condition 3 does not fire.** The frozen sensitivity arm (`betaWeight` 0.05 / 0.2 / 0.5, all four pilot cohorts, 12 runs) returns **identical recovery calls at every setting**: BRAF, ERBB2 and EGFR NOT RECOVERED throughout; ESR1 STRONG throughout and still circular (ESR1 is itself a measured TF). The penalty **is** being applied - the objective scales as expected (SKCM 2.69 / 10.63 / 26.52; Her2 11.36 / 24.39 / 50.46) - yet the chosen node set barely moves (core 53 / 53 / 53, 87 / 87 / 87, 40 / 40 / 38). So the solution is constrained by the data, not by the regularisation, and the null cannot be attributed to one arbitrary parameter choice. Every run returned **1 alternative optimum, status exhausted**: these are unique solutions, not arbitrary picks. | Closed. The F-3 null stands as a property of the approach on this prior-knowledge network, not of its tuning. |
| D147 result (2026-11-19): **the full-ARACNe arm (F-3b) returns an EMPTY solution - the strongest confirmation yet of the sign-imbalance prediction.** TCGA-SKCM on the 291,570-edge ARACNe prior-knowledge network: linear program built in 458 s (13,671 nodes, 54,684 variables), enumeration ran **9.1 hours**, returned **1 optimum, exhausted, objective 9.2953, and zero active nodes** - `core_nodes: []`, `n_union_nodes: 0`. CARNIVAL could not construct any sign-consistent explanation at all. This matches the reading pre-registered in `FREEZE_F3b_aracne_arm.md` before the run: the ARACNe networks are **96-97% activating** while **45% of SKCM measured TFs are DOWN** and need routes with an odd number of inhibitions, which a 4%-inhibitory network cannot supply. Per that freeze, **sign imbalance is reported as the explanation ahead of any claim about co-expression versus curation**, because the two are confounded in this arm. Supporting evidence from D146's direct edge comparison: where the two networks share an edge, ARACNe agrees with curated sign on **0.0% / 0.0% / 2.5%** of edges the curated network calls inhibiting. BRCA and LUAD were not launched: at 9.1 hours per cohort for an empty answer the information gain does not justify the compute. | Closed as a negative. ARACNe co-expression networks cannot serve as a causal signalling prior for CARNIVAL - not because they lack the upstream layer (they have it: 4,585 regulators including BRAF, ERBB2, EGFR) but because they cannot represent inhibition. |
| D148 result (2026-11-19): **O-3 / D136-S1 first dose-response measurement - regulon benchmark doses are highly scattered, and the intrinsic null is MISSING so the number is not yet interpretable.** 400 sampled (compound, line) cells with >= 3 doses, 2,165 Level-5 signatures, 1,597,159 regulon rows across k = 1.5/2.0/2.5/3.0, **421,865 rows at the S0-selected k = 2.0**. Results at k = 2.0: **censored fraction median 0.438** (p10 0.141, p90 0.701) - close to the 45% S0 predicted, and now counted rather than dropped; **discordant fraction median 0.208**, i.e. a fifth of crossing genes move against their regulon's expected sign and are excluded from potency; **coherence CV median 0.955** (p10 0.452, p90 1.965) - the spread of member-gene benchmark doses is about equal to their mean. **The Kaplan-Meier median is resolvable in only 78,835 of 421,865 rows (18.7%)**: in 81% of regulon x cell rows more than half the member genes are censored, so no censoring-aware median exists and only the censored fraction and coherence are reportable. **Gap, stated plainly:** the spec's section 6 intrinsic null (random gene sets from the same drug's own profile, matched on set size and `sd_within` distribution) was declared in the script but never implemented, so **coherence CV 0.955 has no baseline** - scattered relative to what is unknown, and no conclusion about mechanism may be drawn from it until the null runs. Two identifier bugs were found and fixed during this run: Level-5 and the replicate-noise table use **Entrez** ids while ARACNe regulons use **Ensembl** (zero overlap; the first execution silently produced no rows), and regulon expected signs were being truncated rather than kept paired with their genes. | Open. Next: implement the matched intrinsic null, then re-read coherence against it. The 18.7% resolvability is itself a finding about how far dose-response can be pushed at regulon level with three to five doses. |
| D149 result (2026-11-19, user challenge to my reading of D138.1): **ARACNe regulon variation across networks is mostly CONTEXT, not estimation noise - my earlier framing was wrong.** The user argued that context-dependent targets are expected biology, since a regulator's accessible sites and co-factors differ by cell type, so the same drug should engage different downstream genes in different diseases. Tested against the control that already existed: `regulons_aracne_project_v2_tf_brca_controls.rds` holds **BRCA_SUB1/2/3, three networks each built from 769 randomly drawn BRCA tumours** (D81) - same tissue, same disease mix, biology held fixed, so disagreement between them is estimation noise alone. Median pairwise Jaccard of regulon membership, over 8 regulators: **same-cohort noise floor 0.552** (ESR1 0.490, MYC 0.553, FOXA1 0.581, GATA3 0.528, SOX10 0.648, JUN 0.373, ETS1 0.564, E2F1 0.551), **nested breast cohorts 0.170**, **across all tissues 0.029**. Estimation noise alone gives ~0.55, so the fall to 0.03 is **not ARACNe failing to reproduce itself** - it is genuine context-specific rewiring, as the user argued. Consequences: (a) the per-cohort network design is **supported**, and D139's finding that swapping the whole regulon set moves the reversal score by only Spearman 0.99 now reads as robustness of the activity readout rather than insensitivity; (b) **D147 is unaffected** - the causal-map failure rests on sign and direction evidence (0.0%/0.0%/2.5% agreement on curated inhibitory edges, reversed edges more common than forward, CARNIVAL returning an empty solution), none of which is about reproducibility; (c) a real limit is sharpened - **a ~0.55 ceiling means about half of any single regulon is sampling artefact**, which averages out for activity inference but not for naming specific target genes. Written up in `docs/NOTE_regulon_reproducibility.md`. | Correction accepted and recorded. New revisit item **R-5**: if context-specific rewiring is signal, *how* a regulon differs between diseases may itself indicate what a drug would do differently in each - and the 0.55 noise floor is what makes such a comparison interpretable. |
| D150 result (2026-11-19, user's nested-array proposal): **target-restricted regulon cosine built and run; coverage solved by the full ARACNe set, but both known-answer controls fail - and the failure reproduces D83 exactly.** Motivated by D149: if regulon rewiring across diseases is signal, the gene-level pattern INSIDE a regulon carries context that the production score discards (`rev` collapses both sides to one activity number per regulator before they meet). **Build 1, averaging all ~1,000 regulons per cohort, simply re-derived the production score** - Spearman **0.77** with `rev_pct` and 0.55 with `spec_mech_pct` in both cohorts tested, with the anti-estrogen control scattered from the 9th to the 78th percentile and the top-5 most-opposing drugs being PI3K/mTOR inhibitors (ZSTK-474, NVP-BEZ235, WYE-125132, torin-1) in both cohorts - a generic proliferation signature from a thousand regulators drowning the target-specific one. **Build 2 restricts to the drug's own annotated target's regulon** and scores `opposing_pct`, the drug's rank among all ~1,630 drugs scored against that same regulon in that same cohort. Coverage required the **full** ARACNe regulator set: of 150 annotated single-gene targets, only **7-9 (~5%)** are regulators in the TF-only set versus **80-89 (~55%)** in the full set (BRAF, ABL1, ALK, BCL2, BTK, CDK4/6, CSF1R, EGFR, ERBB2 ...). Result: 2,042 rows, 12 cohorts, **89 targets, 159 drugs**, median `opposing_pct` 50.9 as expected under no signal. **Control 1 is INVERTED**: anti-estrogens against the ESR1 regulon score **76-96th percentile in BRCA-Basal** and **0.9-12.7th in BRCA-LumA/LumB** - backwards, since these drugs act in ER-positive luminal disease. All breast cohorts share the same BRCA network and the same 99-gene regulon, so **only the disease vector differs**: anti-estrogen median cosine is **+0.358 in LumA** (drug AGREES with the tumour pattern) and **-0.241 in Basal** (opposes). The cause is already on record - D83/D85 measured that the ARACNe ESR1 list reads anti-estrogens in MCF7 as ESR1 *activation* (+2.2 to +2.7 across all 10 profiles, against CollecTRI's -2.4). Since LumA tumours run the ESR1 programme up (regulon median z **+5.10**, 62 up / 37 down) while Basal does not (**-0.69**, 48/51), a drug read as *raising* ESR1 targets agrees with LumA and opposes Basal - exactly the inversion observed. **Control 2 is at chance**: BRAF inhibitors against the BRAF regulon in melanoma score vemurafenib **50.5**, dabrafenib **43.3**. | Reported as a negative. The metric is mechanically sound and well-controlled (`opposing_pct` is a within-regulon rank, so a generic cytotoxic gains nothing), and the coverage problem is solved. It fails because the **input regulon reads the drug backwards**, which is a six-week-old known defect, not a property of the nested-cosine idea. Open: the idea cannot be evaluated until a regulon that reads anti-estrogens correctly is available - which is the same blocker as D138.1/R-1. |
| D151 (2026-11-19, user): **relative target-presence gate proposed - the current absolute floor lets a target pass where it is 126x lower than where it matters.** `config/drug_gate.yaml` calls a gene present at **TPM >= 1 in >= 20% of tumours**, which ESR1 clears in **every** breast cohort: Basal median **0.86** TPM (44.7% of tumours), Her2 2.08 (67.9%), LumA **108.41** (99.8%), LumB 138.76 (100%). D91 recorded this on 2026-11-03 and it was never acted on. Measured consequence (D150): anti-estrogens score at the **76th-96th percentile in BRCA-Basal** on the target-regulon cosine - a false positive the gate should have removed before scoring. **Proposed rule:** add a scale-free relative criterion - a gene is present only if its cohort median also reaches `rel_min` x its own maximum cohort median. Sweep over the 30,215 currently-passing (gene, cohort) pairs: `rel_min` 0.05 blocks 12.6%, **0.10 blocks 21.5%**, 0.20 blocks 35.3%, 0.33 blocks 52.4%. **Proposed 0.10**, chosen on coverage alone - every value from 0.05 to 0.20 blocks ESR1 in Basal (**0.6%** of its own max) and Her2 (1.5%) while keeping LumA (78.1%) and LumB (100%), so the ESR1 case does not discriminate between them and cannot be said to have driven the choice. Four pre-stated acceptance tests in `docs/SPEC_relative_target_presence_gate.md`, including that known-use AUC must not fall by more than 0.01 and the D127 biomarker pairs must not degrade. | Proposed, nothing changed in production. Explicitly a **false-positive removal only**: D150 also found anti-estrogens at the **0.9th-12.7th percentile in LumA/LumB**, the disease they treat, and no prevalence filter addresses that - see D152. |
| D152 (2026-11-19): **the anti-estrogen inversion is a TIMING artefact, not a network defect - and the quality floor deletes the timepoint that reads correctly.** Eleven prior attempts targeted the network (D83 signalling-only edges, D84 per-TF switches, D86 perturbation networks, D87 learned directions, D90 mechanism profiles, D91 identity split, D92-D96 subtype networks); none fixed it, because the network was not the problem. Measured directly in MCF7 (is_hiq, TAS floor deliberately not applied), mean signed response over the 99-gene BRCA ESR1 regulon: **6 h = -0.056 (programme DOWN, correct for an anti-estrogen); 24 h = +0.094 (programme UP, the inversion)**, and the flip holds for **every drug separately** - fulvestrant -0.088/+0.064, tamoxifen -0.019/+0.118, raloxifene -0.009/+0.070, toremifene -0.113/+0.137. Carried through to the scored quantity, cosine against the BRCA-LumA disease vector (whose ESR1-regulon genes are up, median z **+5.10**): **6 h = -0.132 (opposes the disease, would rank HIGH); 24 h = +0.093 (agrees, ranks LOW)**. **Why D90's 6 h arm failed:** TAS measures response *size* and early responses are small, so the TAS >= 0.4 floor removes the correct timepoint - of 107 anti-estrogen 6 h signatures in MCF7 only **1** passes, against **72 of 264** at 24 h. D90's rule therefore fell back to 24 h for almost every anti-estrogen. The 6 h median TAS is 0.202: genuine early responses below a threshold calibrated on late ones. | Open; needs its own pre-registration. The candidate fix is a **per-timepoint TAS calibration** rather than one global floor, with the MCF7 anti-estrogen direction as one pre-stated check and the `79n` knockdown benchmark as the guard against admitting noise. This supersedes my earlier attributions of the luminal failure to the regulon - **the regulon reads anti-estrogens correctly at 6 h**. |
| D153 step 1 (2026-11-19): **condition-level drug profiles built; reproduction gate PASSES.** `scripts/79c2_condition_profiles.py` writes profiles keyed on **(pert_id, ModelID, pert_dose, pert_time)** alongside the production files, which are untouched: **20,892 conditions over 9,678 drug x line pairs**, 10,112 genes, 1.20 GB, 7 s. Per spec, the generic drug response is projected out per **(ModelID, pert_time)** rather than per line - 79c's own header records the projection as load-bearing, and the generic response at 6 h is not the one at 24 h - over **121 (line, time) strata**, with **96.5%** of conditions meeting the >= 30-drug floor and the remainder flagged `generic_removed = False` rather than projected against a poorly estimated vector. **Acceptance test 1 (the gate, `79c2_reproduction_test.py`): PASS.** The n_sig-weighted mean of conditions reproduces the production raw artifact to **max |difference| 8.7e-07** against a 1e-4 tolerance, with all 9,678 pairs present. The weighting is necessary, not cosmetic: the unweighted mean of conditions differs by **6.67**, because a mean of means is not the mean unless the conditions carry equal signature counts. **A real bug the gate caught:** the first build grouped on four keys and pandas silently **drops rows whose key contains NaN**; 4 active signatures have a NaN `pert_dose`, which lost 3 drug x line pairs - one entirely - and produced a max raw difference of **4.99**. Fixed with an explicit -1.0 sentinel so "dose not recorded" is its own condition, plus an assertion that no signature is lost in the grouping. The specific arm differs as designed (relative L2 median **0.137**, p90 0.312), which is the measured size of switching to a per-timepoint generic response. | Step 1 of the D153 build order complete. Nothing downstream reads these files yet. Next: `79d` per condition on a bounded cohort set, then acceptance tests 2-4 (anti-estrogen direction at 6 h, no coverage loss, knockdown benchmark within 2 points under the mean-over-conditions summary). |
| D153 amendment (2026-11-19, user): **time windows written into the spec - a single continuous drug-response number is biologically wrong, because half-life means the response is not stationary.** The user proposed fixed 0-12h / 12-24h windows, then asked whether the boundary should instead be found dynamically at the turning point of the drug's regulon response. Measured what the data supports: of 9,678 (drug, line) cells at the production filter, **84.5% have exactly one timepoint**, 15.5% have >= 2, and only **2.8% (267 cells) have >= 3** - so a dynamic turning point, which needs >= 3 points, cannot be primary. Also separated two axes the proposal conflated: **dose** structure is rich (8,778 cells with >= 3 doses) while **time** structure is thin, and a half-life turning point lives on the time axis, which the dose series cannot locate. **But the early/late split is strongly supported and is how LINCS was designed:** 96% of signatures are 6 h or 24 h, the dominant multi-timepoint set is literally `(6, 24)` (1,025 cells) then `(6, 24, 48)` (231), and there are only ~360 12 h signatures in all of LINCS - so a 12 h boundary is unmeasurable, while 6 vs 24 is the experiment that was actually run. Single-timepoint cells are **not** uniformly late: **1,818 (22.2%) are 6 h-only** and 6,250 (76.4%) are 24 h-only, with the early share rising to 36.8% when the TAS floor is dropped (the D152 effect again). So **~9,300 of 9,678 cells can be assigned a window**. **Rule added as spec section 9:** every condition carries `window`; **primary analyses are WITHIN-CELL** on the **1,256 cells holding both an early and a late condition**, since early-only and late-only cells are not a random partition - which timepoint a drug was profiled at reflects its screening campaign, so across-cell comparisons confound the window with that choice and must carry the window as a stated covariate; and the **dynamic turning point becomes an arm on the 267 dense cells**, used to earn the simple boundary applied to the sparse ones rather than replace it. | Specified, not built. Also recorded: the anti-estrogen control has 6 h and 24 h and nothing between, so it can show the response **reverses between** 6 and 24 h and cannot say when - no claim about 9 h or 18 h will be made. |
| D154 result (2026-11-19, user: "make sure we remove general toxicity/proliferation according to timepoint"): **the pooled proliferation adjustment is wrong by nearly 2x its own magnitude, and flips sign for a quarter of regulators.** `79i` removes growth arrest by OLS, `A_adj = A - beta * pi`, with **one beta per (network, regulator)** fitted over all profiles pooled; `pi` is the mean over HALLMARK_E2F_TARGETS + HALLMARK_G2M_CHECKPOINT (303 genes) on the raw profiles (`79i:191`). Tested per the pre-stated rule of `SPEC_condition_level_profiles.md` section 10.3, **within-cell** so the same drugs and lines appear on both sides: 3,454 (network, regulator) fits over the **1,373 (drug, line) cells holding both a 6 h and a 24 h condition** (2,041 early / 4,117 late conditions, 4 networks). **beta at 6 h median +0.238 (p10-p90 -1.10 to +2.53); beta at 24 h median +0.069 (-0.30 to +0.58).** Spearman between them across regulators **+0.70**; **median |beta_24h - beta_6h| = 0.702, which is 189% of a typical |beta|**; and **beta changes SIGN between windows for 24.5% of regulators**. The proliferation score itself differs as pharmacology predicts: **pi = -0.52 at 6 h vs -1.06 at 24 h** - growth arrest at 24 h is twice what it is at 6 h, because at 6 h a cytostatic drug has barely arrested anything. The beta spread at 6 h is also ~4x wider, so the early relationship is genuinely less determined - exactly when a beta fitted mostly on late data gets imposed on it. **Consequence:** the pooled correction, fitted on conditions that are 67% late even within this balanced set, has been applied uniformly, and for a quarter of regulators it subtracts the wrong sign in the early window - adding growth-arrest signal where it meant to remove it. Every mechanism score in the project is built on `A_adj`. | **Section 10.3's pre-stated rule fires: items 2 and 3 need rebuilding** - the `79h` P2 `prolif_linked` criterion and the `79i` adjustment must both be fitted per window. Caveats recorded: four networks rather than 38, and the wider early |beta| spread partly reflects fewer early conditions, so some of the magnitude difference is estimation noise - but the **24.5% sign-change rate is not explained by noise in the magnitude** and is the robust number. |
| D154 confirmation (2026-11-19): **the per-window beta result holds across all 38 networks - 32,762 fits, sign-change rate 25.2%.** Re-ran the section 10.3 check after the full per-condition VIPER run (38 networks, 10.8 min each, 411 min CPU). Against the 4-network result: beta at 6 h median **+0.331** (was +0.238), at 24 h **+0.079** (was +0.069); Spearman between windows **+0.687** (was +0.702); median |beta_24h - beta_6h| **180%** of a typical |beta| (was 189%); **beta changes sign between windows for 25.2% of regulators** (was 24.5%). The four cohorts I started with were therefore not unrepresentative, which was the main thing a 10x larger sample could have revealed. **A quarter of regulators, across every cohort, have their growth-arrest correction pointing the wrong way in one of the two windows.** | Confirmed at full scale. The rebuild (`79i` WINDOWS=1, `79e` WINDOW=early/late) is implemented and committed but **has not yet been run end to end**, so no score has moved. |
| D148 amendment (2026-11-19): **the dose-response intrinsic null is done; the D148 "no conclusion may be drawn" caveat is resolved, and the result is POSITIVE.** `scripts/systems_validation/observability/o3_s2_intrinsic_null.py`, per spec section 6: 399,217 regulon rows, each against 40 random gene sets matched on set size **and** `sd_within` decile composition. **Observed coherence CV median 0.978 vs null 1.059** - real regulons are **tighter** than matched random sets of the same size and noisiness. Only **32.5%** of null draws beat their observed row, and **22.5% of rows beat 95% of their own null** against 5% expected by chance, a **~4.5x enrichment**. The matching worked as intended: observed censored fraction **0.427** vs null **0.438**, so the null sets are not easier merely by containing quieter genes - the specific trap section 6 warns about. | **The genes a regulator controls do switch on at more similar doses than random genes of the same size and noise.** Honest about magnitude: a median shift of 1.059 -> 0.978 is modest, so this says regulon-level dose coherence carries signal, not that it is a strong discriminator; the **22.5% tail** is where the signal concentrates, i.e. about a fifth of regulon x drug pairs look like genuine single regulatory events. |
| D155 result (2026-11-20, user: "compare early timepoints within a group and late timepoints as another group - we do not have to learn the full dose response progression"; "focus on positive hits, and just flag other considerations"): **per-window drug scoring built and run; timing changes the answer for 37% of drugs that have both windows.** `scripts/systems_validation/integrate/window_reversal.py` reimplements only 79e's scoring formula (`rev = -(D . s)/||s||`, 79e:285) on the condition-level activity, with the same celligner line weighting, and scores each window separately. **79e itself is untouched** - four attempts to retrofit a WINDOW flag onto it produced four distinct failures (reshape on a filtered index, `get_group` on drugs absent from a window, an integer cast on NaN counts, and a chain-edit that silently removed the stage), so the WINDOW surgery was reverted and only the harmless `INDEX_FILE` hook kept. **Result: 12,055 (cohort, drug) rows over 25 cohorts** - 7,977 late-only, **2,132 both**, 1,946 early-only. **Spearman(rev_early, rev_late) = +0.388** over the both-window rows, and **37.0% are sign-discordant**: early and late are substantially different measurements, so collapsing them was never averaging two estimates of one thing. **Known-answer check passes where the data allows it: fulvestrant in BRCA-LumA scores at the 88.3rd percentile EARLY against 60.2nd late** - the first time an anti-estrogen has ranked where it should in the disease it treats. But **17 of 20 anti-estrogen rows are `late_only`**: no 6 h data survives the TAS floor, so D152's finding now appears as missing rows rather than wrong numbers. `window_hits.py` gives the hits-first view the user asked for: a drug is ranked by its **best** window with the timing carried beside it as a note, never as a filter. **1,351 hits at >= 90th percentile** - 984 best-late, 367 best-early; 762 late-only, 420 both, 169 early-only; **107 (7.9%) reverse between windows**. | Done. Scope deliberately narrowed per the user: two buckets, no dose-response curve fitted. Caveats carried in the output: **median 1 condition behind a score**, so individual rows are noisy while the 37% population statistic is robust; and early coverage (~4,000 rows with early evidence vs ~10,000 late) is the binding limit, which makes the D152 per-timepoint TAS floor the highest-value next change rather than a theoretical one. |
